# Client review: answering the rest of the questions automatically

Written 2026-11-11. A plan, not a change: nothing in the product was altered to produce it.

## Built so far

- 2026-11-12, build item 5 (1 question, A038): CORE sends `ninjaone_server_patching`, the patching rule over the Windows servers alone; the assessment's server service-pack question reads it.
- 2026-11-12, build item 4 (2 questions, A070 and A071): CORE sends `threatmate_google_super_admin_min` and `threatmate_google_super_admin_max`, counted per workspace; the two super administrator questions read them.
- 2026-11-12, part of build item 2 (2 questions, T-encryption and A112): the tech-stack encryption question is answered from the BitLocker readings (NinjaOne, Liongard) and the FileVault fact (Addigy) already held, and says it is about workstations, not only laptops. The Google "2-Step enforced for all users" question is answered from the Google people ThreatMate lists (switched-on accounts refreshed within a week). The other two rules in item 2 (A124, A051) wait on the decisions named there.
- Items 4 and 5 were the ones that needed nothing decided. The three new checks show on the Client posture page with a label and a fix line, and count in the posture score like any other check. Not yet seen with live data: the first NinjaOne and Google passes after CORE is updated will show them.

## Where it stands

The client review has four lists of questions. 164 of them have no automatic answer today:

| List | Questions | Answered automatically | Not answered |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Assessment (vCIO template) | 145 | 14 | 131 |
| Microsoft 365 | 99 | 76 | 23 |
| Technology stack | 24 | 16 | 8 |
| Insurance readiness | varies by client | most | 2 that are always unknown |

Each of the 164 was given the cheapest honest route, then checked by a second reviewer against the code and the vendors' public documentation:

| Route | Questions |
| --- | --- |
| Map a check we already get | 4 |
| New rule over data we already hold | 24 |
| New check in CORE | 104 |
| Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it | 12 |
| A person answers; ask again when it ages | 20 |

The check changed most proposals: 8 stood as written, 147 had details corrected, and 9 were moved to a more modest route because the proposed one did not hold. Confidence after the check: 19 high, 122 medium, 23 low. Low and medium mostly mean a field name or a vendor answer was read in documentation and not yet seen live.

Two things found on the way:

- The laptop encryption question points at a check (`liongard_disk_encryption`) that CORE no longer sends, so it can never be answered today. Nothing tests the mapped checks against what CORE sends.
- CORE has no Google Workspace connection. 60 of the 71 Google questions depend on one.

## How an automatic answer gets to the page today

- CORE sends checks (a name, met / partly / not met, one sentence, up to 40 item lines) once a day per client. A.C.O.R.N keeps the newest list per tool.
- The review page works out all four lists each time it is opened. A new mapping needs no change to the page itself.
- Assessment: one table, `CHECK_ANSWERS` in `assessment_review.py`, maps a question to the checks that answer it. It is not given the Microsoft 365 baseline, the device list or the IT Glue records, so those cannot be used without passing them in.
- Microsoft 365: one table, `CHECK_QUESTIONS` in `m365_review.py`. A report may carry at most 80 checks; the baseline already sends 72.
- Technology stack: no table; each question is a line of code in `tech_stack.py`, and it is not given the tool checks.
- Readiness: one table, `TOOL_ITEMS` in `client_review.py`, plus hand-written rules.
- A status always means "as the template wants it". There is no field for a question where yes is the bad answer; the one such case (public RDP) is hand-coded.
- A typed answer always wins over the automatic one and never ages.

### 1. Hand the checks A.C.O.R.N already stores to the tech-stack and readiness lists

- **Kind and size:** Mapping only, small. Source: CIPP / Microsoft 365 baseline, Cisco Meraki (existing keys only).
- **Questions (4):** T-backup_isp, T-anti_phishing, T-external_email_banner, R-email_filtering
- **What to build:** Path legend for this whole list: AiSOC/ = C:/Users/Chrono/Documents/AiSOC, Jarvis/ = C:/Users/Chrono/Documents/Jarvis. No CORE change. In AiSOC/services/api/app/api/v1/endpoints/client_review.py pass two things the endpoint already loads into tech_stack() (AiSOC/services/api/app/services/tech_stack.py) and readiness() (AiSOC/services/api/app/services/client_review.py): the 'cipp' baseline row's checks (m365_row, today given only to m365_answers) and tool_checks (today not given to tech_stack). Then replace four hard-coded 'unknown' answers with short rules: (a) backup_isp from existing key meraki_dual_wan: met -> seen 'Yes' only when every firewall in the inventory is Meraki, otherwise 'Yes at the Meraki sites' naming the others; partly -> not_seen 'Not at every location'; not_met -> 'None seen'; absent -> unknown. (b) anti_phishing from existing key anti_phishing, only when the same row holds an spf/dkim/dmarc key (mail is on Microsoft 365): seen 'Microsoft 365 built-in anti-phishing' (not 'Defender'). (c) external_email_banner from existing key external_sender_tag: met -> seen 'Outlook external sender tag'; not_met -> unknown (not not_seen), because a mail-flow-rule banner or Inky's banner is not read. (d) readiness e-mail filtering: mail on Exchange Online (spf/dkim/dmarc key present) -> met, evidence quoting safe_attachments, safe_links, common_attachments; no such key -> unknown. Tests next to each rule.
- **Needed from Hal:** Two decisions: does Microsoft's built-in anti-phishing policy count as a yes for the template line (or only a third-party product such as Inky); and should Microsoft's always-on filtering alone show as Met on the readiness line, or Partly unless Safe Attachments / Safe Links or a third-party filter is also on.
- **Risks:** meraki_dual_wan counts firewalls, not locations; a second line that is down today reads as missing; a cellular uplink or two lines from one carrier read as a backup. The anti_phishing answer will be the same for practically every Microsoft client (default policy applies to everyone; that the default row carries Enabled true was not read on a live tenant). Google-mail clients stay unknown.

### 2. Four quick A.C.O.R.N rules over data the review endpoint already has in hand

- **Kind and size:** A.C.O.R.N rule, small. Source: ThreatMate Google, Huntress / Blumira / Field Effect / DNSFilter mapping, NinjaOne / Liongard / Addigy device facts.
- **Questions (4):** T-encryption, A124, A112, A051
- **What to build:** No CORE change. (1) T-encryption in tech_stack.py: use infra_review.encryption_readiness(devices) (devices already passed): met/partly -> seen 'BitLocker'; not_met with some encrypted -> 'BitLocker (some machines)'; none -> 'None seen'; no Windows reading but Addigy details.facts.filevault_enabled true (accept boolean or the text 'true') -> seen 'FileVault'. (2) A124 in assessment_review.py: when the checks dict contains any key starting 'threatmate_google_' answer the less-secure-apps question met with a fixed sentence that Google retired it; never not_met; no Google row -> needs_person. (3) A112: pass the IdentityMfaUser rows the endpoint already loads into the assessment step; over Google rows only (source in mfa_registration.GOOGLE_SOURCES, enabled true, refreshed within about a week): all per_user == 'enforced' -> met, some -> partly, none -> not_met, no rows -> needs_person. (4) A051: pass enrolled_in / mapped_tools (built in the endpoint, not passed today) plus one new ClientLicenseCount query (source huntress, item 'SIEM log sources') and tool_checks['dnsfilter_traffic']: Blumira, Field Effect, or Huntress with SIEM log sources > 0 -> met; Huntress without log sources and/or DNSFilter traffic met -> partly; in none although other clients are -> not_met; no client mapped to any -> needs_person. Do not parse the huntress_extra_coverage evidence sentence. Each rule gets its own test, and each is a special case because CHECK_ANSWERS reads only status and evidence.
- **Needed from Hal:** Agree the tool list for 'the network is monitored' (proposed: Blumira, Field Effect, Huntress SIEM with log sources = met; Huntress EDR and/or DNSFilter alone = partly; Auvik/Meraki uptime alone does not count). Agree that a fixed 'met, Google retired this' answer is acceptable and that the ThreatMate Google connection is the signal for 'client uses Google Workspace'.
- **Risks:** The inventory has no chassis field, so the encryption answer is about workstations, not laptops. The review applies no staleness test to tool rows today, so a disconnected Google workspace keeps answering A124 until its row is removed. A112: a new Google user inside the enrolment grace period may read as not enforced; archived accounts may be counted (no 'archived' flag in ThreatMate's row). A051 shows the client is in the tool, not which services are switched on.

### 3. Typed answers that ask to be confirmed again (person-only questions)

- **Kind and size:** Typed answer that ages, small. Source: none (A.C.O.R.N typed answers).
- **Questions (20):** A002, A008, A018, A019, A029, A033, A037, A044, A052, A076, A077, A088, A089, A115, A118, A119, A120, A123, M04, T-cloud_servers
- **What to build:** These 20 questions stay typed in client_review_answers; no tool can answer them. One small feature in AiSOC/services/api/app/services/review_overrides.py (it has no expiry today; a typed status wins forever): a pure function that marks a typed status older than the agreed interval as due for re-confirmation, with a test, plus a marker in the web page (ClientReview.tsx; merge_assess already copies updated_at, whether the page shows it was not confirmed). Age only rows that hold a status. Because the PUT rewrites updated_at on every save (a note-only edit restarts the clock), either add a 'confirmed on' date or use the last manual status row in client_review_history. Covers sections assess, m365 (M04) and tech (T-cloud_servers). Optional helper evidence that never sets a status, each needing the device list from item 6: Meraki admin facts for A008/A018 (GET /organizations/{id}/admins is already read), listed switches for A019, access-point count for A029, Auvik connected_to cabling for A037, camera count for A052, the threatmate_google_apps evidence as a prompt for A118, server maker and HaloPSA line hints for T-cloud_servers.
- **Needed from Hal:** Decide: the interval (12 months proposed; the CIS control behind A118/A119 says weekly, so pick deliberately, for example quarterly for those two); whether an aged answer is only flagged or falls back to needs_person; whether the clock is a separate 'confirmed on' date. For the Meraki helper on A008/A018: the MSP's own e-mail domains. For A118: whether a new risky app should reopen the question.
- **Risks:** Rows are keyed by the question text, so rewording a question orphans its answer. Helper evidence can be mistaken for an answer if it ever sets a status (cameras seen is not 'monitored'; two cables is not 'redundant'). A076, A077, A088, A089, A115, A123 are settings Google holds but exposes through no documented read API; recheck Google's Policy API settings list if the Google connector (item 9) is built.

### 4. Google super administrator count (not fewer than 2, not more than 4)

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, small. Source: ThreatMate (Google Workspace integration).
- **Questions (2):** A070, A071
- **What to build:** No new vendor call. In Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-threatmate-google.ts keep which workspace each user came from (users are flattened per client today) and add two keys next to the existing threatmate_google_admins, sent under the existing source threatmate-google: threatmate_google_super_admin_min (met when every workspace has 2 or more live super admins; not_met when any has exactly 1) and threatmate_google_super_admin_max (met when every workspace has 1 to 4; not_met when any has 5 or more; evidence says 'N Google super administrators; the template wants no more than 4', addresses in items when over). Send nothing when a workspace shows 0 or the user list came back at the 5000-row limit. Tests in aisoc-threatmate-google.test.ts. A.C.O.R.N: two CHECK_ANSWERS rows in AiSOC/services/api/app/services/assessment_review.py; no endpoint change (threatmate-google is in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES and already reaches assessment_answers).
- **Needed from Hal:** Nothing.
- **Risks:** Only clients whose Google Workspace is integrated in ThreatMate and mapped are covered. The user read is single and unpaged (possible truncation at 5000). Both new checks count in the posture score at weight 1.

### 5. Server-only patching check

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, small. Source: NinjaOne.
- **Questions (1):** A038
- **What to build:** In Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-ninja-checks.ts add key ninjaone_server_patching by running the existing patchingCheck rule over machines with kind === 'server' only (no new API call: /v2/queries/os-patches and /v2/devices are already read). Not sent when no Windows server was heard from in 30 days. It goes out in the same NinjaOne report. A.C.O.R.N: one CHECK_ANSWERS line for the 'latest service pack' question.
- **Needed from Hal:** Nothing (accept that 'latest service pack' is read as 'no critical or important OS update pending more than 30 days').
- **Risks:** A server whose patch scanning is switched off in NinjaOne lists nothing pending and would count as current. With few servers one late server is normally not_met.

### 6. Pass the device list into the assessment step and add the held-device rules

- **Kind and size:** A.C.O.R.N rule, medium. Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik, NinjaOne, Slide, Datto BCDR rows already stored).
- **Questions (12):** A013, A014, A016, A020, A021, A022, A024, A028, A034, A046, A055, A056
- **What to build:** assessment_answers(checks) in AiSOC/services/api/app/services/assessment_review.py takes only checks; change it (or add an overlay in endpoints/client_review.py) to also receive `shown`, the devices the endpoint already loads, plus enrolled_in for the Auvik test. One shared helper: network gear by role via infra_review.network_role(), classed Meraki / UniFi / other by DeviceSource vendor and maker text (not network_role's brand string). Rules, each with a test: A014, A016, A021, A022: all listed switches / access points are Meraki or UniFi -> 'does not apply'; any other brand -> needs_person naming them; none listed -> needs_person. A013: every listed switch Meraki -> met; UniFi from details.adopted_at -> partly at most; other brands needs_person. A020: Device.model against a new small model table (model -> slowest port speed); unknown model -> needs_person. A024: every AP has a meraki/unifi source row synced in the last 7 days -> met; else needs_person. A028: dedicated APs listed -> met; Meraki/UniFi inventory with no AP and a gateway model with a built-in radio -> not_met; else needs_person. A034: hypervisor_of() plus NinjaOne node_class VMWARE_VM_HOST / HYPERV_VMM_HOST / NMS_VM_HOST or Auvik device_type 'hypervisor' -> met; Microsoft virtual hardware seen only from guests -> needs_person. A046: NinjaOne details.processor, memory_gb, disks[] on workstations with last contact within 30 days: i5-class or better, >= 7.5 GB, C: volume >= 220; all pass -> met, under 10% fail -> partly, else not_met. A055: non-server computers with a Slide or datto-bcdr source; Datto last_snapshot_at within about 24 hours counts as daily; a Slide machine counts only as 'protected' until CORE's device feed adds agent_mode and backup_interval_minutes from the /v1/agent rows already fetched (small CORE addition in the Slide device records). A056: any Auvik device with details.device_type 'ups' -> met; never not_met.
- **Needed from Hal:** Decisions: may A.C.O.R.N set 'not applicable' automatically, and only for Auvik-watched clients or all; should an expired Meraki licence make A013 Not met or stay Met per the template; how UniFi switches read after a year; who confirms each switch model's port speed for the table; whether AMD Ryzen 5+, Apple silicon and Snapdragon X count as 'i5 or higher'; do laptops count as 'desktops' and is 'not imaged by design' Not met or Not applicable; confirm the Meraki API key is a full organization administrator (or approve the small /administered/identities/me check). One look at a stored UniFi row and a stored NinjaOne row is advised before trusting the rules.
- **Risks:** An automatic not_applicable is new: assessment_answers emits only met/partly/not_met/needs_person today, so page, CSV and history must be checked. Gear no tool lists (unmanaged switches, consumer routers, a UPS without a network card) is invisible, so Met means 'every device the tools list'; without Auvik the 'all Meraki/UniFi' test cannot fail. Unconfirmed live: NinjaOne filling memory/processors/volumes on devices-detailed rows, Auvik 'ups' and 'hypervisor' type values, built-in-radio model list, that every MS/UniFi switch is gigabit, Datto BCDR fields (never checked against a live account). A055 needs its own 24-hour threshold (STALE_SNAPSHOT_DAYS is 3).

### 7. Meraki end of support per product type (firewall, switch, access point) and the firewall warranty inference

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: Cisco Meraki.
- **Questions (4):** A009, A010, A012, A025
- **What to build:** The existing meraki_end_of_support check (endOfSupportCheck in Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts) counts every product and unused stock in one status, so a plain mapping is not honest. Recommended single route for all four questions: from the same GET /organizations/{id}/inventory/devices rows (productType, networkId, eox.endOfSupportAt), add three keys, named by me after the existing key since the items do not name them: meraki_firewall_end_of_support, meraki_switch_end_of_support, meraki_wireless_end_of_support. Each counts only devices of that productType that are in a network, carries the date in evidence, sends met 'no end date announced' when none has a date, and is not sent when the inventory read failed or the client has no device of that type. Then three CHECK_ANSWERS lines (A010, A012, A025), evidence starting with the literal answer ('No: none of 6 access points...'). A009 (firewall warranty) is an A.C.O.R.N rule on top: every firewall is Meraki with a model starting MX or Z, the firewall end-of-support key is met and meraki_licence is met or partly -> met; within 6 months -> partly; past end of support or licence not_met -> not_met; other brands -> needs_person (needs the device list from item 6). Alternative with no CORE change (what A009/A025 proposed): parse the existing per-model items in A.C.O.R.N against the client's device models.
- **Needed from Hal:** Choose the route (new product-type keys in CORE, recommended, or item-string parsing in A.C.O.R.N). Confirm 'end of support within 6 months' shows as Partly. Confirm he accepts 'Meraki lifetime warranty until end of support, with a current licence' as the warranty answer for MX/Z firewalls. For UniFi-only clients: keep a small maintained end-of-support model list or leave it to a person.
- **Risks:** Meraki only: UniFi has no end-of-support data; non-Meraki gear needs the Auvik lifecycle read (item 20 follow-on). The warranty answer is an inference from Meraki's published policy, not a per-device entitlement; it does not cover second-hand units; other appliance models (C8000, vMX) are outside it. If the parsing route is chosen: absent check cannot be told from 'no date announced', spare units and other product types pollute the items, and model text may be Auvik's makeModel. Three new checks count in the posture score.

### 8. Microsoft 365 baseline: five direct reads through CIPP, and raise the 80-check cap first

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: CIPP (Graph and Exchange Online passthrough).
- **Questions (5):** M01, M02, M03, M05, M06
- **What to build:** FIRST, in A.C.O.R.N: raise M365ChecksReport.checks max_length=80 (AiSOC/services/api/app/api/v1/endpoints/client_review.py line 193, confirmed) and deploy before CORE sends any new key; an over-length report is refused whole. The baseline sends 72 today; this item and item 10 add up to 23, item 21 one more. Then in readM365Checks (Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-m365-checks.ts), new unprefixed baseline keys: m365web_third_party_storage (ListGraphRequest Endpoint=servicePrincipals with $filter appId eq 'c1f33bc0-bdb4-4248-ba9b-096807ddb43e' and $select as SEPARATE query parameters, as in aisoc-defender.ts; accountEnabled false -> met, true or principal absent -> not_met, failed read -> nothing; do not use CIPP's CIS_1_3_7 test); user_owned_apps (Endpoint=admin/appsAndServices: isOfficeStoreEnabled and isAppAndServicesTrialEnabled both false -> met, one false -> partly, both true -> not_met); forms_phishing_protection (Endpoint=admin/forms/settings, isInOrgFormsPhishingScanEnabled); priority_account_protection (cippExoRead Get-EmailTenantSettings EnablePriorityAccountProtection plus Get-User with cmdParams IsVIP:true; widen the exo() helper to pass cmdParams; no Defender for Office 365 Plan 2 -> not_met with a LICENSE_NOTES line; on with tagged users -> partly, never met automatically); entra_admin_center_restricted (Endpoint=admin/entra/uxSetting, restrictNonAdminAccess, boolean or string) ONLY if a one-tenant read-only probe answers; if refused, M06 stays typed. A.C.O.R.N per key: a CHECK_QUESTIONS line in m365_review.py, CORE_CHECK_KEYS and the count in tests/test_m365_review.py, a line in docs/M365_CHECKS.md.
- **Needed from Hal:** Approve one read-only probe of admin/entra/uxSetting through CIPP on one client tenant (decides whether M06 is a check or stays typed). Nothing else.
- **Risks:** Not run live. Per-tenant consent for the org-settings reads (OrgSettings-AppsAndServices.Read.All, OrgSettings-Forms.Read.All) is unverified; a refused read just leaves the check out. M05: the plan name to test (THREAT_INTELLIGENCE) is from memory; that CIPP's Exchange session may run Get-EmailTenantSettings and accept the IsVIP switch is unconfirmed; the alert-policy part is not read. M06: Microsoft lists no supported permission and CIPP's own test is manual, so the probe will likely be refused.

### 9. Google Workspace admin connector, phase 1: settings whose fields and values are documented

- **Kind and size:** New connection, large. Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API).
- **Questions (47):** A059, A060, A061, A062, A063, A064, A065, A066, A067, A068, A069, A072, A073, A074, A075, A078, A079, A080, A081, A082, A083, A084, A085, A086, A087, A090, A091, A093, A094, A095, A096, A097, A098, A099, A100, A101, A102, A103, A104, A105, A106, A107, A108, A111, A114, A116, A122
- **What to build:** CORE has no Google admin connection (its only Google sign-in is one mailbox with Gmail and Calendar scopes, and the device flow cannot carry this scope). New connector in Jarvis/hud/src (registered in connector-manager.ts, a sweep beside the other aisoc-*-checks.ts files): a service account with domain-wide delegation, read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies paged (pageSize max 100), filtered per family with setting.type.matches(...). One shared reducer, tested: per target the policy with the greatest policyQuery.sortOrder wins, per field for Merge settings; judge the customer-level value; a looser organisational-unit or group value turns met into partly; accept camelCase and snake_case field names; unknown value -> check left out. A.C.O.R.N: new vendor and source (suggest 'google-workspace') in infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES (the ingest refuses an unlisted source), a client mapping (ClientAlias) from Google customer id or primary domain to the client, one CHECK_ANSWERS row per key using the FULL question text. Keys, one prefix recommended (gws_): Groups gws_groups_external_access, gws_groups_creation, gws_groups_view_default; services gws_sites_service_off, gws_external_groups_off; Chat gws_chat_external_files, gws_chat_internal_files, gws_chat_external_domains, gws_chat_external_spaces, gws_chat_apps_install, gws_chat_webhooks; directory and calendar (unnamed in the items; my suggestions) gws_directory_external_sharing, gws_calendar_primary_external_sharing, gws_calendar_external_invite_warning, gws_calendar_secondary_external_sharing; Drive gws_drive_external_warning, gws_drive_publishing_blocked, gws_drive_sharing_allowlist, gws_drive_allowlist_warning, gws_drive_access_checker, gws_drive_distribute_internal_only, gws_shared_drive_creation, gws_shared_drive_manager_override, gws_shared_drive_members_only, gws_shared_drive_download_limit; Gmail (proposed as google_gmail_* in A090-A101 and gws_gmail_* in A102-A110) mail delegation, web offline, encrypted / script / anomalous attachments, shortener scanning, image scanning, untrusted link warning, similar-domain / employee-name / own-domain spoofing, unauthenticated email, groups spoofing, POP and IMAP, auto forwarding, outbound gateway, external recipient warning, pre-delivery scanning; gws_marketplace_restricted; security (proposed as google_user_recovery, google_password_policy, google_session_control). All checks sent in the good direction; yes-is-bad questions open the evidence with the literal answer.
- **Needed from Hal:** The decision to build it (the Google service-account route was put on hold on 2026-11-04). Then: one Google Cloud project with the Cloud Identity API on and one service account, its key stored safely on the CORE VM; in EACH Google client's Admin console a super administrator authorises that client ID for domain-wide delegation with exactly the read-only scope, and names an admin address to act as (several items say Google allows only a super administrator); the Google-customer-to-client mapping. Policy decisions: one rule for a setting absent from the response (documented default, or send nothing); whether 'private groups only' is met for A103; password thresholds for A116 (CIS 14 characters; whether the 365-day reset counts); confirm 12 hours for A122.
- **Risks:** Nothing was called live. Quota is 1 request per second per project AND per customer and cannot be raised, so a pass over all clients runs one request at a time. Acting as a super administrator in every client is a security decision for the owner and each client, and the connector is itself an entry on each client's delegation list (A119). Unconfirmed: whether the read-only scope works with delegation (Google's setup guide authorises cloud-identity.policies); what an untouched tenant returns; serviceState value strings (A062, A063); the setting type string for external spaces (A067); enum-to-console-label mapping for A072; meaning of allow_publishing_files in allowlist mode (A079); the console/API inversion on shared-drive creation (A084); the reducer for A107. Each key must be compared with one live tenant's console before it is trusted. Only helps Google Workspace clients. About 47 new checks count in the posture score.

### 10. Microsoft 365 baseline: SharePoint and Teams settings from CIPP's nightly cache (ListDBCache)

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: CIPP (reporting cache).
- **Questions (17):** M07, M08, M09, M10, M11, M12, M13, M14, M15, M16, M17, M18, M19, M20, M21, M22, M23
- **What to build:** Depends on the cap raise in item 8. CORE calls no ListDBCache today. In readM365Checks (Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-m365-checks.ts) add a cached-read helper: GET /api/ListDBCache?tenantFilter=<tenant>&type=<collection> (response {Results:[...]}, which unwrap() already handles) plus the countsOnly=true call for LastRefresh, and a mandatory age guard: send nothing when LastRefresh is older than about 7 days (CIPP leaves the old row in place when a tenant's read fails). Collections and new unprefixed baseline keys: SPOTenant -> sharepoint_b2b_integration (EnableAzureADB2BIntegration), sharepoint_default_link_type (DefaultSharingLinkType, numeric or name), sharepoint_guest_expiry (ExternalUserExpirationRequired + ExternalUserExpireInDays <= 30), sharepoint_reauth_code (EmailAttestationRequired + EmailAttestationReAuthDays <= 15), sharepoint_default_link_permission (DefaultLinkPermission, 1/View -> met). CsTeamsMeetingPolicy (Global only) -> teams_anonymous_start_meeting, teams_dialin_lobby, teams_anonymous_join, teams_external_control, teams_presenters (DesignatedPresenterRoleMode == OrganizerOnlyUserOverride), teams_lobby_bypass (AutoAdmittedUsers in EveryoneInCompanyExcludingGuests / OrganizerOnly / InvitedUsers). CsTeamsClientConfiguration -> teams_channel_email. CsTenantFederationConfiguration + CsExternalAccessPolicy (Global) -> teams_unmanaged_users, teams_external_initiate, teams_trial_tenants. CsTeamsAppPermissionPolicy (Identity Global) -> teams_app_permissions. CsTeamsMessagingPolicy + cippExoRead('Get-ReportSubmissionPolicy') -> teams_security_reporting (with a LICENSE_NOTES line). Missing or non-boolean field -> check not sent. Evidence says 'org-wide default (Global) policy; custom policies not judged'. A.C.O.R.N per key: CHECK_QUESTIONS line, CORE_CHECK_KEYS and count, docs/M365_CHECKS.md line.
- **Needed from Hal:** One read-only call on one tenant to confirm the CIPP instance has ListDBCache with the SPOTenant and Teams collections (the items name both type=_availableTypes and type=_shape), and that SharePoint and Teams access is consented for CIPP in client tenants. Decisions: does 'only people in your organization' count as met or partly for M08; follow CIPP's stricter test (fail) or the softer partly for M09/M10; one look at whether clients are on Teams app centric management (if most are, do not build teams_app_permissions and M21 stays typed).
- **Risks:** Not verified live: that this CIPP instance has the cache types populated, that ListDBCache resolves the tenant id form CORE passes, that the cached rows carry ExternalAccessWithTrialTenants (M20) and the app-permission list fields (M21). Only the Global policy is cached, so a custom policy assigned to some users is not seen. A tenant that blocks anonymous join only with the tenant-wide switch still reads not_met. For app centric management tenants a met on M21 can describe a dead policy. Cache data is up to a day old by design.

### 11. Slide backup retention and encryption

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, small. Source: Slide.
- **Questions (2):** A053, A054
- **What to build:** No new API call: SLIDE_SWEEP.loadContext already fetches every /v1/agent and /v1/device row. In Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-slide-checks.ts add slide_retention (local_retention_policy.retention_policy_max_age_months >= 1 on every system still being backed up -> met; evidence names the shortest value, the policy name and the plan's cloud retention text; a system with no value is left out; nothing sent if none has a value) and slide_encryption (encryption_algorithm naming AES with a 256-bit key on every system -> met; any other -> not_met naming up to 3; field absent everywhere -> nothing sent). A.C.O.R.N: two CHECK_ANSWERS lines plus one small special case in assessment_review.py (like the RDP one): when the client also holds liongard_datto_bcdr, cap both answers at partly, because Datto BCDR retention and encryption are not read anywhere. While in this file, the Slide device records can gain agent_mode and backup_interval_minutes for A055 (item 6).
- **Needed from Hal:** Decision: does Slide's 1-month minimum retention count as '30 days' (every Slide client would then show Met; not_met cannot occur from this field). Accept that Datto BCDR clients keep a typed answer and mixed Slide + Datto clients show Partly.
- **Risks:** Not verified live: the values this account returns; encryption_algorithm is not in the schema's required list. 'Or to compliance standards for your industry' stays with a person. slide_retention is effectively 'met whenever the client is on Slide', which must be said in the evidence.

### 12. Meraki Wi-Fi checks from the SSID rows already read

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: Cisco Meraki.
- **Questions (3):** A026, A027, A030
- **What to build:** readSsids() in Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts already fetches GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/ssids whole (the SSID list is not sent to A.C.O.R.N). Add: meraki_wifi_encryption (over enabled SSIDs, reading wpaEncryptionMode which CORE does not read today: all 'WPA2 only' or WPA3 -> met; 'WPA1 and WPA2', open behind a sign-in page, or open-enhanced -> partly; WEP, 'WPA1 only' or open without sign-in page -> not_met; treat open, open-with-radius and open-with-nac as unencrypted, not only authMode exactly 'open'); meraki_wifi_passphrase (psk SSIDs: 13+ characters with letter, digit and symbol; tested in memory, only counts and SSID names sent; nothing sent when psk is missing, masked, or the key's orgAccess is not 'full'); meraki_guest_wifi_isolation (guest SSIDs picked by open authMode, splashPage, or a name containing guest/public/visitor; one new read per guest SSID, GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/ssids/{number}/firewall/l3FirewallRules, allowLanAccess false on all -> met; LAN access but own tagged VLAN -> partly; LAN access with no VLAN tag -> not_met; no guest SSID -> nothing sent). A.C.O.R.N: three CHECK_ANSWERS lines.
- **Needed from Hal:** Decisions: do WPA3 and WPA2-Enterprise count as met for the 'WPA2-PSK' question, and is an open guest network behind a sign-in page partly or acceptable; is it acceptable for CORE to evaluate Wi-Fi passphrases in memory (a departure from CORE's practice of never reading a password value); how a 'public' Wi-Fi is recognised (or adopt the word Guest in the SSID name). Confirm the Meraki API key is a full administrator.
- **Risks:** At most 40 wireless networks per organization are read. Wi-Fi served by a firewall's built-in radio is a different endpoint and is not read. UniFi Wi-Fi settings are not in the API CORE uses, so UniFi clients stay with a person. How Meraki masks the psk is unconfirmed; the passphrase check must be tried once against a live organization. Whether CORE's key can read per-SSID firewall rules is not verified. A guest network with an ordinary name and a shared passphrase would be missed. The passphrase or its length must never be stored, logged or sent.

### 13. Firmware 'latest major' for firewalls, switches and access points

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: Cisco Meraki (new read); UniFi (held, unused).
- **Questions (3):** A005, A017, A023
- **What to build:** Meraki: one new read per network, GET /networks/{networkId}/firmwareUpgrades (not called anywhere in CORE), whose products block carries appliance, switch and wireless together (currentVersion {firmware, shortName, releaseType, releaseDate}, availableVersions[], nextUpgrade). Three keys in Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts: meraki_firewall_firmware, meraki_switch_firmware, and for access points a third key the items did not name (my suggestion: meraki_wireless_firmware). One shared rule: current major equals the highest major among availableVersions whose releaseType is 'stable' -> met; a newer stable major exists -> not_met, or partly when some networks are behind / an upgrade to that major is scheduled; evidence names running and newest versions, clipped to 300; not sent on a failed read. Do not decide on isUpgradeAvailable alone (Cisco defines it as a recommended upgrade, which also fires for a minor release). Add a per-organization network cap like MAX_WIRELESS_NETWORKS = 40. Then three CHECK_ANSWERS lines. UniFi (no new call): either a first UniFi check in CORE (the UniFi sweep sends none; 'unifi' is not in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES, confirmed) or an A.C.O.R.N rule over DeviceSource.details.firmware_status / update_available / Device.os_version using the device list from item 6: upToDate, or update in the same major -> met; higher major -> not_met/partly; any other status value -> needs_person. Mixed estates take the worst result.
- **Needed from Hal:** Decision: judge 'latest' on stable releases only (ignore beta/candidate). Agreement to one more read per network per day. A look at one live Meraki response and one stored UniFi device row before the rules are trusted.
- **Risks:** releaseType has no documented enum; the shortName format for switches is unconfirmed (Cisco's example is copied camera data); whether template-bound networks and dashboard-managed Catalyst switches answer per network is unconfirmed. UniFi field values were never read live and 'upToDate' is relative to the console's release channel. Other brands seen only through Auvik have nothing to compare against and stay with a person. One call per network adds load against Meraki's rate limit.

### 14. Read IT Glue records in the client review, and the four rules that need little or no new convention

- **Kind and size:** A.C.O.R.N rule, medium. Source: IT Glue (client_documents already stored); HaloPSA recurring-invoice lines; Addigy.
- **Questions (4):** A032, R-ir_plan, T-password_manager, T-abm
- **What to build:** The review endpoint (AiSOC/services/api/app/api/v1/endpoints/client_review.py) reads no ClientDocument today. Add one query for the client's client_documents rows (in the client's row-level-security scope) and pass them, with max received_at, to a new pure helper used by items 15 and 16 as well: 'does IT Glue speak for this client' = mapped and rows received within 14 days (client_docs.STALE_DAYS). Rules: A032: for each device a network tool gives a role, find an IT Glue configuration (kind 'configuration') with the same normalised serial or MAC (treat a 12-hex-digit serial as a MAC; UniFi matches by MAC only; reuse device_identity.mac_key and device_merge.macs_of) carrying Manufacturer, Model and Serial number lines: all -> met, some -> partly, none with fresh records -> not_met. R-ir_plan: a document titled '...Incident Response Plan...' updated within 12 months -> met; older, or almost no text -> partly; none -> unknown until the convention is agreed, then not_met only with other fresh rows. T-password_manager: also load client_license_counts source 'halopsa'; an item naming a password manager -> seen with the product; else an IT Glue password entry for a password-manager console -> seen; neither -> unknown, never not_seen. T-abm: a password entry whose URL contains business.apple.com / school.apple.com or whose name matches 'Apple Business Manager' / 'ABM' -> seen; optional small CORE change: add the Addigy fact enrolled_via_dep to ADDIGY_FACTS in Jarvis/hud/src/addigy.ts so any Mac enrolled that way -> seen 'In use'.
- **Needed from Hal:** Decisions: does 'documented' for A032 mean recorded in IT Glue or does the Meraki/Auvik inventory count on its own; the incident-response-plan title convention, whether a missing plan counts as Not met, and whether plans are kept as IT Glue documents or attached files (CORE does not read attached files' text). Confirm the item name a password manager is billed under on HaloPSA recurring invoices (or bill it as its own line). Convention: every client's ABM login saved in IT Glue Passwords with the URL business.apple.com.
- **Risks:** The IT Glue documents sweep is marked NOT VERIFIED live in its own header. A.C.O.R.N deletes records not re-received for 14 days; unchanged documents are re-sent only on the weekly full read, which can be cut short by the 4000-section budget, so a missing document is weaker evidence than a missing asset. Serial and MAC formats were not compared on real records. network_role covers firewalls, switches and access points only. The Addigy identifier spelling enrolled_via_dep is unconfirmed. A HaloPSA line or IT Glue entry shows a product is sold or documented, not that staff use it.

### 15. IT Glue flexible-asset conventions (onboarding plan, remote access, WAN and carrier, host storage, UPS)

- **Kind and size:** Agreed place in IT Glue, medium. Source: IT Glue.
- **Questions (7):** A001, A004, A031, A035, A036, A057, A058
- **What to build:** Uses the reader from item 14. Flexible assets already arrive as client_documents kind 'asset', folder = asset type name, body = 'field name: value' lines (assetRecord in Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-itglue-docs.ts). One generic A.C.O.R.N rule helper: find the asset of an agreed type (exact folder match) and read agreed field lines; plus a 'convention in force from' setting so a missing record reads not_met only after that date, with fresh rows for a mapped client. Per question: A001 'Employee Onboarding Plan' asset with 'handled by' fields, updated within 12 months -> met, older or fields missing -> partly. A004 a 'User VPN' choice field (None / SSL VPN / IPsec client VPN / ZTNA): SSL VPN -> not_met, others -> met, missing -> needs_person. A031 carrier, public WAN IP and private subnet lines: all three -> met, one or two -> partly, none with fresh records -> not_met; note in evidence when the Meraki firewall's public IP is not among the documented ones. A035 / A036 one 'Host storage' field answers both; no status until the polarity is decided, value shown in evidence. A057 / A058 one asset per UPS: 'Shutdown software configured' Yes/No; 'Runtime at current load (minutes)' >= 15 with 'Runtime checked on' within 12 months (use the dated field, not vendor_updated_at). CORE fixes needed: traitText() returns nothing for a Tag-kind field (so a carrier recorded as a vendor tag never arrives); CORE does not read IT Glue's 'archived' or 'is-uploaded' attributes. Field names must avoid the words isSecretFieldName drops (password, pin, token, credential, 2fa, recovery, secret, community).
- **Needed from Hal:** For each: the IT Glue flexible asset type name and exact field names and choices (the account's asset types were not read; a code comment names a scripted 'Network Details (auto)' type whose fields were not seen), who fills them in, and the date from which a missing record reads Not met. Polarity decisions: is 'SSL VPN with MFA' acceptable, partly or a finding (A004); is 'attached storage' the wanted answer (A035) and does 'local storage only' count as met (A036). Whether the carrier is a text field or a vendor tag, and whether a script-written '(auto)' asset counts as documented.
- **Risks:** The real cost is the team creating and maintaining the records for every client. These are recorded statements, not measurements (no tool shows UPS runtime, shutdown settings or host storage). Missing records make an answer disappear after 14 days rather than go stale. The asset list is read once per type for all organizations and capped at 20,000 records; a cut-short read is not flagged per client. The optional Meraki Secure Client sign-in signal for A004 is a beta endpoint that can only prove a yes.

### 16. IT Glue password-entry and configuration conventions (switch password age, server credentials, remote consoles, server end of life)

- **Kind and size:** Agreed place in IT Glue, medium. Source: IT Glue.
- **Questions (4):** A015, A039, A040, A042
- **What to build:** Uses the reader from item 14. Password entries arrive as kind 'password' (name, user name, URL, category, entry updated-at; never the value); configurations as kind 'configuration'. A039: entries in an agreed 'server / domain admin' category -> needs_person with evidence 'IT Glue holds N entries, newest changed <date>; not tested' (met only if the owner decides a documented entry is enough; never not_met). A040: count remote-console records (agreed configuration type or password category, licence level in an agreed place) against the client's physical servers and hypervisor hosts from the device list (item 6): all with licence stated -> met, some -> partly, none -> needs_person. A015: after the applies-or-not test from item 6, find each other-brand switch's password entry; changed within 365 days on all -> met, some -> partly, none -> not_met; needs a CORE change to keep the password attribute password-updated-at (DocRecord has no slot) and the resource-id / resource-type link. A042: CORE change in configurationRecord() (Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-itglue-docs.ts) to add 'End of life: <date>' and 'Warranty expires: <date>' lines from mitp-end-of-life-date and warranty-expires-at; A.C.O.R.N rule over physical-server configurations: any past -> not_met, one within 12 months -> partly, all dated and future -> met, some undated -> needs_person. The same 'Warranty expires' line is the fallback for A009, A013, A043 and A045.
- **Needed from Hal:** Conventions: which password category means 'server / domain admin credential' and whether a documented entry alone may count as met; where remote consoles are documented and how the licence level is written; which category or naming marks a switch's admin login and that rotations are always recorded there; that the team records a server's end-of-life date on its configuration, in which field, and which configuration type names mean 'server'. One live look at a password row (does IT Glue return password-updated-at and resource-id) and one configuration row (are the mitp-* fields filled).
- **Risks:** password-updated-at and resource-id / resource-type are confirmed only in community sources, not IT Glue's own reference. An entry date proves the IT Glue entry changed, not the device password. The mitp-* fields may be empty everywhere. An all-virtual client with no host listed would undercount physical machines for A040. Auvik 'ipmi' device type is unconfirmed. Same 14-day deletion and NOT-VERIFIED-live caveats as item 14.

### 17. Hardware warranty dates from NinjaOne (servers and workstations) and the end-of-life stand-in

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: NinjaOne (warranty tracking).
- **Questions (3):** A043, A045, A047
- **What to build:** One collection, three rules. In CORE's NinjaOne device records (ninjaDevices() in Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-devices.ts; flattenFacts() stops at two levels so the nested warranty object is dropped today) add explicit details.warranty_end, details.warranty_start and details.shipped_at from the device row's references.warranty {startDate, endDate, manufacturerFulfillmentDate} on GET /v2/devices-detailed, which is already read. Test that the fields survive the device ingest. A.C.O.R.N rules over the device list (item 6), virtual machines left out with hypervisor_of(): A043 servers: any past -> not_met, one within 90 days -> partly, all dated and future -> met, some undated -> needs_person. A045 workstations (WINDOWS_WORKSTATION or MAC, last contact within 30 days): all dated and future -> met, under 10% lapsed / some within 60 days / some unread -> partly, 10% or more lapsed -> not_met; combined with a business-class model list (Latitude, OptiPlex, EliteBook, ThinkPad... vs Inspiron, Pavilion, IdeaPad...), worse half wins, never met on models alone. A047: a workstation is end-of-life when warranty has ended AND it shipped more than N years ago; none -> met, under 25% -> partly, else not_met. Alternative the items also describe: send two checks from the ninjaone sweep (ninjaone_workstation_warranty, ninjaone_workstation_end_of_life) with plain CHECK_ANSWERS lines.
- **Needed from Hal:** Confirm the NinjaOne Warranty Tracking app is enabled. Approve one live read of a device row to settle the field path. Agree the business-class model list (Vostro, XPS, Surface Go, Macs). Decide whether 'out of manufacturer warranty and older than N years' is what the team means by end-of-life, and set N (5 suggested); otherwise A047 is a typed answer.
- **Risks:** Low confidence: the warranty field path is from a third-party copy of NinjaOne's spec (one item recalls it as references.warrantyDate instead) and was not seen live; list calls may not return it. NinjaOne's automatic sync covers Dell, HP (non-Enterprise), Lenovo, Microsoft and Toshiba only, so HPE servers and Macs have a date only if typed in. A047 is a proxy, not the manufacturer's end-of-life fact. Lenovo often reports a type code as the model.

### 18. Active Directory password policy and local administrator checks through Liongard custom metrics

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: Liongard (Active Directory and Windows Workstation inspectors); Addigy for Macs.
- **Questions (3):** A048, A049, A050
- **What to build:** In Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-liongard.ts (existing GET /api/v1/metrics + POST /api/v2/metrics/evaluate path) add three FACT_METRICS entries, each added to the rowsOf list in readFacts with a new EnvFacts field, and three checks under source 'liongard': liongard_ad_password_history (remembered passwords 3 or more on every inspected domain -> met, some -> partly, none -> not_met), liongard_ad_password_complexity (complexity on in every domain's default policy -> met, evidence stating fine-grained policies are not shown), liongard_local_admins (no account outside an allowed list in the local Administrators group on any recently inspected computer -> met; found on under 10% -> partly; else not_met; evidence opens with the literal answer). No row for the client -> check not sent. Parsers accept number or text and send nothing when unreadable. Macs: a second check in the Addigy sweep from the admin_users fact already fetched (stored, read by nothing). A.C.O.R.N: three CHECK_ANSWERS lines. Fallback only if Liongard's group list proves incomplete: a NinjaOne device custom field written by a scheduled script and read with /v2/queries/custom-fields (CORE reads no custom fields today).
- **Needed from Hal:** Create three custom Liongard metrics and give CORE their exact names (suggested queries AccountPolicy.EnforcePasswordHistory, AccountPolicy.PasswordComplexity, and local Administrators membership on the Windows Workstation inspector); check one live value of each, including one domain-joined machine. The list of account names allowed to be local admin on Windows and Macs. Decisions: how cloud-only clients read; whether 'domain default has complexity on' is accepted for privileged logins; whether Domain Admins or Entra 'selected users' count as staff with admin rights.
- **Risks:** The two AD metrics on Liongard's site are community submissions, not built-in, so they almost certainly must be created; field names and value shapes are unconfirmed. The value is the Default Domain Password Policy only; fine-grained policies are not covered. If Liongard's Users table lists only local accounts, a domain-joined client would wrongly read met on local admins. Addigy admin_users text format is unconfirmed.

### 19. Meraki firewall configuration checks: inbound NAT, content filtering, VPN tunnels

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, large. Source: Cisco Meraki.
- **Questions (3):** A003, A011, A006
- **What to build:** In Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts add a shared appliance-network list (reuse the network list readSsids fetches, filter productTypes containing 'appliance'), GET /networks/{id}/appliance/settings deploymentMode to skip passthrough networks, and a cap matching MAX_WIRELESS_NETWORKS. Keys: meraki_inbound_nat (portForwardingRules, oneToOneNatRules counted only for allowedInbound entries, oneToManyNatRules: none -> met; all limited to named addresses -> partly; any allowedIps 'any' -> not_met; evidence gives counts and public ports only, clipped to 300; not sent if ANY appliance-network read failed). meraki_content_filtering (GET /networks/{id}/appliance/contentFiltering: every routed network blocks at least one URL category -> met; some -> partly; none -> not_met with 'or the licence does not include it'). meraki_vpn_tunnels (GET /networks/{id}/appliance/vpn/siteToSiteVpn mode plus GET /organizations/{id}/appliance/vpn/thirdPartyVPNPeers, picking ONLY name / publicIp / ikeVersion / networkTags because the response includes the pre-shared key 'secret'). A.C.O.R.N: two CHECK_ANSWERS lines; for VPN a special-case rule: no tunnel -> met; tunnels -> met/not_met from an agreed IT Glue VPN record via the reader in item 14, needs_person with counts until that convention exists; never let 'tunnels exist' lower the score.
- **Needed from Hal:** Decisions: confirm 'no inbound NAT' is the wanted answer, that rules limited to named sources count as partly, and that these may count in the posture score; that the VPN key must not lower the score (map non-met to needs_person or keep the key out of the score). The agreed IT Glue place for VPN documentation (asset type or title rule).
- **Risks:** Three to five new reads per appliance network against Meraki's rate limit. Unconfirmed: how NAT and content-filtering endpoints answer for passthrough or template-bound networks; siteToSiteVpn mode values; whether the licence edition (Advanced Security needed for content filtering) can be read, so an Enterprise-licensed MX reads 'off' when it is 'not licensed'. The pre-shared key sits in CORE's 90-second in-memory read cache and must never be logged or sent. UniFi, SonicWall, Fortinet and WatchGuard have no source and stay typed.

### 20. First Auvik checks: firewall running-config backup (and the door for lifecycle, warranty and manage-status follow-ons)

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: Auvik.
- **Questions (1):** A007
- **What to build:** The Auvik sweep sends no checks today (Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-auvik-counts.ts: checksFor returns [] and the spec says countsOnly: true, which must be removed) and 'auvik' is not in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES (confirmed in AiSOC/services/api/app/services/infra_review.py), so a report is stored but never read by the review. Add 'auvik' to TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES, then key auvik_config_backup: GET /v1/inventory/configuration?tenants=...&filter[isRunning]=true joined to the device list the sweep already loads; judge devices whose deviceType is firewall, utm or securityAppliance and whose maker is not Cisco Meraki or Ubiquiti: every one has a running configuration held -> met, some -> partly, none -> not_met; backupTime in evidence, no '30 days' rule; optional GET /v1/inventory/device/detail discoveryStatus.login notAuthorized -> partly. Not sent when Auvik lists no such firewall. One CHECK_ANSWERS line. Follow-ons this unlocks, each a further key in the same sweep and each improving a question owned by another item: /v1/inventory/device/lifecycle (non-Meraki end of support: A010, A012, A025), /v1/inventory/device/warranty (A013, A021, A009), device/detail manageStatus and discoveryStatus (A019, A024).
- **Needed from Hal:** Confirm the Auvik API user can read configurations for client tenants; that a running configuration held in Auvik counts as 'recent and documented'; whether Auvik 'router' devices should be judged as firewalls. Go-ahead for the Auvik lifecycle / warranty / manage-status follow-ons, with a look at one live warranty row first.
- **Risks:** Nothing was tried live. Only some clients have Auvik; a non-Meraki firewall not in Auvik is invisible. When every firewall is Meraki or UniFi the honest answer is 'does not apply', which today can only be typed. Auvik's warranty and lifecycle data are Cisco-sourced and may exist only for some makers; warranty status values are free text.

### 21. Third-party mail filter detection (Inky and gateways)

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: CIPP / Exchange Online.
- **Questions (1):** T-spam_filter
- **What to build:** New key proposed as cipp_mail_filter in the baseline sweep (Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-m365-checks.ts, source 'cipp'; note the other baseline keys are unprefixed, so mail_filter would match the existing naming better). Inputs: ListDomainAnalyser MailProvider (already read; today only 'Microsoft 365' rows are kept), ListTransportRules rows (already read), and one new call GET /api/ListExchangeConnectors?tenantFilter=... (Get-OutboundConnector / Get-InboundConnector; answers HTTP 500 on failure). Third-party product named in the mail route, an enabled connector name / smart host / TLS certificate name, or an enabled mail-flow rule name -> met with the product in items[0] or evidence; none -> not_met. Counts toward the raised cap from item 8. A.C.O.R.N: tech_stack reads it (using the cipp row passed in by item 1): met -> seen with the product; not_met -> not_seen 'None seen'. When it names a product it also upgrades T-anti_phishing, T-external_email_banner ('Inky banner') and R-email_filtering; an added rule test on the ListTransportRules rows (enabled rule prepending '[EXTERNAL]' or a disclaimer) is what would let the external-banner answer become not_seen. Interim with held data only: a HaloPSA recurring-invoice line naming the product -> seen (yes-side only).
- **Needed from Hal:** One client known to run Inky, to confirm the connector and rule names to match; whether Inky appears as its own line on HaloPSA recurring invoices.
- **Risks:** The connector and rule names Inky creates are unconfirmed, so a 'None seen' cannot be trusted until tested on one live Inky tenant. Products that work only through a consented application leave no connector and would read as none. Google Workspace mail has no data. Transport-rule field names for the banner test are from memory.

### 22. vCenter / System Center presence

- **Kind and size:** New CORE check in an existing sweep, medium. Source: NinjaOne installed software; Liongard inspected systems.
- **Questions (1):** A041
- **What to build:** Descriptive question, low confidence. New match on the NinjaOne software list already paged by ninjaSoftware() in Jarvis/hud/src/aisoc-remote-tools.ts for System Center server products (Virtual Machine Manager, Operations Manager, Configuration Manager site server; NOT the Configuration Manager client, vSphere Client, PowerCLI or VMware Tools), and for vCenter an active Liongard system whose Inspector.Name is the VMware vCenter inspector (GET /api/v1/systems, already read by aisoc-liongard-health.ts). The result must NOT be posted on its own under source 'ninjaone' (A.C.O.R.N keeps one row per client per source and replaces the whole list, so a separate post would wipe the other NinjaOne checks): feed it into the NinjaOne tool sweep's own report or add a new source to TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES. The items name no key. Found -> met with product and server named; not found -> send nothing (needs_person), never not_met. One CHECK_ANSWERS line.
- **Needed from Hal:** Nothing to start; decide whether a purely descriptive 'met' may count in the posture score.
- **Risks:** Unconfirmed: program display names, NinjaOne's software row field names (not checked against a live tenant), the exact Liongard Inspector.Name string (listed under Liongard's beta heading). A current vCenter is an appliance and will not appear in a Windows software list.

### 23. Google Workspace admin connector, phase 2: settings whose shape or polarity must be seen live first

- **Kind and size:** New connection, medium. Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API).
- **Questions (13):** A092, A109, A110, A113, A117, A121, A125, A126, A127, A128, A129, A130, A131
- **What to build:** Same connector, reducer, source and mapping as item 9; these 13 keys are held back until one live response per setting has been read. Alert rules (one call, setting type rule.system_defined_alerts, match display_name by exact equality, met = state ACTIVE and notifying, partly = ACTIVE notifying nobody, not_met = INACTIVE, rule absent = left out): gws_rule_government_backed_attacks, gws_rule_user_suspended_suspicious, gws_rule_admin_privilege_granted, gws_rule_suspicious_programmatic_login, gws_rule_suspicious_login, gws_rule_leaked_password, gws_rule_gmail_employee_spoofing. Gmail: quarantine notifications (gmail.email_quarantines send_notifications; proposed google_gmail_quarantine_notify), gws_gmail_spam_internal_bypass (gmail.spam_override_lists joined to gmail.rule_states), gws_gmail_comprehensive_storage (rule_id joined to rule_states; send only when a rule_states entry is found). Security: super admin account recovery (security.super_admin_account_recovery enableAccountRecovery; proposed google_super_admin_recovery; polarity undecided), API access (api_controls.google_services services[] is_enabled; proposed google_api_access_restricted), Drive DLP (rule.dlp with the Drive trigger, state ACTIVE and an action stronger than auditOnly; proposed google_drive_dlp). CHECK_ANSWERS rows must use the full question text: a short prefix such as 'Do you ensure Suspicious' matches two questions and _question raises ValueError, which breaks the page for any client holding the check.
- **Needed from Hal:** Everything in item 9, plus: the polarity for super admin recovery (the template says 'enabled'; current CIS 1.3.0 says 'disabled' and Google's default is off); whether 'configured' for an alert rule means active only or active with a recipient; a test tenant whose spam settings, comprehensive mail storage and quarantines are known, to prove the rules.
- **Risks:** Unconfirmed until read live: whether alert rules nobody has edited are returned at all (if not, 'User granted Admin privilege', inactive by default, is silent exactly where the answer is no; absence must never become not_met); the display_name values; the 'settings/' prefix and filter strings for rule.* types; whether v1 returns rule.dlp; which boolean of is_enabled means restricted and how 'allow any app' is returned; the link between rule_id and rule_states; whether the Default quarantine is listed and whether that checkbox is what the template means. Drive DLP edition limits were not verified, so no rules may be a licence limit.

## Decisions only Hal can make

- Google Workspace admin connector: build it or not (it was put on hold on 2026-11-04). It unlocks 60 questions but needs one service account whose key lives on the CORE VM, a super administrator in EACH Google client to authorise domain-wide delegation, and CORE acting as an administrator of every Google client.
- Typed answers: add a re-confirmation rule or not; the interval (12 months, or shorter for the two 'reviewed periodically' questions); whether an aged answer is only flagged or falls back to needs_person; whether the clock is a separate 'confirmed on' date.
- May A.C.O.R.N set 'Not applicable' automatically for the '(not Meraki/UniFi)' questions, and for all clients or only those Auvik watches.
- Does Microsoft's built-in protection count: as a yes for the anti-phishing line, and as Met (or only Partly) for the readiness 'e-mail filtered' line.
- Meraki end of support: add product-type keys in CORE (recommended) or parse the existing per-model items in A.C.O.R.N; and does 'end of support within 6 months' show as Partly.
- Meraki firewall warranty: accept 'lifetime warranty until end of support, with a current licence' as the answer for MX/Z firewalls; and for switches, does an expired Meraki licence make the warranty question Not met or stay Met per the template.
- Is it acceptable for CORE to evaluate Meraki Wi-Fi passphrases in memory (A027), given CORE's practice of never reading a password value.
- Wi-Fi: do WPA3 and WPA2-Enterprise count as met for the 'WPA2-PSK' question; is an open guest network behind a sign-in page partly or acceptable; how a 'public' Wi-Fi is recognised (or adopt 'Guest' in the SSID name).
- Firewall rules: is 'no inbound NAT' the wanted answer and are rules limited to named sources Partly; is 'SSL VPN with MFA' acceptable, partly or a finding.
- Posture score: may the new checks lower a client's score; in particular VPN tunnels existing (A006) and purely descriptive findings (A041) should probably not.
- IT Glue conventions: the asset type names, field names, password categories and configuration types for the onboarding plan, remote access, WAN/carrier, host storage, UPS, VPN, server credentials, remote consoles, switch logins, server end-of-life dates, incident response plan and ABM login; who fills them in; and the date from which a missing record reads Not met.
- Host storage polarity: is 'attached storage' the wanted answer (A035) and does 'local storage only' count as met (A036).
- Does 'documented' for network gear (A032) mean recorded in IT Glue, or does the inventory Meraki/Auvik keep count on its own.
- Does a documented IT Glue credential entry alone count as 'we have working admin credentials' (A039), or must a person confirm.
- Workstation rules: do AMD Ryzen 5+, Apple silicon and Snapdragon X count as 'i5 or higher'; the business-class model list; whether 'out of warranty and older than N years' is the team's meaning of end-of-life, and N.
- Backups: does Slide's 1-month minimum retention count as '30 days'; accept that Datto BCDR clients keep typed answers and mixed clients show Partly; do laptops count as 'desktops' and is 'not imaged by design' Not met or Not applicable.
- Which tools count as 'the network is monitored' (Blumira, Field Effect, Huntress SIEM = met; Huntress EDR / DNSFilter alone = partly).
- Auvik: go-ahead for a first Auvik check sweep (config backup, then lifecycle / warranty / manage-status); whether Auvik 'router' devices are judged as firewalls; whether a running configuration held in Auvik counts as 'recent and documented'.
- Liongard: create the three custom metrics (password history, complexity, local Administrators membership); supply the allowed local-admin account names; decide how cloud-only clients read and whether Domain Admins count as staff with admin rights.
- CIPP: approve one read-only probe of admin/entra/uxSetting and one read-only ListDBCache call on one tenant; decide M08 ('only people in your organization' met or partly), M09/M10 (follow CIPP's stricter fail or the softer partly), and whether clients are on Teams app centric management (M21).
- Google policy decisions if the connector is built: polarity of super admin account recovery (template 'enabled' versus current CIS 'disabled'); one rule for absent settings (documented default or send nothing); password thresholds (14 characters, whether a 365-day reset counts); 12-hour session threshold; whether 'private groups only' is met; whether an alert rule must have a recipient to count as configured.
- Approve the one-off live looks several rules depend on: one NinjaOne device row (warranty fields), one stored UniFi device row (firmware fields), one Meraki firmwareUpgrades response, one Auvik warranty row, one IT Glue password row and configuration row, one client known to run Inky.
- Confirm the Meraki API key belongs to a full organization administrator (needed for A024 and A027), and that NinjaOne Warranty Tracking is enabled.
- Fixed answer for 'less secure app access disabled': accept 'met, Google retired this' for every client with a ThreatMate Google connection.
- HaloPSA billing convention: whether Inky, a password manager and Azure hosting each appear as their own recurring-invoice line (and under what name), which is the only way A.C.O.R.N can suggest them on its own.

## Things the proposals disagree on (to settle before building)

The same kind of question was sometimes given two different rules by different reviewers. Each of these needs one answer so the checks behave alike:

- Meraki end of support, same kind of question routed two ways: A025 (access points) says no CORE change and parses the per-model items of meraki_end_of_support in A.C.O.R.N; A009 parses items too; A010 (firewall) offers a firewall-only CORE key as an equal alternative; A012 (switches) recommends a switch-only CORE key. Work item 7 picks one route (product-type keys) for all four.
- Firmware 'latest major' (A005, A017, A023) uses three different rules: A017 accepts 'isUpgradeAvailable is false' as met, while A005 and A023 say not to rely on isUpgradeAvailable because it also fires for a minor release. 'Partly' means 'an upgrade to the new major is scheduled' in A005 but 'some networks are behind' in A017/A023. For UniFi, A005 says 'no update available = met, else partly' while A017/A023 compare major numbers. A023 names no key for the access-point check.
- Template questions marked '(not Meraki/UniFi)' are handled four ways: A021/A022 set an automatic 'not applicable' for any client whose listed access points are all Meraki/UniFi; A014/A016 make it firm only when Auvik watches the client and a suggestion otherwise; A013 answers Met ('the template treats Meraki as covered') instead of not applicable; A007 says 'does not apply' can only be typed today.
- Typed-answer expiry is described differently across the person-only items: flag for re-confirmation but keep the status (A002, A033, A044, A052, M04) versus fall back to needs_person (A008, A076, A077, A088, A089, A118); the interval is 12 months everywhere except A118/A119, whose CIS control says weekly; the clock is updated_at in most items, a separate 'confirmed on' date in A002, and the last manual row in client_review_history in A033. M04 calls it a display rule only; others call it new code with a test.
- Google Workspace check keys use three prefixes for the same connector: gws_ (A059-A069, A078-A087, A102-A111, A125-A131), google_gmail_ (A090-A101), google_ (A113, A114, A116, A117, A121, A122); A072-A075 propose no key at all. The source name appears as 'google-workspace' in A059/A072 and is unnamed elsewhere.
- Google: what to do when a setting is absent from the response differs for the same kind of setting. Some items answer from Google's documented default (A098 not_met, A105 not_met, A107 met, A111 not_met, A114 not_met, A116 fill defaults, A113 use default, A090 met), others send nothing until a live tenant is checked even where a default or benchmark default is cited (A059, A060, A073, A074, A078 hedged). A122 says treat absent as not_met 'only after checking'.
- Google: how the effective value is worked out is described three ways: 'the customer-level row with the greatest sortOrder' (A059-A075), 'the greatest sortOrder supplies every field / per field' (A078-A087, A090-A101, Merge settings in A104/A111), and 'reduce per org unit or group, then judge the top org unit' (A102-A110). A107 notes its reducer is not documented.
- Google: connection requirements disagree. Admin role: A059, A072 and A125 say the role needed is not stated in the docs; A078, A090, A102, A117, A121 say Google states only a super administrator can use the Policy API; A113 says Google's setup page names no role and cites a third party. Scope: most say exactly cloud-identity.policies.readonly; A078 says Google's setup guide authorises cloud-identity.policies and the read-only scope is not verified with delegation. Service account: A059/A072 say ONE MSP service account; A078 and A090 are worded as a service account per client.
- NinjaOne warranty: A043 gives the field path as references.warrantyDate.endDate (from memory); A045/A047 give references.warranty {startDate, endDate, manufacturerFulfillmentDate}. A043 routes it as a device detail plus an A.C.O.R.N rule; A045/A047 offer a new check key or the device-detail route. The 'ending soon' window is 90 days for servers (A043) and 60 days for workstations (A045); the bad-share band is 10% in A045 and 25% in A047.
- IT Glue conventions put the same kind of fact in different record types: flexible asset (A001, A004, A031, A035/A036, A057/A058), document title (R-ir_plan; A006 allows either an asset type or a title), configuration (A040, A042, A032), password category (A039, A015, A040 alternative). A001 argues a flexible asset is better than a titled document, while R-ir_plan proposes a titled document.
- A missing IT Glue record is scored differently: not_met after the convention is in force and fresh rows exist (A001, A031, A032, R-ir_plan); never not_met automatically (A035, A039, A040, T-password_manager, T-abm); needs_person (A004, A057, A058, A015 when no entry is found).
- Hardware warranty has five different sources across similar questions: Meraki published policy inferred from end of support and licence (A009), the template's 'Meraki is covered' (A013), Auvik warranty (A013 says use the boolean coveredUnderWarranty filter; A021 says the status and date are free text and a live row must be seen first), NinjaOne warranty tracking (A043, A045), and IT Glue warranty-expires-at as fallback (A009, A013, A042, A043, A045).
- Descriptive questions with no good or bad answer are scored inconsistently: A034 (virtualization in use) and A041 (vCenter / System Center) give met when found; A035/A036 (host storage) give no status until the owner fixes the polarity; A006 gives met when there is no VPN tunnel.
- Posture score side effect: every stored check counts at weight 1 (fleet_posture.summarize). A003 and A011 accept that; A006 says its key must not lower the score; the descriptive checks (A041), the roughly 60 Google keys and the 23 Microsoft 365 keys do not address it.
- The 80-check cap on a report: M01 counts 72 baseline keys plus '11 new keys' = 83 and says the cap must be raised first; T-spam_filter says its key fits as '73 of 80' without counting the M batch; M12 mentions the Teams group will exceed the cap; with M01-M23 and the mail-filter key the baseline could reach about 95.
- CIPP cache items disagree on details: M07-M12 call the LastRefresh age guard mandatory, M13-M23 do not mention it for the same collections; the confirmation call is ListDBCache type=_availableTypes in M07 and type=_shape in M13/M20; M07 dates the cache files to 2026-05-04 while M13 says an instance older than 'the August 2026 releases' needs updating.
- Microsoft's built-in mail protection is judged three ways: T-spam_filter says the stock filter does not count; T-anti_phishing says it counts as worded (and notes its not_met branch is nearly unreachable); R-email_filtering says it is Met for every Exchange Online client while the original proposal graded it on Safe Attachments.
- Key naming for the baseline source: existing 'cipp' baseline keys and the M-batch keys are unprefixed (anti_phishing, external_sender_tag, user_owned_apps, teams_*), while T-spam_filter proposes cipp_mail_filter for the same source.
- Freshness windows differ with no shared rule, and the review applies no staleness test to tool rows today (A051, A124): 7 days for device source rows and Google MFA rows (A024, A112) and CIPP cache (M07), 14 days for IT Glue records, 30 days for NinjaOne machines (A045-A047), 'months old' for A051.
- Third-party vendor admin logins is asked twice (A008 firewall, A018 switch) and both stay typed, but the Meraki helper differs: A008 uses the admin list plus configurationChanges as the access log; A018 uses accountStatus and lastActive, and only A018 asks for the MSP's own e-mail domains.
- Meraki per-network caps: A003 says the existing cap of 40 applies to wireless networks only and a matching cap must be added for appliance networks; A017 and A023 speak as if the 40 cap already covers their new per-network read.
- A055 depends on Slide fields (agent_mode, backup_interval_minutes) that belong in the same CORE file as the Slide checks of A053/A054 but is routed as held data; and A015 is routed as a documentation convention while its applies-or-not half is the same held-data helper as A014/A016.

## Every question

Ids: A = assessment, M = Microsoft 365, T = technology stack, R = insurance readiness.

### Assessment: Administrative / Employee onboarding and offboarding

**A001. Is there a current onboarding plan including who handles specific services and applications?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: IT Glue (records CORE already sends to A.C.O.R.N as client_documents)
- How: A.C.O.R.N table client_documents (model ClientDocument: kind, title, folder, body, url, vendor_updated_at, received_at), filled by CORE hud/src/aisoc-itglue-docs.ts (sweep 'itglue-docs' in direct-sweeps.ts, daily, full read weekly) from IT Glue GET /organizations/{id}/relationships/documents (+ /documents/{id}/relationships/sections) and GET /flexible_assets?filter[flexible-asset-type-id]. No check key exists for this (grep for 'onboarding' in hud/src aisoc-*.ts and services/api/app finds only tests, comments and the question itself). Recommended convention: ONE flexible asset per client of an agreed type, e.g. 'Employee Onboarding Plan' (stored as kind='asset', folder=<type name>, body = 'field name: value' lines, empty fields left out), with fixed fields such as one 'handled by' field per service/application. A flexible asset is better than a titled document because the 'who handles' half becomes checkable (agreed field names present in body) and an uploaded file cannot be mistaken for an empty plan. New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N plus a new query in api/v1/endpoints/client_review.py (it does not read ClientDocument today; assessment_answers() takes only a checks dict, so its signature or the endpoint overlay must change). One small CORE change is also needed if documents are allowed: CORE does not read IT Glue's 'archived' or 'is-uploaded' attributes (grep: zero hits), so an archived plan would count and an uploaded PDF/Word plan arrives with an empty body.
- Rule: Polarity: yes is good. Match on kind='asset' AND folder equals the agreed type name exactly (not a loose 'onboarding' title match: the MSP's own client-onboarding SOWs/checklists would match that and give a false yes; this question is about EMPLOYEE onboarding). met = a matching record exists, the agreed 'handled by' fields are present in body, and vendor_updated_at is within 12 months. partly = record exists but older than 12 months, or some agreed fields are missing. not_met = ONLY after the owner has declared the convention in force (a setting/date), the client is mapped in IT Glue, has client_documents rows received in the last 14 days, and none matches. Before the convention is in force, or when the client is unmapped or has no recent rows, stay needs_person: otherwise every client shows Not met on day one. Evidence: title, last-updated date, IT Glue url. Caveats verified in code: (1) A.C.O.R.N deletes non-ticket records not re-received for 14 days and unchanged documents are re-sent only on the weekly full read, which can be cut short by the 4000-section budget per run, so a missing document is weaker evidence than a missing flexible asset (assets are re-sent every daily pass). (2) CORE drops any flexible-asset field whose name contains password, pin, token, credential(s), 2fa, recovery, secret, community (isSecretFieldName), so the agreed field names must avoid those words (e.g. not '2FA enrolment handled by'). (3) UNCONFIRMED: whether a document's updated-at changes when only a section is edited (IT Glue's public reference lists the attribute but does not define it); for a flexible asset I did not re-read the attribute list this session either. (4) UNCONFIRMED: what the live IT Glue account calls any existing onboarding record; no live data was read.
- Needs: Decide the convention: flexible asset type name and its fixed fields (recommended) or a document title prefix; how old still counts as current (suggested 12 months); and the date from which a missing record should read as Not met. The team then has to create that record for every client, which is the real cost.
