- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds (data is stored, but it only answers the question under an agreed convention). Fixed five details. (1) 'No new CORE read is needed' is not fully true for documents: IT Glue documents have 'archived' and 'is-uploaded' attributes (confirmed in the public reference) that CORE neither reads nor filters, so an archived plan would pass and an uploaded file would look empty; the proposed 'empty body = partly' is wrong for uploads. Recommending a flexible asset avoids both. (2) A loose title match on 'Onboarding' can hit client-onboarding (MSP) documents; match an exact agreed type/prefix. (3) not_met as proposed would mark every client Not met until the team has created the records; gated it behind an in-force date. (4) Added the 14-day deletion / weekly full read / section-budget risk for documents and the secret-word field-name drop in CORE. (5) Effort raised from S to M: the endpoint must load client_documents, assessment_answers() needs a new input, plus tests and (for documents) a CORE change; this first rule builds the helper later convention questions reuse. The feed itself does run through direct-sweeps.ts; I did not read live data.

**A002. Has client been advised that Live Oak requests at minimum, a 72 hour notification of any new hires and to also advise LO when new equipment needs to be ordered by LO. This may require extra time due to shipping/purchasing.**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer in A.C.O.R.N table client_review_answers (model ClientReviewAnswer: section 'assess', key = the full question text, status, note, updated_by, updated_at), written by PUT /api/v1/client-review/{client_id}/answers and laid over the list by review_overrides.merge_assess. No check key, stored data or vendor API holds this fact (grep for '72' / 'new hire' in services/api/app finds only the question). Proposed addition: a 'due for re-confirmation' rule computed from updated_at.
- Rule: Polarity: yes is good. It is a conversation with the client, so no tool can measure it. met = a person records the client was told (note says when and how). not_met = a person records the client has not been told. The answer stands while updated_at is within 12 months; after that the page should flag it for re-confirmation rather than silently drop it. Verified in code: review_overrides.py has no expiry today (a typed status wins forever), and the PUT rewrites updated_at on EVERY save, including a note-only edit, so the 12-month clock restarts whenever anyone touches the note, not only when the client is re-advised; if that matters, the expiry needs its own 'confirmed on' date rather than updated_at. The row is keyed by the question text, so rewording the question orphans the answer. merge_assess already copies updated_at onto the answer; I did not confirm the web page displays it, so showing a 'confirm again' flag likely needs a small change in ClientReview.tsx as well as the pure function and its test. Optional later upgrade, UNCONFIRMED: if the advice is always given through one agreed record (a field in the A001 onboarding-plan asset such as 'New-hire notice advised on: <date>'), the A001 rule could suggest met from it; no such record is known to exist today.
- Needs: Decision only: accept a typed answer that is flagged for re-confirmation after 12 months, and whether the flag should keep the status or clear it back to needs_person.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, source and effort hold: nothing automatic answers this and no cheaper honest route exists. Two details added after reading the PUT handler: updated_at is rewritten on every save including note-only edits, so it is not a clean 'last confirmed' date; and the expiry flag is not only a pure function, the page must show it (web use of updated_at not confirmed). Everything else in the proposal matched the code.

### Assessment: Network and infrastructure / Firewalls

**A003. Are there inbound NAT Rules setup leveraging the Public IP(s)?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 19; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki Dashboard API (Meraki firewalls only)
- How: New check (suggested key meraki_inbound_nat) in the existing Meraki sweep (hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts), per appliance network: GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/firewall/portForwardingRules (rules[]: name, protocol, publicPort, localPort, lanIp, allowedIps, uplink); GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/firewall/oneToOneNatRules (rules[]: name, publicIp, lanIp, uplink, allowedInbound[]{protocol, destinationPorts, allowedIps}); GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/firewall/oneToManyNatRules (rules[]: publicIp, uplink, portRules[]{name, protocol, publicPort, localIp, localPort, allowedIps}). All three paths and field lists were read on Cisco's operation pages this session. Then one CHECK_ANSWERS line in assessment_review.py; source 'meraki' is in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES and tool_checks already reach assessment_answers (endpoints/client_review.py).
- Rule: Polarity: YES is the finding, so the check is written in the good direction and statuses pass straight through. Count inbound-allowing entries: every port-forward rule, every 1:many portRule, and each 1:1 NAT rule ONLY for its allowedInbound entries (a 1:1 mapping with an empty allowedInbound lets nothing in and must not count). met = no such entry on any appliance network. partly = entries exist and every one limits allowedIps to named addresses. not_met = at least one entry whose allowedIps is 'any'; evidence gives the count and public ports only (no inside IPs or names), clipped to 300 chars (the existing Meraki checks do not clip). Not sent when there is no appliance network, and also not sent when ANY appliance-network read failed (otherwise a partial read could claim met). Skip networks whose GET /networks/{id}/appliance/settings deploymentMode is 'passthrough' (field confirmed) or accept the failed read; how the NAT endpoints answer for passthrough or template-bound networks is UNCONFIRMED. Reuse the network list readSsids already fetches (filter productTypes containing 'appliance'); the existing cap (MAX_WIRELESS_NETWORKS = 40) applies to wireless only, so a matching cap must be added. Side effect: every stored check counts in the client posture score at weight 1 (fleet_posture.summarize), so not_met here lowers the score. Not covered: UniFi (Site Manager API has only /v1/devices in CORE), SonicWall/Fortinet/WatchGuard (no connector; a Liongard metric is UNCONFIRMED). Those stay typed.
- Needs: Decision: confirm 'no inbound NAT' is the wanted answer, that rules limited to named source addresses count as partly rather than met, and that this may count in the posture score.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and endpoints hold (all three paths now read, including the 1:1 and 1:many response fields the proposer had not read). Fixed the rule: a 1:1 NAT rule only exposes anything through its allowedInbound entries, so counting every 1:1 rule would give false not_met. Added: do not send on a partial read; the 40-network cap in code is for wireless networks only; passthrough networks; the new check enters the posture score.

**A004. Is there User SSL VPN Services provided for remote access on this device?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue flexible asset (read by CORE's existing documentation sweep); optional Meraki signal that can only prove a yes
- How: Convention: one agreed IT Glue flexible asset per client firewall (or a 'Remote access' asset) with a fixed-choice field such as 'User VPN: None / SSL VPN / IPsec client VPN / ZTNA'. CORE already forwards every non-secret flexible-asset field as 'name: value' text (assetRecord in aisoc-itglue-docs.ts -> client_documents, kind 'asset', folder = asset type name; model ClientDocument has kind, folder, body). A new A.C.O.R.N rule reads that one line; the client-review endpoint does not load client_documents today (no ClientDocument reference in endpoints/client_review.py) and assessment_answers takes only checks, so both need a new input. Optional Meraki-only signal: GET /organizations/{organizationId}/appliance/vpn/remoteAccess/secureClient/authentication/byClient (page read: marked beta, t0 at most 8 days back, returns client{id, username, publicIp}, successes.count, failures.count).
- Rule: Polarity: YES is the answer needing attention. met = field says None, ZTNA, or IPsec client VPN (not an SSL VPN as worded; the proposer left this value unmapped); not_met = field says SSL VPN; partly unused unless the owner wants 'SSL VPN with MFA' = partly; field missing = needs_person. The Meraki beta endpoint can only prove a yes (Secure Client sign-ins in the last 7 days -> not_met with a count; usernames and public IPs must not be forwarded); zero sign-ins proves nothing. Meraki's classic Client VPN is L2TP/IPsec, not SSL, so it does not count. No documented Meraki endpoint returns the AnyConnect/Secure Client configuration: a community thread names /networks/{id}/appliance/clientVpn/anyconnectVpn and reports it not working, UNCONFIRMED. UniFi and other brands have no source. A.C.O.R.N drops documents not refreshed for two weeks and the IT Glue documentation sweep is marked NOT VERIFIED live in its own header.
- Needs: The IT Glue flexible asset type name and field name to use (the account's asset types were not read), and a decision on polarity: is 'SSL VPN with MFA' acceptable, partly, or still a finding.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds. The beta Meraki endpoint was confirmed on Cisco's page (path, beta flag, 8-day limit, fields). Added the missing status for the 'IPsec client VPN' choice, noted that the Meraki response carries usernames and public IPs that must not be sent on, and that assessment_answers itself (not only the endpoint) needs a new input.

**A005. Is the firewall running the latest Major revision of the firmware?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 13; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki Dashboard API (Meraki); UniFi device data already held as an unconfirmed add-on
- How: New check (suggested key meraki_firewall_firmware) in the Meraki sweep: GET /networks/{networkId}/firmwareUpgrades per appliance network, reading products.appliance.currentVersion {firmware, shortName, releaseType, releaseDate}, availableVersions[] (same fields), isUpgradeAvailable, nextUpgrade {time, toVersion}. Path and fields read on Cisco's page. Then one CHECK_ANSWERS line. UniFi add-on (A.C.O.R.N-side rule, no CORE change): DeviceSource.details.firmware_status / update_available on UniFi gateways, stored by aisoc-unifi.ts and read by nothing in services/api/app (grep: zero hits); this needs devices passed into assessment_answers, which today takes only checks.
- Rule: met = the appliance's current version has the same major number as the newest version in availableVersions whose releaseType is 'stable' (no higher stable major offered). partly = a higher stable major exists and nextUpgrade.toVersion is that major. not_met = a higher stable major exists and nothing is scheduled. Evidence names running and newest versions, clipped to 300. Not sent with no appliance network or a failed read. Cisco's spec does NOT enumerate releaseType (plain string; the page's example shows 'stable' and 'beta' but is copy-pasted camera data), and isUpgradeAvailable is documented as 'an upgraded recommended firmware version is available', which is not the same as a newer major, so the first build must look at one live response before the rule is trusted: UNCONFIRMED until then. Whether template-bound networks answer per network is UNCONFIRMED. UniFi rule: gateway (model matching _UNIFI_FIREWALL) with no update available = met, else partly; this is 'any update', not 'major revision', and the values of firmwareStatus/updateAvailable are UNCONFIRMED (Ubiquiti's page did not render; aisoc-unifi.ts says NOT VERIFIED live). Other brands seen only through Auvik: version stored, nothing to compare against (Auvik's recommendedSoftwareVersion sits in its Cisco-sourced warranty record), so they stay with a person.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and endpoint hold. Tightened what is and is not documented: releaseType has no documented value list, isUpgradeAvailable means a recommended upgrade exists (not a major one), and the UniFi field values could not be confirmed. Noted that the UniFi half is a separate A.C.O.R.N rule that needs the device list passed to assessment_answers.

**A006. Are there VPN tunnels configured on the firewall? If yes, is the configuration documented for easy reference?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 19; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki Dashboard API for 'are there tunnels'; IT Glue documentation (convention) for 'is it documented'
- How: New check (suggested key meraki_vpn_tunnels) in the Meraki sweep: GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/vpn/siteToSiteVpn per appliance network (mode, hubs[], subnets[]) plus one call per organization GET /organizations/{organizationId}/appliance/vpn/thirdPartyVPNPeers (peers[]: name, publicIp, ikeVersion, networkTags, network{names, ids}, ... and secret). Both paths read on Cisco's pages. Then a special-case rule in assessment_review.py (like the existing RDP one), and for the documentation half a lookup in client_documents, which neither the endpoint nor assessment_answers reads today.
- Rule: Two questions in one. Tunnels exist when any appliance network's siteToSiteVpn mode is not 'none' (the page shows only the example 'spoke'; values none/hub/spoke are UNCONFIRMED) or the organization lists a third-party peer that applies to at least one network (a peer whose networkTags is ['none'] applies to none and must not count). No tunnel -> met (nothing to document). Tunnels exist -> the answer depends on documentation: with an agreed IT Glue place (asset type 'VPN' or a document titled 'VPN - ...') A.C.O.R.N answers met when such a record exists and not_met when none does; until that convention is agreed it should show needs_person with the counts as evidence. Do NOT have CORE send 'partly' merely because tunnels exist, as proposed: every stored check counts in the posture score (fleet_posture.summarize, weight 1), so a client would lose points for having a VPN. Either the A.C.O.R.N rule maps any non-met status to needs_person, or the key is kept out of the score. SAFETY (confirmed): the thirdPartyVPNPeers response includes 'secret' (the pre-shared key). CORE must pick only name/publicIp/ikeVersion/networkTags and never log or send the rest; CORE's read cache holds the whole response in memory for up to 90 s (memory only) and the shape watcher records field names only, so nothing writes the value to disk. Meraki only; UniFi and other brands have no source.
- Needs: The agreed IT Glue place for VPN documentation (asset type or title rule); without it only 'no tunnels' can be answered automatically. Also a decision that this key should not lower the posture score.
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoints and the 'secret' field are confirmed. Changed: (1) the proposed 'partly when tunnels exist' status would cost posture-score points for simply having a VPN, so the tunnels-exist case should become needs_person (or met/not_met from the documentation convention), not a check status; (2) third-party peers with networkTags ['none'] must be excluded; (3) siteToSiteVpn mode values are not listed on Cisco's page; (4) effort raised from M to L because the question as worded needs both the CORE check and a first-ever client_documents reader plus a convention (the check alone is M).

**A007. Has a recent running configuration backup been exported and added to the documentation? Does not include Cisco Meraki or UniFi UDM. Create ticket to verify that location is in Auvik and backups are turned on.**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 20; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Auvik API
- How: New check (suggested key auvik_config_backup) in the existing Auvik sweep (aisoc-auvik-counts.ts: checksFor is () => [] and the spec says countsOnly: true, which must be removed): GET /v1/inventory/configuration?tenants=...&filter[isRunning]=true (attributes backupTime, isRunning; relationships device, tenant), read in Auvik's published spec this session. Join to the device list the sweep already loads (/v1/inventory/device/info: deviceType, vendorName). Optional health signal: GET /v1/inventory/device/detail (manageStatus, discoveryStatus.login: authorized / notAuthorized / ...). A.C.O.R.N side: add 'auvik' to TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES in infra_review.py (not there today: a report under vendor 'auvik' is accepted and stored but never read by the review) and one CHECK_ANSWERS line.
- Rule: Judge only devices whose Auvik deviceType is firewall, utm or securityAppliance (the same set A.C.O.R.N's _AUVIK_ROLE treats as a firewall; 'router' is NOT in that set, so including it is an owner decision) and whose maker is not Cisco Meraki or Ubiquiti. met = every such firewall has a configuration with isRunning=true held in Auvik. partly = some do. not_met = none does. Do NOT use 'backed up within 30 days': Auvik's own articles say it scans about hourly and saves a new backup only when the configuration changed, so an unchanged firewall legitimately has an old backupTime. Put backupTime in the evidence, and use discoveryStatus.login (notAuthorized) from device/detail to say 'Auvik can no longer sign in' -> partly. Not sent when Auvik lists no such firewall. Exclusion as worded: when the inventory shows every firewall is Meraki or UniFi the honest answer is 'does not apply', which today can only be typed (the check status allows only met/partly/not_met and assessment_answers has no device input), so that needs new A.C.O.R.N code or a needs_person line saying so. The template's own instruction ('verify that location is in Auvik and backups are turned on') is what this measures; a copy filed in IT Glue is not checked. A non-Meraki firewall not in Auvik is invisible and stays with a person. Nothing was tried live; whether the API user's role may read configurations is UNCONFIRMED.
- Needs: Confirm the Auvik API user can read configurations for client tenants, that a running configuration held in Auvik counts as 'recent and documented', and whether Auvik 'router' devices should be judged as firewalls.
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, filters and attributes confirmed in Auvik's spec, and 'auvik' is confirmed missing from TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES. The 30-day freshness rule was wrong: Auvik backs up on change, so an old backupTime is normal for a stable firewall; replaced with 'a running configuration is held' plus the login-status signal from device/detail. Device types aligned with A.C.O.R.N's own firewall set (firewall, utm, securityAppliance; router is a decision). Noted the countsOnly flag on the Auvik sweep must be dropped.

**A008. Do all 3rd party vendors have working unique admin credentials to manage the device with access logging**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess'), with a new 12-month expiry so it falls back to needs_person when stale (review_overrides.merge_assess has no date test today, so typed answers never expire). Optional helper evidence for Meraki clients from the read CORE already makes: GET /organizations/{id}/admins (already used for meraki_admin_2fa); Meraki also has GET /organizations/{organizationId}/configurationChanges (adminName, adminEmail, ts; up to 365 days back) as the access log.
- Rule: No system knows which outside vendors (phone, ISP, line-of-business provider) are supposed to manage the firewall, or whether their logins work, so the status is a person's: met / not_met / not_applicable (all three are allowed typed statuses for 'assess'). Lightest honest upkeep: the typed answer carries its date and is shown as due for re-confirmation after 12 months. For Meraki only, a tool could add a helper sentence under the question, not an answer: 'N dashboard administrators, each a named account; M are from outside the MSP's and the client's own e-mail domains', since Meraki accounts are per person and every change is logged by administrator. That supports a 'yes' but cannot decide it, and nothing comparable exists for UniFi, SonicWall or Fortinet in CORE.
- Needs: Decision: add a 12-month expiry to typed answers for person-only questions (applies to every such question, not just this one).

**A009. Is the firewall covered with a hardware warranty / Advanced RMA?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 7; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Device inventory (firewall brand and model) plus the Meraki checks already stored; Meraki MX / Z-series firewalls only; an inference from Meraki's published warranty policy, not a per-device entitlement lookup
- How: New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N over data it already holds: infra_review.network_role(device) == ('firewall', 'Meraki') with the device's model; tool_checks['meraki_end_of_support'] (status and items, which are per model, e.g. 'MX64 x1: past end of support' / '...: end of support within 6 months'); tool_checks['meraki_licence']. assessment_answers takes only checks today, so the device list must be passed in. Later extensions, each a CORE change: Auvik GET /v1/inventory/device/warranty (warrantyCoverageStatus, warrantyExpirationDate, serviceCoverageStatus; confirmed in the spec, which says the value 'is what is returned by Cisco device', so probably Cisco gear only) and IT Glue configuration attribute warranty-expires-at, which configurationRecord() does not keep (attribute name not re-read this session).
- Rule: Basis (Meraki's returns/warranty page, read this session): 'MX or Z-Series: Lifetime'; lifetime ends at the product's End-of-Support date; a replacement ships in advance once Support confirms the fault; 'You must have a current service contract to return a defective part'; licence validity does not extend the warranty; datasheets take precedence. Apply only when every firewall of the client is Meraki and its model starts MX or Z (vMX is virtual; other appliance models such as C8000 have a 2-year warranty -> needs_person). met = no firewall model appears in the meraki_end_of_support items and meraki_licence is met or partly. partly = the firewall model's item says end of support within 6 months. not_met = the firewall model's item says past end of support, or meraki_licence is not_met. needs_person for a non-Meraki firewall or none listed. Caveats: (1) meraki_end_of_support is not sent when no device has an announced date (endOfSupportCheck returns null) AND when the inventory read failed; A.C.O.R.N cannot tell these apart, so treating 'absent' as met is a small guess unless CORE is changed to send met 'no end date announced'; (2) this is the first review rule to read 'items', and it matches on model text; (3) the policy does not cover second-hand or grey-market units, which no data shows. UniFi has no warranty data.
- Needs: Confirm he accepts 'Meraki lifetime warranty until end of support, with a current licence' as the answer for Meraki MX/Z firewalls, and whether an absent end-of-support check may be read as 'no end date announced'. For other brands: a convention that the firewall's IT Glue configuration has its warranty expiry date filled in (and CORE changed to keep it).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds and the warranty basis was confirmed on Meraki's page (MX and Z-series lifetime, ends at end of support, advance replacement, current service contract needed). Changed: limit the rule to MX/Z models (other appliance models have different terms); 'absent' end-of-support check also happens on a failed read, so it is not a clean 'met'; effort raised S to M because assessment_answers needs a new device input and this is the first rule to read check items; stated plainly that this is a policy inference, not a measured entitlement.

**A010. Is the Network Appliance (Firewall/Router End of Support?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 7; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki check already stored (meraki_end_of_support) combined with the device inventory; Meraki firewalls only
- How: New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N: take the client's firewalls from the inventory (infra_review.network_role(device) == ('firewall', 'Meraki'), with model) and look for each model in tool_checks['meraki_end_of_support'].items, which CORE sends per model ('MX64 x1: past end of support', '...: end of support within 6 months'; end of support is a per-model date, so a model match is exact). assessment_answers takes only checks today, so the device list must be passed in. Same machinery as A009. Alternative of equal size: a firewall-only CORE key (filter productType 'appliance' on the inventory rows endOfSupportCheck already reads, and send met when no date is announced), after which a single CHECK_ANSWERS line would be honest.
- Rule: Polarity: YES is bad; the rule is written in the good direction. Only when the client has at least one Meraki firewall in the inventory: not_met ('Yes, end of support') = a firewall model appears in the items as past end of support; partly = a firewall model appears as within 6 months; met ('No') = the check is present and no firewall model appears in its items (including when the check is not_met only because of switches or access points). needs_person when the client has no Meraki firewall, any non-Meraki firewall, or the check is absent. Absent is common: endOfSupportCheck returns null when no device has an announced date and also when the inventory read failed, so clients with all-current gear stay needs_person until CORE sends met 'no end date announced' (small CORE change). The plain one-line mapping proposed does not hold as worded: the check counts every device in the organization's Meraki inventory (switches, access points, and claimed spares not in any network), so an old access point would mark the firewall end of support, and a client with a non-Meraki firewall but current Meraki switches would get a false 'met'. UniFi has no end-of-support data; for Cisco gear in Auvik, GET /v1/inventory/device/lifecycle (lastSupportStatus, securitySoftwareMaintenanceStatus; confirmed in the spec, Cisco-sourced) could feed a second key later.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The key meraki_end_of_support exists and its polarity is right, but it does not answer THIS question as worded: it judges every Meraki device in the organization's inventory, not the firewall, and it is not tied to the client's firewall being Meraki at all. A mapping line alone would give a false 'end of support' from an old access point or spare, and a false 'supported' for a client whose firewall is another brand. Changed from map_existing_check (S, high) to derive_from_held_data (M, medium): a rule that matches the inventory's firewall model against the check's per-model items. The readiness list already uses this check for the question it does answer ('Is the network equipment still supported by its maker?').

**A011. Does the firewall support content filtering and if so, is it turned on?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 19; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki Dashboard API (Meraki firewalls only)
- How: New check (suggested key meraki_content_filtering) in the Meraki sweep: GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/contentFiltering per appliance network, reading blockedUrlCategories[] (id, name), blockedUrlPatterns, allowedUrlPatterns, urlCategoryListSize (path and fields read on Cisco's page). Use GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/settings deploymentMode ('routed' / 'passthrough', confirmed) to leave out passthrough/VPN-concentrator networks. Then one CHECK_ANSWERS line (source 'meraki' already reaches assessment_answers).
- Rule: met = every routed appliance network blocks at least one URL category. partly = some do and some do not. not_met = none blocks any category (a blocked-URL list alone counts as not_met). Evidence gives network counts and up to three category names, clipped to 300. Not sent with no appliance network or when any read fails. Leave out passthrough networks (content filtering does not apply to a concentrator and would give a false not_met). The 'does it support it' half: Cisco's API page states no licence requirement, but Meraki's content-filtering documentation (seen only through a search result of its translated page, so UNCONFIRMED in the English original) says the feature needs an Advanced Security or Secure SD-WAN Plus licence; an Enterprise-licensed MX would therefore read as 'off' when it is really 'not licensed'. The check should say only what is switched on, and the evidence for not_met should add 'or the licence does not include it'. Whether the licence edition can be read from the licence overview CORE already fetches is UNCONFIRMED. How template-bound networks answer is UNCONFIRMED. dnsfilter_threat_blocking is a different layer (DNS filtering on machines) and does not answer this question as worded; it may be shown as a second evidence line. UniFi and other brands have no source and keep a typed answer. The new check counts in the posture score at weight 1.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and endpoint hold (path and fields confirmed). Added: exclude passthrough networks using deploymentMode from /appliance/settings (confirmed), otherwise a VPN concentrator produces a false not_met; the feature is licence-gated per Meraki's own documentation (not stated on the API page), so not_met must be worded as 'off or not licensed'; do not send on a partial read; the check enters the posture score.

### Assessment: Network and infrastructure / Network Switches

**A012. Is the Network Switch(es) End of Support? (If yes document date in comments)**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 7; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Meraki (existing meraki_end_of_support check and its item list) crossed with the device inventory; Auvik lifecycle for other brands (not collected)
- How: client_m365_checks row source "meraki", key meraki_end_of_support (hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts endOfSupportCheck; items "MODEL xN: past end of support" / "MODEL xN: end of support within 6 months"), crossed with merged devices that carry a DeviceSource vendor=meraki with details.product_type="switch". The whole check dict, items included, already reaches assessment_answers() through tool_checks; only the device list is new plumbing. More robust alternative with no new API call: a switch-only key in the Meraki sweep built from the same GET /organizations/{id}/inventory/devices rows (each row has productType, networkId, eox.endOfSupportAt per Cisco's page), which could also carry the date and send met when no switch has a date. Non-Meraki: Auvik GET /v1/inventory/device/lifecycle (attributes salesAvailability, softwareMaintenanceStatus, securitySoftwareMaintenanceStatus, lastSupportStatus; shared enum covered, available, expired, securityOnly, unpublished, empty; confirmed in Auvik's spec; CORE's Auvik sweep sends no checks).
- Rule: Polarity: yes is bad, so met = no switch past end of support. A straight key mapping is NOT honest: the check counts every Meraki product AND unused inventory (CORE calls inventory/devices with no usedState filter), so an old access point or a spare in a box would fail the switch question. Rule, only when the client has at least one listed switch and every one has a Meraki source: check met -> met. Check partly/not_met -> take the model at the start of each item and compare (tolerantly; a merged device's model may be Auvik's makeModel text) with the models of the client's deployed Meraki switches: a 'past end of support' item matches -> not_met; only 'within 6 months' items match -> partly; none match -> met. Gaps that remain: (1) no date in items or evidence, though the template asks for it; (2) CORE sends no check when no device has an end-of-support date (endOfSupportCheck returns null), so a client with all-current gear stays needs_person rather than met; (3) UniFi: no end-of-support field read; (4) Auvik-only switches need a new check: lastSupportStatus expired -> not_met, covered/available -> met, unpublished/empty -> needs_person (value names confirmed, their exact meaning per field is not).
- Needs: Decision: does 'end of support within 6 months' show as Partly. Decision: parse the existing items in A.C.O.R.N, or add a small switch-only Meraki check in CORE that carries the date and can say met (my recommendation; fixes gaps 1 and 2). Go-ahead for a first Auvik check sweep (source "auvik" is not in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES).
- Reviewer (corrected): Key, item format, null-when-undated and the 'counts all products' objection all confirmed in code. Fixed: (a) the proposal said assessment_answers() gets no items; it already gets the full check dict including items (tool_checks holds the whole dict), only devices are missing. (b) network_role()'s brand is not a safe Meraki test: the endpoint sorts a device's sources alphabetically, so a Meraki switch that Auvik also lists is read through its Auvik source first and gets the maker's name (for example 'Cisco Meraki'), not 'Meraki'; test for a meraki source instead. (c) The check also counts unused inventory, which the proposal did not mention. (d) Auvik lifecycle value names, which the proposal left unread, are confirmed. (e) Effort raised from S to M: two new inputs, text parsing that couples A.C.O.R.N to CORE's sentence format, and it still cannot say met for most healthy clients without a CORE change.

**A013. Is the Network Switch(es) currently under warranty? Excludes Meraki (automatically has a warranty) UniFi devices usually carry a 1yr limited warranty and are cheap to replace but device availability could impact shipping and receiving times. For any non Meraki or UniFi device, verify EoL via manuf...**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: Device inventory (Meraki / UniFi / Auvik); Auvik warranty data or IT Glue configuration warranty date for other brands (neither collected)
- How: Merged devices where infra_review.network_role(device) gives role "switch"; classify Meraki / UniFi by the presence of a meraki or unifi DeviceSource (or the maker text), not by network_role's brand. UniFi: DeviceSource.details.adopted_at (CORE sends it from adoptionTime; stored, read by nothing). Other brands: Auvik GET /v1/inventory/device/warranty (attributes deviceName, warrantyCoverageStatus, warrantyExpirationDate, serviceCoverageStatus, serviceAttachmentStatus, contractRenewalAvailability, recommendedSoftwareVersion; boolean filters coveredUnderWarranty / coveredUnderService; confirmed in Auvik's spec, not collected). Or IT Glue configuration attribute warranty-expires-at (confirmed in IT Glue's reference; on rows CORE already fetches, dropped by configurationRecord()).
- Rule: met = under warranty. Every listed switch is Meraki -> met, evidence 'N Meraki switches; the template treats Meraki as covered'. Do not turn an expired Meraki licence into not_met without the owner's say: the template says Meraki is automatically covered, and meraki_licence is an organization-wide licence state, not a hardware warranty (show it as evidence only). UniFi switches: template says a 1-year limited warranty; adopted_at under 365 days ago suggests covered, older suggests out, but adoption date is not purchase date (and Ubiquiti's page shows it null on a console), so Partly at most with the date as evidence. Any other brand: needs_person today. With an Auvik check: use the coveredUnderWarranty filter (boolean) rather than warrantyCoverageStatus, whose values the spec does not list (UNCONFIRMED): all covered -> met, some -> partly, none -> not_met, earliest warrantyExpirationDate in evidence. Auvik's warranty data may exist only for some makers (UNCONFIRMED). The cut-off tail of the question ('verify EoL via manuf...') is A012.
- Needs: Decision: should an expired Meraki licence make this Not met (proposer's idea) or stay Met per the template. Decision: how UniFi switches read after a year (Not met, Partly, or Not applicable as cheap to replace). Go-ahead for an Auvik warranty check, or a convention to always fill warranty-expires-at on IT Glue switch configurations.
- Reviewer (corrected): Stored fields and both vendor sources confirmed (Auvik warranty attribute names read in the spec; IT Glue warranty-expires-at seen in the official example; configurationRecord() keeps neither warranty nor purchase date). Fixed: (a) the meraki_licence not_met -> not_met branch goes beyond the question, which excludes Meraki outright; made it an owner decision and evidence only. (b) Brand must not come from network_role(): with sources sorted alphabetically an Auvik-listed Meraki or UniFi switch is branded by its maker text and would be counted as 'other brand'. (c) Auvik rule should use the boolean coveredUnderWarranty filter, since the status field's values are not published. S effort covers the Meraki / UniFi rule only; the Auvik or IT Glue route is separate work (M).

**A014. Is the Network Switch(es) Password Complex? Excludes Meraki and UniFi devices.**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: Device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik) for whether the question applies; none for the password itself
- How: Merged devices where infra_review.network_role(device) gives role "switch", counted as Meraki, UniFi or other. Classify by which DeviceSource vendors the device has (meraki / unifi) plus the maker text, not by network_role's brand string. Use the client's tool mapping (enrolled_in, already built in the review endpoint) to know whether Auvik watches this client. One shared helper serves A014, A015, A016.
- Rule: Two questions in one. Tool part (does it apply): at least one switch listed and every one is Meraki or UniFi -> automatic 'does not apply: all N listed switches are Meraki/UniFi'. Any other-brand switch (these only arrive through Auvik) -> needs_person, naming them. No switch listed -> needs_person. Weakness to state on the page: for a client that is not in Auvik, the only tools that list switches are Meraki and UniFi, so 'every listed switch is Meraki/UniFi' is true by construction and proves nothing about a Netgear or HP switch nobody lists. So: assert 'does not apply' only when the client is mapped in Auvik and Auvik lists no other-brand switch; otherwise show it as a suggestion a person confirms. Person part: whether the password is complex. No system holds it and CORE never asks IT Glue for a password value; typed answer, re-checked after 12 months or when the set of other-brand switches changes. Status note: assessment_answers() emits only met / partly / not_met / needs_person today; AssessAnswer.status is a plain string and review_overrides allows not_applicable for typed answers, so an automatic not_applicable needs a check of the page, CSV and history (not verified here).
- Needs: Decision: may A.C.O.R.N set Not applicable automatically, and only for Auvik-watched clients or for all. Agree a 12-month expiry for typed answers (review_overrides has no expiry today).
- Reviewer (corrected): Confirmed: not_applicable is typed-only today, no expiry exists, CORE reads IT Glue passwords without show_password, the password fact itself is person-only. Fixed two things. (1) The brand count as proposed is wrong for exactly the clients it matters for: the endpoint sorts each device's sources alphabetically, so a Meraki or UniFi switch that Auvik also lists is read via its Auvik source and branded by maker ('Cisco Meraki', 'Ubiquiti'), landing in 'other'. (2) The proposal's 'limit' is stronger than stated: without Auvik the rule cannot fail, so the automatic 'does not apply' should be firm only for Auvik-watched clients and a suggestion elsewhere.

**A015. Has the Network Switch(es) administrative password been changed in the last one (1) year? Does not apply to Network as a Service products such as Meraki or UniFi**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 16; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue password entry dates (never the password); device inventory for whether the question applies
- How: Applies-or-not from the device inventory as A014 (same corrected brand test). For other-brand switches: IT Glue GET /organizations/{id}/relationships/passwords, already read by hud/src/aisoc-itglue-docs.ts without show_password. CORE keeps only name, username, URL, category and the entry's updated-at, stored as client_documents.vendor_updated_at (kind "password", folder = category). The wanted field is the password attribute password-updated-at: UNCONFIRMED in IT Glue's own reference (the Passwords section could not be reached this session either; only community sources list it). CORE's DocRecord has no slot for it, and the attributes that tie a password to a configuration (resource-id / resource-type) are likewise community-sourced and not kept. The client review endpoint reads no client_documents today.
- Rule: All listed switches Meraki/UniFi -> does not apply (as A014, with the same limit). Otherwise, once the convention exists: for each other-brand switch find its password entry; password changed within 365 days on all -> met; some -> partly; none -> not_met with the oldest date; no matching entry -> needs_person ('no switch password entry found in IT Glue'). Convention needed: one IT Glue password entry per switch (embedded in the switch's configuration, or in one agreed category with the switch name in the entry name), AND the team updates that entry whenever the device password is rotated, because the date proves the IT Glue entry changed, not the switch. Interim with held data: the entry's last-edit date is evidence for a person only (an edit can be a rename). Only dates and names are read.
- Needs: The convention: which IT Glue category or naming marks a switch's admin login, and that rotations are always recorded there. One live look that IT Glue returns password-updated-at (and resource-id / resource-type) on password rows. Same Not-applicable decision as A014.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, effort and low confidence hold; code claims confirmed (passwordRecord keeps name, username, URL, category, updated-at; vendor_updated_at exists; the review endpoint never reads client_documents; the IT Glue docs sweep is marked not verified live in its own header). Added what was missing: the work spans three places (CORE must send the new date, A.C.O.R.N must store it, the review must read documents and match entries to switches); the date only proves the IT Glue entry changed, so the convention must include recording every rotation; the brand test inherited from A014 needed the same fix. password-updated-at stays UNCONFIRMED.

**A016. Can all switches be managed via browser? (Does not apply to Meraki or UniFi devices.)**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: Device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik) for whether the question applies; none for the web interface itself
- How: Same helper as A014: merged devices with role "switch", counted as Meraki, UniFi or other by their DeviceSource vendors and maker text (not network_role's brand string), plus whether the client is mapped in Auvik.
- Rule: Tool part: every listed switch is Meraki or UniFi -> automatic 'does not apply', firm only when Auvik watches the client and lists no other-brand switch, a suggestion otherwise (without Auvik the rule cannot fail; see A014). Any other-brand switch -> needs_person, naming the switches. Person part: whether each of those switches has a working web management page. Nothing collected shows it: Auvik's device detail has discoveryStatus.snmp / login (values disabled, determining, notSupported, notAuthorized, authorizing, authorized, privileged; confirmed in the spec) which says whether Auvik can sign in, not whether a browser page exists, and no open-port data is stored for network gear. It is a property of the hardware, so a typed answer re-checked when the client's set of other-brand switch models changes, otherwise after 12 months.
- Needs: Same decisions as A014 (automatic Not applicable and for which clients; typed-answer expiry). Nothing else.
- Reviewer (corrected): The split (tools answer 'does it apply', a person answers the rest) holds and Auvik's discoveryStatus values are now confirmed. Same two fixes as A014: the brand count must not use network_role()'s brand (Auvik-listed Meraki/UniFi switches would be counted as other brands), and the automatic 'does not apply' is only meaningful for clients Auvik watches.

**A017. Is the Network Switch(es) running the latest Major revision of the firmware?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 13; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Meraki (new read); UniFi (already held, unused); Auvik for other brands
- How: Meraki: GET /networks/{networkId}/firmwareUpgrades -> products.switch.currentVersion {id, firmware, shortName, releaseType, releaseDate}, isUpgradeAvailable (boolean: a recommended upgrade exists), availableVersions[] with the same fields, lastUpgrade, nextUpgrade (all confirmed on Cisco's page; read-only; not called anywhere in CORE). A new key such as meraki_switch_firmware in the Meraki sweep (does not exist today). UniFi: DeviceSource.details.firmware_status and update_available plus Device.os_version are sent by aisoc-unifi.ts and read by nothing; the UniFi sweep sends no checks (checksFor returns []), so either a first UniFi check in CORE or an A.C.O.R.N rule over devices, with no new call. Auvik-listed switches: details.firmware_version held; recommendedSoftwareVersion is an attribute of Auvik's warranty resource (name confirmed, meaning and coverage UNCONFIRMED).
- Rule: met = on the latest major line. Meraki, per network that has switches: the major number of currentVersion equals the highest major among availableVersions whose releaseType is stable, or isUpgradeAvailable is false -> met; a newer stable major exists -> not_met; some networks behind -> partly. A newer minor in the same major is still met. UniFi: firmware_status upToDate -> met; update waiting -> compare the major of os_version with update_available: same major -> met (note the minor), higher -> not_met; mixed -> partly. Other brands: needs_person. UNCONFIRMED and to check on first live read: the text format of shortName for switches (Cisco's own example for the switch block is copied from camera data), release types beyond the two the page shows (stable, beta), and whether Catalyst switches managed in the dashboard report under products.switch. UniFi fields follow Ubiquiti's published page (firmwareStatus 'upToDate, updateAvailable, etc.'; updateAvailable a version string or null) and were never read live, per CORE's own header. Cost: one call per network; CORE already caps per-network Wi-Fi reads at 40 per organization.
- Needs: No new credential (existing Meraki and UniFi keys). Decision: ignore beta / candidate releases when judging 'latest' (proposed: stable only).
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, field names (including isUpgradeAvailable) and the UniFi stored fields all confirmed; category and effort stand. Small fixes only: the proposal relied on a 'candidate' release type and on parsing a major number from shortName, neither of which Cisco's page shows for switches (its switch example is camera data), so both are flagged UNCONFIRMED; Catalyst-in-dashboard coverage is unknown; and the UniFi half is not a 'check' today because the UniFi sweep sends none, so it needs either a first UniFi check or the device list passed into the review.

**A018. Do 3rd party vendors have unique working admin credentials to manage the device?**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none (the Meraki administrator list can give supporting facts)
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section "assess"; not_applicable is an allowed typed status there). Supporting facts only: Meraki GET /organizations/{organizationId}/admins, already read by CORE for meraki_admin_2fa; each admin has email, orgAccess (full, read-only, enterprise, camera-sensor, none), accountStatus (ok, locked, pending, unverified), lastActive, authenticationMethod, hasApiKey (confirmed on Cisco's page). CORE uses only accountStatus, authenticationMethod and the 2FA flag and stores none of the rest.
- Rule: Needs a person: whether an outside vendor manages the switch at all, and whether they were given their own login and not a shared one, is an arrangement no tool records. Lightest honest upkeep: a typed answer (Met / Not met / Not applicable when no vendor manages the device) that expires after 12 months. Optional help for Meraki clients, as evidence beside the question and never as the answer: list dashboard administrators whose e-mail domain is neither the MSP's nor the client's, with accountStatus and lastActive. A Meraki login is one per e-mail address, so those are unique by construction and lastActive speaks to 'working'; it cannot show a vendor using someone else's login, and nothing comparable exists for local accounts on other switch brands.
- Needs: Agree the 12-month expiry for typed answers (none exists today). If the Meraki helper is wanted: the list of the MSP's own e-mail domains.

**A019. Are all network switches managed and configured?**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none for the answer; the device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik) lists the managed switches as supporting evidence
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section "assess"). Beside it, as evidence: the client's listed switches from merged devices where infra_review.network_role(device) gives role "switch" (count per tool and models). A later real signal for Auvik-watched clients would be a new CORE read of Auvik GET /v1/inventory/device/detail (manageStatus boolean; discoveryStatus.snmp / login with values such as authorized, notAuthorized; confirmed in the spec, not collected).
- Rule: A person answers; A.C.O.R.N shows 'the network tools list N switches (x Meraki, y UniFi, z seen by Auvik), all of them managed types' next to the question and never sets the status itself. Reason: on the data held the rule can only ever come out Met. Meraki's device list contains only devices already assigned to a network (Cisco's own description of the call CORE uses), so the 'Meraki switch with no network' branch can never fire; UniFi's isManaged is a boolean whose meaning Ubiquiti does not document, and CORE's UniFi fields were never read live; and an unmanaged switch has no management interface, so no tool lists it. The thing the question asks about is exactly what the tools cannot see. Upkeep: typed answer that expires after 12 months and is flagged for re-check when the client's list of switches changes. If Auvik's manageStatus / discoveryStatus is collected later, an Auvik-seen switch that is not managed or where sign-in is notAuthorized could raise an automatic not_met; that would be a new CORE check.
- Needs: Agree the 12-month expiry for typed answers. Optional: go-ahead to collect Auvik manageStatus / discoveryStatus so a switch Auvik sees but cannot manage is flagged automatically.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The automatic route does not hold. The proposer flagged two things as not verified; both resolve against the rule: Cisco describes GET /organizations/{id}/devices as listing only devices 'assigned to a network', so details.network_id is always set and that not_met branch is dead; and isManaged has no documented meaning. With unmanaged switches invisible to every tool, the rule returns Met whenever any switch is listed, which is a constant, not a measurement. Changed to person_only with the inventory as evidence beside the typed answer; the Auvik device-detail read is kept as a possible later check.

**A020. Are all switch ports 1 Gbps or faster?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence low).
- Source: Device inventory: switch models (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik) against a small model table kept in A.C.O.R.N; Auvik link speeds as supporting evidence
- How: Device.model of merged devices where infra_review.network_role(device) gives role "switch", looked up in a new model table in A.C.O.R.N (model or model prefix -> slowest access-port speed). The table does not exist today and is new reference data, not something held. Match tolerantly: a merged device's model may be Auvik's makeModel text. Supporting only: Auvik DeviceSource.details.connected_to[].speed (Auvik's negotiatedSpeed, a string such as "1.0 Gbit/s" per the spec; at most 24 links per device; read today only by client_environment.py). Not collected: Meraki GET /organizations/{organizationId}/switch/ports/statuses/bySwitch (per port: speed from "10 Mbps" to "400 Gbps" or empty, status, enabled; confirmed on Cisco's page; paged at 3 to 20 switches per page).
- Rule: The question is about what the ports can do; the tools report only the speed each link negotiated, which can be low because of the attached device or cable. So judge by model: every listed switch model is in the table at 1 Gbps or faster -> met, evidence lists the models; any model in the table as Fast Ethernet -> not_met naming it; a model not in the table -> needs_person (and the table gains a line once). Negotiated speeds are evidence beside the answer ('k links negotiate under 1 Gbps') and must not by themselves make it not met. UNCONFIRMED: that every Meraki MS and UniFi switch model has gigabit or faster ports; I did not check that in vendor documentation either, so the table must be filled from the models actually in the fleet and checked by the team, not by a blanket prefix rule. Limit: switches no tool lists (unmanaged ones, the likeliest to be old 100 Mbps units) are invisible, so Met means 'every switch the tools list'. Plumbing: assessment_answers() needs the device list passed in.
- Needs: Who confirms each model's port speed for the table (one-off, then per new model). Decision: whether Met may be shown when unlisted switches cannot be ruled out, or whether a person confirms once. Optional: collect Meraki per-port speeds as extra evidence (several paged reads per organization, not one).
- Reviewer (corrected): Route kept at low confidence. Fixed details: Auvik's negotiatedSpeed format, which the proposal left unverified, is a string like '1.0 Gbit/s' in the spec; the Meraki per-port read is not 'one more read per organization' but pages of at most 20 switches; the model table is new hand-kept reference data and should be stated as such; model matching must tolerate Auvik's makeModel text on merged devices; and the unlisted-switch blind spot, which the proposal raised for A014 and A019, applies here too and was not mentioned.

### Assessment: Network and infrastructure / Wireless Access

**A021. Are the wireless access points under warranty? (N/A to Meraki or UniFi.)**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik rows) for the not-applicable part; Auvik API for other brands
- How: New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N over the devices the endpoint already loads (`shown`, endpoints/client_review.py): infra_review.network_role(device) == ('wireless', brand). assessment_answers() takes only a checks dict today (assessment_review.py line 90; called at the assess_worked line), so its signature and the endpoint call must change. Follow-on for other brands only: new Auvik check from GET /v1/inventory/device/warranty. Response attributes confirmed in Auvik's published spec (schema deviceWarrantyAttributes): deviceName, recommendedSoftwareVersion, serviceCoverageStatus, warrantyCoverageStatus, warrantyExpirationDate, optional serviceAttachmentStatus and contractRenewalAvailability. All are free-text strings with no enum.
- Rule: The template excludes Meraki and UniFi. At least one access point listed and every one is Meraki or UniFi: automatic not_applicable, evidence 'All N access points are Meraki/UniFi; the template does not ask this of them'. No access point listed: needs_person. Any other-brand access point listed: needs_person, evidence naming the models. Brand test: network_role returns 'Meraki'/'UniFi' only when the Meraki or UniFi tool lists the device; when only Auvik lists it the brand is the maker string Auvik reports, so match loosely on 'meraki' / 'ubiquiti' / 'unifi'. Limit: an access point no tool lists cannot be seen, so a client with Meraki APs plus one unmanaged AP would wrongly get not_applicable. Auvik follow-on (met = every other-brand AP covered, not_met = all expired): UNCONFIRMED how to code it, because warrantyCoverageStatus and warrantyExpirationDate are free text with no documented format, and which brands Auvik has warranty data for is not documented in the spec. A live row must be looked at first.
- Needs: Decision: may A.C.O.R.N mark a question 'not applicable' automatically (today only a person can). For the Auvik follow-on: a look at one live Auvik warranty row before the rule is written.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds for the Meraki/UniFi part. Fixed: (1) effort S to M: this is the first rule that needs the device list passed into assessment_answers, plus an automatic not_applicable status, and the others in this group reuse that work. (2) Page side now checked: ClientReview.tsx renders not_applicable by status whether typed or not, but reviewExport.ts answeredAutomatically() returns false for not_applicable, so an automatic N/A would be counted as not automatic in the export until that is changed. (3) The Auvik warranty attribute names are confirmed, but they are free-text strings, so the proposed met/partly/not_met rule cannot be written from the spec alone. (4) Auvik-only brand matching needs a loose maker match. Confirmed: Auvik sends no checks (checksFor returns []) and 'auvik' is not in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES. No warranty field is read anywhere in CORE's IT Glue, Auvik, Meraki or UniFi files (grep, zero hits).

**A022. Is the wireless access point administrative password complex? (Does not apply to any Network as a Service devices. i.e. Meraki, UniFi.)**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik rows); none for other brands
- How: Same rule and plumbing as A021: infra_review.network_role(device) == 'wireless' plus brand, over the devices already loaded; assessment_answers() needs the device list passed in. No tool holds the strength of a local admin password: CORE's IT Glue sweep reads a password entry's name, user name, category and link only and never the value (aisoc-itglue-docs.ts header and passwordRecord).
- Rule: The template excludes network-as-a-service gear. At least one AP listed and all are Meraki or UniFi: automatic not_applicable, evidence 'All N access points are Meraki/UniFi; the template does not ask this of them'. No AP listed: needs_person. Any other-brand AP listed: needs_person with evidence naming the models; a person types the answer. Never infer complexity from an IT Glue entry existing. Same limits as A021: automatic not_applicable is not an automatic status today, and an AP no tool lists cannot be seen.
- Needs: Same decision as A021 (automatic 'not applicable'). For other-brand APs: whether a typed answer older than 12 months should be shown as due for re-check (no expiry exists today).
- Reviewer (corrected): Route and category hold. One wording fix: the proposed evidence said 'there is no local admin password' for Meraki/UniFi. That is not something A.C.O.R.N holds (UniFi devices do have a device login set in the console), so the evidence should cite the template's own exclusion instead. Effort S is right only once A021's plumbing exists.

**A023. Is the Wireless Access Point running the latest major firmware release?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 13; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: UniFi device rows already stored (firmware_status, update_available, os_version); Meraki API for Meraki APs (not collected)
- How: UniFi, held today and read by nothing: DeviceSource.details.firmware_status and details.update_available plus Device.os_version, sent by aisoc-unifi.ts from GET https://api.ui.com/v1/devices through the device sync (aisoc-devices.ts line 846). Ubiquiti's field reference: firmwareStatus is 'Status of device firmware (upToDate, updateAvailable, etc.)'; updateAvailable is the version of an available update, null when none. Meraki, not held: os_version has the running firmware but no 'latest'. Needs a new check in the Meraki sweep from GET /networks/{networkId}/firmwareUpgrades: products.wireless.currentVersion {firmware, shortName, releaseType, releaseDate}, availableVersions[], isUpgradeAvailable (confirmed in Cisco's reference). Auvik-listed other brands: firmware_version is stored but there is nothing to compare it with.
- Rule: Scope: devices whose network_role is 'wireless'. UniFi: met when every AP has firmware_status 'upToDate', or its update_available has the same major number as os_version; partly when some APs have an update to a higher major number; not_met when all do. Any other firmware_status value: needs_person (the full value list is not documented). Evidence gives counts and models. Meraki APs: needs_person until the new check exists; then met when currentVersion is the newest stable major among availableVersions on every wireless network, partly when some networks are behind, not_met when all are. Do not use isUpgradeAvailable alone: Cisco defines it as 'an upgraded recommended firmware version is available', which also fires for a minor release. releaseType has no documented enum (examples show stable and beta). Mixed estates take the worst result. Other-brand APs: needs_person. Not verified: CORE's UniFi file says it was never checked against a live account, and 'upToDate' is relative to the release channel the console is set to.
- Needs: A look at one stored UniFi device row to confirm the firmware_status and update_available values before the rule is trusted. For Meraki: agreement to one more read per wireless network per day (CORE caps wireless networks at 40 per organization).
- Reviewer (corrected): The UniFi part holds as derive_from_held_data: the fields exist, the device sync sends them, and Ubiquiti's reference gives the two values. The Meraki part is honestly a new_core_check, so the question is only automatic for UniFi clients until that is built; effort raised S to M to cover both. Fixed the Meraki rule: isUpgradeAvailable means a newer recommended version exists, not a newer major one, so the proposal's 'or isUpgradeAvailable false' shortcut is fine for met but must not drive partly/not_met. Added a fallback for undocumented firmware_status values. Cisco's example payload for this endpoint is unreliable (it shows camera firmware under wireless), so the shape needs one live read.

**A024. Do we have working admin credentials to manage this device?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory: Meraki and UniFi source rows and when CORE last synced them; Auvik API for other brands
- How: New pure rule over held devices: access points (network_role 'wireless') with a 'meraki' or 'unifi' source row whose last_seen is recent. devices.py states last_seen is 'only when CORE last synced it', so a fresh value means CORE's key listed the device on that date. UniFi: Ubiquiti's List Devices returns devices only for hosts where the calling account is owner or super admin, so listing proves admin level. Meraki: listing proves the key reaches the organization, not that it is a full admin. Optional small CORE addition to prove it: GET /administered/identities/me (email of the current user) matched against GET /organizations/{id}/admins, which CORE already reads and which carries orgAccess (full, read-only, enterprise, none, camera-sensor). Follow-on for other brands: new Auvik check from GET /v1/inventory/device/detail, attribute discoveryStatus.login / .snmp (enum disabled, determining, notSupported, notAuthorized, authorizing, authorized, privileged; confirmed in Auvik's spec).
- Rule: met when at least one AP is listed, every listed AP is Meraki or UniFi, and each one's Meraki/UniFi source row was synced in the last 7 days: evidence 'N access points are managed in the Meraki dashboard / UniFi console, which CORE read on <date>'. needs_person when no AP is listed, the rows are stale, or any AP is of another brand (say in the evidence which APs were confirmed; do not call it partly, because nothing is known to be missing). With the Auvik check: met = login authorized or privileged on every other-brand AP, not_met = notAuthorized on any. Honest limits: this shows the MSP's API account reaches the device, not that a named technician can sign in; for Meraki 'admin' is assumed unless the orgAccess match is added. UNCONFIRMED: that /administered/identities/me returns the API key's owner (Cisco's page says only 'the current user').
- Needs: Confirm the Meraki API key belongs to a full organization administrator, or approve the small CORE addition that checks it. Nothing for UniFi.
- Reviewer (corrected): Route holds. Fixed: (1) a mixed estate was 'partly' in the proposal; a status means the practice is partly in place, and here the other-brand APs are simply unknown, so it is needs_person with evidence. (2) Use the source row's last_seen (CORE's sync time) for freshness; details.last_reported_at is when the Meraki device last reported to Meraki's cloud and says nothing about CORE's access. (3) Added a concrete way to remove the 'admin is assumed' gap for Meraki: orgAccess is on the admins rows CORE already fetches. Effort S assumes A021's plumbing exists. Auvik discoveryStatus enum confirmed in the spec.

**A025. Is the Wireless Access Point end of support?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 7; small; confidence medium).
- Source: Check meraki_end_of_support (status and items, already sent and stored) joined with the Meraki wireless device rows A.C.O.R.N holds; Auvik for other brands
- How: No CORE change needed for Meraki. aisoc-meraki-checks.ts endOfSupportCheck already sends meraki_end_of_support with items by model, in the form 'MR33 x2: past end of support' / 'MR42 x1: end of support within 6 months' (withItems; up to 40 items of 120 chars; items are accepted and stored by A.C.O.R.N's M365CheckIn). A.C.O.R.N already knows which models are access points: Meraki device rows with details.product_type 'wireless' and Device.model. New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N: intersect the check's items with the models of the client's listed Meraki access points. It needs the device list passed into assessment_answers (same plumbing as A021) and a special case, because CHECK_ANSWERS reads only status and evidence. Alternative if parsing an item string is unwanted: a separate CORE key (endOfSupportCheck over inventory rows whose productType is 'wireless'). Other brands: Auvik GET /v1/inventory/device/lifecycle, attributes lastSupportStatus, securitySoftwareMaintenanceStatus, softwareMaintenanceStatus, salesAvailability (enum covered, available, expired, securityOnly, unpublished, empty; confirmed in spec) as a later Auvik check.
- Rule: Polarity: 'yes' is the bad answer; a status means 'as the template wants', so met = no access point is past or within 180 days of end of support. Client has at least one listed Meraki AP and the check is present: check met -> met. Check partly or not_met -> look only at items whose model is one of the client's AP models: any 'past end of support' -> not_met; else any 'within 6 months' -> partly; else met (the flagged hardware is a switch or firewall). Start the evidence with the literal answer ('No: none of 6 access points...' / 'Yes: 2 MR33 are past end of support') so 'Met' beside this question is not misread. Check absent, or no Meraki AP listed: needs_person. Limits: CORE's check reads the organization inventory, which also holds unused stock (Cisco's reference has a usedState filter); matching against deployed AP models avoids a shelf spare flagging the client. A model with no end-of-support date from Cisco counts as supported. UniFi: no end-of-support field found in the Site Manager API, so UniFi-only clients stay needs_person. UNCONFIRMED: which brands Auvik's lifecycle data covers.
- Needs: Nothing for Meraki. For UniFi-only clients: a decision whether to keep a small maintained list of end-of-support UniFi models, or leave it to a person.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category changed from new_core_check to the cheaper derive_from_held_data. The proposal said the existing key 'names no model'; that is true of the evidence sentence but not of the check's items, which list each affected model with its count and state, and A.C.O.R.N stores items. Joined with the wireless device rows it already holds, A.C.O.R.N can answer for access points alone with no CORE change, and the same join answers the firewall and switch end-of-support questions. It is also more accurate than the proposed new key, which would have counted unused inventory. The proposal's interim rule (map only when the check is met) remains valid as a first step. Effort M to S, assuming A021's plumbing. The cost is a dependency on CORE's item wording, which a test should pin.

**A026. Is all wireless traffic encrypted using WPA2-PSK?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 12; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki (SSID rows CORE already reads)
- How: New key, for example meraki_wifi_encryption, in aisoc-meraki-checks.ts from GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/ssids, which readSsids() already fetches for meraki_open_wifi (no new request). Fields confirmed in Cisco's reference: enabled, authMode (psk, ipsk-*, 8021x-*, open, open-enhanced, open-enhanced-with-radius, open-with-radius, open-with-nac), encryptionMode (wep, wpa), wpaEncryptionMode ('WPA1 and WPA2', 'WPA1 only', 'WPA2 only', 'WPA3 Transition Mode', 'WPA3 only', 'WPA3 192-bit Security'; present only when the authMode requires encryption), splashPage. CORE reads encryptionMode, authMode and splashPage today but not wpaEncryptionMode, and the SSID list is not sent to A.C.O.R.N, so this cannot be derived there.
- Rule: Over enabled SSIDs. met = every one uses 'WPA2 only' or a WPA3 mode (shared passphrase, iPSK or 802.1X; the evidence says which). partly = any SSID on 'WPA1 and WPA2', or an open SSID behind a sign-in page, or an open-enhanced SSID (encrypted but not WPA2). not_met = any WEP, 'WPA1 only', or an open SSID with no sign-in page. Treat open, open-with-radius and open-with-nac as unencrypted; the existing meraki_open_wifi tests only authMode exactly 'open', so the new check must not copy that test. Evidence names SSIDs by network and name, never a passphrase. Interim with no CORE change: answer not_met when meraki_open_wifi is not_met; a met there proves nothing about WPA2. That interim needs its own code like the RDP special case, since CHECK_ANSWERS cannot map on one status only. Limits: at most 40 wireless networks per organization are read; Wi-Fi served by a firewall's built-in radio is a different endpoint and is not read; UniFi Wi-Fi settings are not in the API CORE uses, so UniFi clients stay needs_person. As worded, WPA2-Enterprise and WPA3 are not 'WPA2-PSK'; the rule counts them as meeting it.
- Needs: Decision: do WPA3 and WPA2-Enterprise count as 'met' for this question, and is an open guest network behind a sign-in page 'partly' or acceptable.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, endpoint and fields confirmed (wpaEncryptionMode and its six values are in Cisco's reference). Details fixed: the rule now covers the other open authModes (open-with-radius, open-with-nac are unencrypted; open-enhanced is encrypted but not WPA2), which the proposal and the existing open-Wi-Fi check both miss; the 'map only when not_met' interim is not expressible in CHECK_ANSWERS and needs a special case; and Wi-Fi on a firewall's own radio is outside this read.

**A027. Are strong passphrases in use for all wireless access points (13 characters, alphanumeric with symbols)?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 12; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki (SSID rows CORE already reads)
- How: New key, for example meraki_wifi_passphrase, from GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/ssids: field psk, 'only present when authMode is psk'; Cisco's reference says 'Read-only admins receive a masked value'. readSsids() already fetches these rows whole, so the passphrase is already in CORE's memory during the Meraki sweep for a full-admin key. The new code would test it in memory and send only counts and SSID names; the passphrase and its length must never be stored, logged, put in evidence or items, or sent. No new request.
- Rule: Over enabled SSIDs with authMode 'psk'. A passphrase passes when it has 13 or more characters and contains a letter, a digit and a symbol. met = all pass; partly = some fail; not_met = all fail. 802.1X and iPSK SSIDs have no single shared passphrase and are left out; if only those exist, send nothing (needs_person, or not_applicable if automatic N/A is allowed). Masked values: send nothing when psk is missing, is one repeated character, or the key's own orgAccess is not 'full' (orgAccess is on the admins rows CORE already reads). How the mask looks is UNCONFIRMED, so the check must be tried once against a live organization before it is trusted. Evidence: 'N of M Wi-Fi names with a shared passphrase meet the 13-character rule (fails: <network: name>)'. UniFi: no Wi-Fi settings in the API CORE uses, so needs_person.
- Needs: Decision: is it acceptable for CORE to evaluate Wi-Fi passphrases in memory. This departs from CORE's stated practice of never reading a password value (the IT Glue sweep refuses to), even though the SSID read already returns them. Also confirm the Meraki API key is a full administrator; otherwise the value is masked and the check cannot run.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and endpoint confirmed: psk is returned by the SSID list for psk SSIDs and is masked for read-only admins (Cisco's wording). Fixed: the proposal's 'send nothing if masked' had no way to detect a mask; added a concrete guard (missing or single-repeated-character value, or the key's orgAccess not full). Made the owner decision explicit that this is a change of practice against CORE's own rule of not reading secrets, not only a formality. Whether CORE's key is a full admin is unknown, so the check may produce nothing until that is settled.

**A028. Is wireless access provided via WAP or WLAN vs. router based wireless?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory (Meraki, UniFi, Auvik rows: role and model)
- How: New pure rule over held devices using infra_review.network_role(): count devices whose role is 'wireless' (Meraki details.product_type 'wireless'; UniFi model matching ^(UAP/U6/U7/UWB/UBB/UAL/E7); Auvik device_type accesspoint/thinaccesspoint) and look at the gateway's model for a built-in radio, from a short list kept in the rule. assessment_answers() needs the device list passed in (same plumbing as A021). tech_stack.py already uses network_answer(devices, 'wireless', ...) for 'What wireless system is in place'.
- Rule: Polarity: met = Wi-Fi comes from dedicated access points. met = one or more dedicated APs listed; evidence gives the count and models, and adds 'the gateway model also has a built-in radio; whether it is switched on is not visible' when that applies. not_met = the client's network gear is listed by Meraki or UniFi (a complete cloud inventory), no AP among it, and the gateway is a model with a built-in radio. needs_person = no network gear listed; or gear listed with no AP and a gateway without a radio (an unmanaged consumer router is seen by no tool); or the client is listed only by Auvik with no AP (Auvik's 'router' type is not a role in _AUVIK_ROLE, so a wireless router there is not recognised). UNCONFIRMED: the built-in-radio model list (Meraki MX models ending W or CW and the Z series; UniFi UDR, UDW, UX and the base UDM) is from product knowledge, not checked against vendor documents; restrict the Meraki test to models starting MX or Z, because newer Meraki access points have models starting CW. It needs a review and a test per model family.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and source hold; the helper and the stored fields exist as described. Fixed: (1) the proposal gave 'partly' when dedicated APs exist and the gateway model has a radio. Nothing held shows that radio is in use, so that is a guess; it is now met with the caveat in the evidence. (2) not_met is limited to clients whose gear is listed by Meraki or UniFi; Auvik-only clients cannot be judged because a discovered router has no role in infra_review. (3) The 'ends in CW' model test would wrongly catch Meraki's CW-series access points unless limited to MX/Z models. Effort S assumes A021's plumbing.

**A029. Is wireless hardware sufficient to properly cover the intended usage area**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none
- How: No system holds the intended usage area or a site survey. Keep it as a typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess'), shown as due for re-confirmation after 12 months. Optional help for the person, not an answer: put the number and models of access points from the held device inventory in the evidence line. Meraki also offers GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/connectionStats (assoc, auth, dhcp, dns failures and success counts, up to 7 days; confirmed in Cisco's reference), which could be shown as a hint for Meraki clients but does not measure coverage.
- Rule: Status stays needs_person until a person types met / partly / not_met; after 12 months the typed answer is flagged for re-check (there is no expiry today: a typed answer wins for ever). The automatic evidence, if added, reads 'N access points listed (models ...); coverage is a judgement' and never sets a status. Do not turn connection-failure counts into met/not_met: failed joins have many causes other than coverage.
- Needs: Decision: should typed answers carry a 12-month re-check flag (new behaviour, applies to every person-only question).

**A030. Is all public wireless access completely segregated from the private network?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 12; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Cisco Meraki
- How: New key, for example meraki_guest_wifi_isolation, in the Meraki sweep. Guest SSIDs are picked from the SSID rows already read (GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/ssids: enabled, an open authMode, splashPage not 'None', or a name containing guest/public/visitor), using ipAssignmentMode, useVlanTagging, defaultVlanId, lanIsolationEnabled (all confirmed in Cisco's reference; useVlanTagging and defaultVlanId are present only in Bridge mode or Layer 3 roaming). One new read per guest SSID: GET /networks/{networkId}/wireless/ssids/{number}/firewall/l3FirewallRules, which returns rules[] and allowLanAccess ('true allows access and false denies access' to the local LAN; confirmed).
