- Rule: met = every guest SSID has allowLanAccess false. partly = a guest SSID allows LAN access but is bridged to its own tagged VLAN (useVlanTagging true with a defaultVlanId): separation then depends on firewall rules between VLANs, which CORE does not read, so a person confirms. not_met = a guest SSID allows LAN access with no VLAN tag. lanIsolationEnabled alone does not count: it separates wireless clients from each other, not from the private network. No guest SSID found: send nothing (needs_person; a person can mark it not applicable), because 'public' is guessed from the name and sign-in page and a guest network with a shared passphrase and an ordinary name would be missed. Evidence names the SSIDs. UniFi: the API CORE uses has no Wi-Fi or network settings, so UniFi clients stay needs_person. Not verified live: that CORE's key can read the per-SSID firewall rules, and at most 40 wireless networks per organization are read.
- Needs: Agree how a 'public' Wi-Fi name is recognised (open or sign-in page, or a name with guest/public/visitor), or adopt a convention such as the word Guest in the SSID name.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, both endpoints and the allowLanAccess meaning are confirmed in Cisco's reference, and the rule's polarity is right. Small fixes only: stated that lanIsolationEnabled must not be taken as segregation (the proposal listed the field without saying how it is used), that 'open' covers several authMode values, and that the VLAN fields exist only in bridge or layer 3 roaming mode. Dropped the 'Meraki tag' convention idea because SSIDs carry no tag field in this response.

### Assessment: Network and infrastructure / Documentation

**A031. IS the Public WAN IP, private network IP and carrier information documented?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: IT Glue flexible assets (already stored in A.C.O.R.N as text); optional cross-check against the public IP the Meraki firewall reports
- How: A.C.O.R.N table client_documents (vendor 'itglue', kind 'asset', folder = flexible asset type name, body = 'Field name: value' lines), written by CORE hud/src/aisoc-itglue-docs.ts assetRecord() through POST core-feed/{token}/client-documents and client_docs.apply_documents. Needs: (1) a new read of ClientDocument in endpoints/client_review.py (it reads none today, verified by grep) passed to a new pure rule beside assessment_answers (which today takes only checks); (2) a small CORE fix: traitText() in aisoc-itglue-docs.ts returns nothing for a Tag-kind field, because IT Glue sends a tag trait as {type, values:[{id,name}]} (confirmed in IT Glue's developer reference) and the code reads only .name / .value of an object. A carrier recorded as a tag to a vendor record therefore never reaches A.C.O.R.N. Cross-check values: Device.ip_addresses for meraki (LAN and public IP mixed in one list, so split by private range), details.public_ip for ninjaone / huntress.
- Rule: Yes is good. met = the client holds an asset of the agreed type(s) whose body has a non-empty carrier/provider line, a public IP address, and a private (RFC1918) address or subnet. partly = one or two of the three are present. not_met = IT Glue is mapped for the client, asset records were received within 14 days (max received_at; client_docs.STALE_DAYS), and no asset of the agreed type exists. needs_person = IT Glue not mapped or no records held. Cross-check, as an evidence note rather than a downgrade: say so when the Meraki firewall's public IP (product_type appliance) is not among the documented ones. Do NOT compare against NinjaOne / Huntress public_ip: laptops roam, so a mismatch there says nothing about the office line; and Meraki's device status gives one public IP, so a second line or a dynamic address would look stale when it is not. Carrier cannot be cross-checked: Meraki's appliance uplink status has interface, status, ip, gateway, publicIp, DNS and ipAssignedBy only (confirmed in Cisco's doc). UNCONFIRMED: the field names of IT Glue's stock Internet/WAN and LAN templates (shown only as an image; IT Glue's blog says the template uses tag fields and links the vendor) and which types this account uses. A code comment in client_docs.py names a scripted 'Network Details (auto)' asset type in this account; its fields were not seen. Build the rule on names read from one real stored record. Risk for not_met: the asset list is read once per type for all organizations and capped at 20,000 records; a cut-short read is not flagged per client.
- Needs: Name the IT Glue flexible asset type(s) and field names used for carrier, public WAN IP and LAN subnet, and agree they are filled per client site. Say whether the carrier is a plain text field or a tag to a vendor record (a tag needs the CORE fix first). Say whether a script-written '(auto)' asset counts as documented.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds, but 'No CORE change' is wrong when the carrier is a tag field: CORE's traitText() drops IT Glue tag traits (shape {type, values:[...]} confirmed in IT Glue's API reference; the CORE test uses a bare array instead). Effort raised from S to M: new database read in the review endpoint, free-text parsing, IP classification, and possibly the CORE fix. Cross-check narrowed to the Meraki firewall and made an evidence note, because NinjaOne / Huntress public IPs come from roaming machines and Meraki's ip_addresses mixes LAN and public. Added the 'Network Details (auto)' hint found in client_docs.py and the asset-list cap risk. Everything else was verified by reading: the asset record shape, the ingest route, STALE_DAYS = 14, the sweep is wired in direct-sweeps.ts, public_ip fields exist for NinjaOne and Huntress.

**A032. Is the Make, Model & Serial Number of the network elements documented?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 14; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: IT Glue configuration records (already stored as text) matched to the network devices Meraki / Auvik / UniFi list
- How: A.C.O.R.N devices (Device.manufacturer, Device.model, Device.serial_number; sources in infra_review.NETWORK_TOOLS = meraki, unifi, auvik; role from infra_review.network_role) joined to client_documents rows vendor 'itglue', kind 'configuration', whose body carries 'Serial number:', 'Manufacturer:', 'Model:', 'MAC address:' lines (CORE aisoc-itglue-docs.ts configurationRecord, from IT Glue attributes serial-number, manufacturer-name, model-name, mac-address, all confirmed in IT Glue's API reference; an empty value writes no line). New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N plus a new read of ClientDocument in endpoints/client_review.py; no CORE change. Reuse device_identity.mac_key and device_merge.macs_of for the MAC side.
- Rule: Yes is good. For every device a network tool gives a role (firewall, switch, wireless), look for an IT Glue configuration record with the same serial number after normalising case, spaces and dashes, or the same MAC address. Treat a device serial that is twelve hex digits as a MAC, not a serial: Auvik keeps the MAC as the serial of a device with none (device_merge.py). UniFi devices arrive with serial null, so they match by MAC only. A record counts when it has all three lines Manufacturer, Model and Serial number. met = every such device has a complete record. partly = some do, or records match but lack one of the three lines. not_met = IT Glue is mapped, configuration records were received within 14 days, and none matches the network gear. needs_person = no network tool lists gear for the client, or IT Glue is not mapped. Evidence: 'N of M network devices have an IT Glue configuration with make, model and serial; missing: <first few names>'. Looser option if the owner accepts the tool inventory as documentation: met when every network device has manufacturer, model and serial in A.C.O.R.N (always true for Meraki, since CORE drops a Meraki device without a serial; not true for UniFi unless merged with an Auvik record that has one). Limits: network_role covers firewalls, switches and access points only, so routers, modems and cellular gateways are outside the count; gear no tool lists is invisible and still needs a person. NOT VERIFIED: that serial and MAC formats agree between the tools and IT Glue on real records; the IT Glue sweep header says its shapes were not verified against a live account.
- Needs: One decision: does 'documented' mean recorded in IT Glue (strict rule), or does the inventory Meraki / Auvik keep and A.C.O.R.N shows count on its own?
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and route hold; both sides of the join were verified in source and the IT Glue attribute names in its API reference. Fixed details: an Auvik 'serial' can be a MAC address (device_merge.py says so), so the rule must treat a twelve-hex-digit serial as a MAC; the existing helpers mac_key and macs_of do the normalising. Noted that network_role leaves out routers and cellular gateways, so 'every network element' is narrower than the question. Effort raised from S to M: this is the first review rule to read client_documents (new query under the client's row-level scope, new input to the assessment answers, a normalised join, tests). If A031 is built first and brings that read, this one drops to S.

**A033. Are the network appliances clearly labeled with the Device Name and/or IP on the front panel?**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess', key = the question text) through PUT /api/v1/client-review/{client}/answers. The row carries updated_at (models/client_review_answer.py) and review_overrides.merge_assess already copies it onto the answer. Proposed addition: treat a typed status older than 12 months as due for re-confirmation.
- Rule: Yes is good, but no system holds it: a label on a front panel is seen only on site. The tools know the name and IP that should be on the label (Meraki device name and lanIp, Auvik deviceName and ipAddresses), not whether a label is there. Nothing is suggested automatically; the status stays needs_person until a person types met / partly / not_met after a site visit, with a note saying who looked and when. To keep it current: when the typed status is more than 12 months old, show it as 'confirm again' (or fall back to needs_person). Two details for that ageing rule: updated_at is rewritten on every save, including a note-only edit, so editing the note restarts the clock; and a note-only row has updated_at with no status, so age only rows that hold a status. The last manual status row in client_review_history gives a truer 'last confirmed' date. Verified that review_overrides has no ageing today: a typed status wins indefinitely. Optional convention, not needed: a Meraki device tag such as 'labeled' would already reach A.C.O.R.N (details.tags), but it covers Meraki gear only and is still a person's record.
- Needs: Decide whether typed answers to physical-fact questions age out after 12 months, and whether an aged answer reverts to needs_person or is only flagged.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, source and effort hold; no check key or stored field speaks to a physical label. One detail added to the ageing proposal: the PUT handler sets updated_at on every save, including a note-only edit, so the 12-month clock must be tied to the status (or to the last manual status row in the history table), not to updated_at alone. Also noted the Meraki tag option as a possible but unnecessary convention.

### Assessment: Servers / Virtualization

**A034. Is VMware ESXI, Microsoft Hyper-V, Citrix Xenserver, or Red Hat Virtualization in use**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: Device inventory A.C.O.R.N already holds: NinjaOne node class, Auvik device type, and the make/model each machine reports (NinjaOne, Liongard)
- How: infra_review.hypervisor_of(device) over Device.manufacturer + Device.model, plus DeviceSource.details.node_class (ninjaone: VMWARE_VM_HOST, HYPERV_VMM_HOST, NMS_VM_HOST) and details.device_type == 'hypervisor' (auvik). assessment_answers() takes only checks today, so the endpoint line `assess_worked = assessment_answers(...)` in endpoints/client_review.py must also pass `shown` (in scope there; tech_stack already gets it).
- Rule: Descriptive question; a yes is neither good nor bad. met when (a) a NinjaOne device has node class VMWARE_VM_HOST / HYPERV_VMM_HOST / NMS_VM_HOST or an Auvik device is type 'hypervisor' (a real on-site host), or (b) a machine with a server operating system reports VMware or Xen hardware. Microsoft 'Virtual Machine' hardware seen only from guests is Hyper-V OR Azure (the helper's own docstring says so; a cloud PC reports the same), so that case stays needs_person with the evidence 'N machines report Microsoft virtual hardware: Hyper-V on site or Azure, confirm'. KVM/QEMU and VirtualBox are not among the four named products: evidence only, no status. Count server-OS machines only, so a VMware Workstation/Fusion desktop VM does not answer yes. Nothing seen = needs_person, never not_met. Red Hat Virtualization is only caught if the model text says KVM/QEMU. UNCONFIRMED live: no stored NinjaOne or Auvik row was read; the NinjaOne enum values were read in a community copy of its API schema, the Auvik value in a third-party module's list and A.C.O.R.N's own code.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds: the fields exist and are filled by the devices sweep (ninjaDevices sends node_class for every NinjaOne device, auvikDevices sends device_type). Three fixes. 1) The proposed 'met when any machine reports a hypervisor' gives a false yes for Azure VMs and cloud PCs (same Microsoft 'Virtual Machine' text as Hyper-V) and for desktop VMs (VMware Workstation, VirtualBox); the rule now needs a host node or a server-OS guest, and leaves guest-only Microsoft hardware to a person. 2) The NinjaOne node classes are confirmed in the community schema copy (VMWARE_VM_HOST, VMWARE_VM_GUEST, HYPERV_VMM_HOST, HYPERV_VMM_GUEST, NMS_VM_HOST all listed), so the host signal should be the first choice, not an optional extra. 3) Confidence lowered from high to medium: nothing was seen in a live row.

**A035. Do the hosts have attached storage? Are they direct attached, iSCSI, or fiber channel?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue flexible asset (already sent to A.C.O.R.N as text by the IT Glue documents sweep)
- How: Convention: one IT Glue flexible asset per client (or per host) of an agreed type with a select field such as 'Host storage' = Local only / Direct attached / iSCSI / Fibre Channel / NAS-NFS. CORE needs no change: assetRecord() sends each field as 'field name: value' with folder = the asset type name (client_documents kind 'asset'). A.C.O.R.N needs a new rule that reads that client's ClientDocument rows (kind='asset', folder=<agreed type>) and parses the line from body; the review endpoint reads no client_documents today, so that read is new and is shared with A036, A039 and A040.
- Rule: Two questions in one and no stated good answer. Until the owner fixes the polarity, give NO status: keep needs_person and put the value in the evidence ('IT Glue: host storage is iSCSI'); the page shows evidence beside a needs_person row, so no web change is needed. If the owner agrees that yes = attached storage exists: Direct attached / iSCSI / Fibre Channel = met with the kind named; 'Local only' = needs_person (never not_met automatically, and never met, because 'Met' beside this question would read as 'yes, attached'). Field empty or asset missing = needs_person. A record not refreshed for 14 days is deleted by A.C.O.R.N, so a failed IT Glue read makes the answer disappear, not go stale. Tool routes are UNCONFIRMED: Liongard lists a Hyper-V and a VMware ESXi inspector in its doc index but no datastore-type field was read; an Auvik device of type 'storage' would only show that a NAS/SAN exists, not that hosts use it.
- Needs: Agree the IT Glue flexible asset type name, the exact field name and its choices, and who fills it in; decide whether 'attached storage' is the wanted answer. The account's existing asset type names were not read (needs live data).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and mechanism hold (assetRecord, ClientDocument.kind/folder/body and the 14-day forget rule were read). The rule was wrong on polarity: 'field filled = met' would print 'Met' next to 'Do the hosts have attached storage?' when the value is 'Local only', i.e. when the answer is no. Changed to evidence-only under needs_person until the owner sets the polarity (ClientReview.tsx renders evidence for needs_person rows). Also corrected: the proposer said Hyper-V was not confirmed at all in Liongard; its doc index does list a Hyper-V inspector, though no storage field was read. CORE's own header says the IT Glue shapes are not verified against a live account.

**A036. is the virtualization environment supported by local storage?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; small; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue flexible asset (same field as A035)
- How: Same convention and same A.C.O.R.N reader as A035: the 'Host storage' field on the agreed IT Glue flexible asset type, arriving as client_documents kind 'asset' via assetRecord(). One field answers both questions; this item is only a second rule over the same parsed value.
- Rule: The template does not say which answer it wants, and this question is the mirror of A035 (local only = yes here, no there). Until the owner decides, give NO status: needs_person with the value in the evidence ('IT Glue: hosts run on local storage only' / 'IT Glue: hosts use iSCSI shared storage'). Do not show met merely because the field is filled: 'Met' beside this question would be read as 'yes, local storage' even when the value is iSCSI. Once the owner decides, map the two values to met / not_met accordingly. Field empty = needs_person. No tool CORE reads holds host datastore or disk-controller data (no such field in any device source).
- Needs: Decision: does 'runs on local storage only' count as met or not met here. Plus the A035 convention (asset type and field name).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds; S is only true on top of A035's reader (alone it is M). The rule 'show met when the field is filled' is removed: it asserts a status the data does not support and reads the wrong way for a shared-storage value. Replaced by evidence-only under needs_person until the owner sets the polarity.

**A037. Do all hosts have redundant networking**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none (Auvik cabling already stored per device can pre-fill a hint)
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess'), re-confirmed after 12 months (same small expiry rule as A044). Optional hint, no status: for Auvik devices with details.device_type == 'hypervisor', list details.connected_to (each entry is {id, name, port, remote_port, speed}, built by auvikLinksByDevice in CORE) and write it into the evidence of the needs_person row. Needs the device list passed into assessment_answers, as for A034.
- Rule: A person answers: met = every host has teamed or failover uplinks, ideally to two switches; partly = some hosts; not_met = single uplink. The Auvik hint must never set met or not_met: two seen cables can be LAN + storage, or LAN + a management port, or two ports on one switch, and say nothing about teaming or failover; one seen cable does not prove a single uplink, because CORE only links interfaces it read (40 pages of 1,000 interfaces per group of 20 clients, both ends must be read and the neighbour must have a name). Hint text example: 'Auvik sees host HV01 cabled to SW1 port 12 and SW2 port 12; teaming is not read'. The owner may choose to let 'every host linked to two different switches' show as partly. UNCONFIRMED: 'hypervisor' as a live Auvik value (third-party list and A.C.O.R.N code only), whether Hyper-V hosts are typed hypervisor or server, and whether connected_to is filled in stored rows (CORE's header says only tenants and device/info were checked live). Only some clients have Auvik.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The stored data exists (connected_to and device_type are sent by auvikDevices and read by client_environment.py; Auvik's spec does define interfaceRelationships_connectedTo), but it cannot honestly produce the proposed statuses. 'Two or more links on different ports = met' is refuted: link count is not redundancy (storage NIC, management port, same switch). 'Exactly one link = not_met' is refuted: CORE's interface read is capped and drops links whose far end was not read, so a missing link is normal. With neither met nor not_met defensible, the automatic route is a hint, not an answer, so the category becomes person_only with the Auvik cabling shown as evidence. The proposal also left out that connected_to carries the neighbour's id, which is what would let a rule tell two switches from one.

### Assessment: Servers / Operating Systems

**A038. Are all Windows Server Operating Systems up to the latest service pack for their respective OS?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 5; small; confidence medium).
- Source: NinjaOne
- How: New key (for example ninjaone_server_patching; no such key exists, grep of hud/src) built in aisoc-ninja-checks.ts by running the existing patchingCheck rule over machines with kind === 'server' only. No new API call: GET /v2/queries/os-patches and GET /v2/devices are already read, and NinjaMachine.kind is already set from nodeClass WINDOWS_SERVER. It goes out in the same NinjaOne report, so the row replacement is safe. Then one CHECK_ANSWERS line mapping this question to the new key.
- Rule: met = no Windows server has a critical or important OS update (not rejected) pending more than 30 days; partly / not_met by the same bands as today (under 10% of servers partly, otherwise not_met; with few servers one late server is normally not_met). No Windows server heard from in 30 days = check not sent, question stays needs_person (a person can mark not applicable). 'Latest service pack' is read as 'no overdue critical or important update', with a 30-day grace. Limit: a server whose patch scanning is switched off in NinjaOne lists nothing pending and would count as current; not checked whether NinjaOne exposes that.
- Reviewer (downgraded): ninjaone_patching exists and is already passed to assessment_answers, but it does not answer this question as worded: patchingCheck counts servers and workstations together (machines.length is the whole Windows fleet), so a client with every server current and a few late laptops would show 'Partly' or 'Not met' beside a servers-only question, and a client with no server at all would show 'Met'. The proposer named this limit and still chose the mapping. Only the met case is exact, and CHECK_ANSWERS cannot map one status only without special-case code. The honest cheapest route is the server-only variant the proposer described as optional: a small CORE check with no new API call. The existing sister mapping ('Are all servers running currently supported versions' to ninjaone_unsupported_os) has the same flaw.

### Assessment: Servers / Physical Hardware

**A039. Do we have working admin credentials to manage their servers?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 16; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue password entries (name, user name, category and entry date only; already sent by the IT Glue documents sweep, never the secret)
- How: Convention: server / domain admin credentials are filed in IT Glue under one agreed password category. CORE needs no change: passwordRecord() sends each entry as client_documents kind 'password' with folder = password-category-name and updated_at = the entry's updated-at. A.C.O.R.N needs a new rule over the client's ClientDocument rows (kind='password', folder=<agreed category>); the review endpoint reads no client_documents today (reader shared with A035/A040).
- Rule: Two questions in one. 'Do we hold one' a tool can see; 'does it work' no system holds. Default: needs_person with evidence 'IT Glue holds N entries in <category>, newest changed <date>; not tested', and a person confirms met. Only if the owner decides that a documented entry is enough may 'at least one entry exists' show as met. No entry = needs_person, never not_met (missing documentation is not proof of no access). The entry's age must not drive the status: an old password can work, a new one can be wrong, and updated-at moves when anyone edits the entry's name or notes. Supporting signals that do not answer the question and must not be mapped alone: liongard_inspections (credentialed inspections running) and a live NinjaOne agent on each server (management access without a stored credential).
- Needs: Which IT Glue password category (or categories) means 'server / domain admin credential', and whether a documented entry alone may count as met. The account's category names were not read (needs live data).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and data path hold (passwordRecord sends folder = category and updated_at; ClientDocument stores kind, folder, vendor_updated_at; password entries are kept by A.C.O.R.N). The rule is refuted: 'met when an entry exists and was changed within 12 months' claims the credential works, which nothing tests, and uses the entry's edit date as if it were a test date. Changed to evidence-only under needs_person unless the owner accepts 'documented' as met; the 12-month partly band is dropped. Confidence lowered from medium to low: IT Glue's password attributes could not be read in its reference this session (page cut off) and CORE's header says these shapes are not verified live.

**A040. Do all servers have licensed and configured remote console capabilities? (iDRAC, iLO, etc...)**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 16; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue configurations or password entries (already sent as text), compared with the client's physical servers and hosts in the device inventory; Auvik devices of type 'ipmi' as a supporting sighting
- How: Convention: each server's remote console is documented in IT Glue as a configuration of one agreed type (client_documents kind 'configuration', folder = configuration-type-name, via configurationRecord) or a password entry in one agreed category (kind 'password', folder = category), with the licence level written in an agreed place (name or notes). New A.C.O.R.N rule counts those records against the client's physical machines: server-OS devices where infra_review.hypervisor_of() is None PLUS hypervisor hosts (NinjaOne node class VMWARE_VM_HOST / HYPERV_VMM_HOST, Auvik device_type 'hypervisor'). No CORE change.
- Rule: met only when every physical server and host has a record AND each record states the licence level; partly when some have one or the licence is not stated; needs_person when there is no record, or no physical machine is known. Never not_met automatically. An all-virtual client with no host listed must stay needs_person: an ESXi host is the physical server that needs the console and has no agent, so counting only server-OS devices would undercount and give a false met. Auvik devices of type 'ipmi' can be named in the evidence ('Auvik sees 2 management cards') but show neither licence nor which server they belong to. UNCONFIRMED: 'ipmi' as an Auvik device type (third-party module list only; Auvik's own schema section could not be read). Verified: nothing in hud/src mentions iDRAC, iLO or IPMI, and Liongard's doc index lists no such inspector.
- Needs: Agree where remote consoles are documented in IT Glue (configuration type name or password category name) and how the licence level (iDRAC Enterprise, iLO Advanced) is written.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds. Two fixes to the rule. 1) 'met when there are at least as many records as physical servers' ignored the word 'licensed' in the question; met now needs the licence stated, otherwise partly. 2) The physical-server count was wrong: server_brand_answer's split only sees machines with a server operating system, so ESXi hosts (no agent, no 'server' in os) are missed and an all-virtual client could be marked met with zero consoles; hosts from NinjaOne node class and Auvik type are now counted and the all-virtual case stays with a person. Added the Auvik 'ipmi' device type as a possible sighting, marked unconfirmed.

**A041. Is VMware vCenter or Microsoft Systems Center installed for management?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 22; medium; confidence low).
- Source: NinjaOne installed-software list for System Center; Liongard's list of inspected systems for vCenter
- How: GET /v2/queries/software on NinjaOne, already paged by ninjaSoftware() in aisoc-remote-tools.ts (only remote-tool matches are sent today, to the /remote-tools route, not as checks). New match for System Center server products (Virtual Machine Manager, Operations Manager, Configuration Manager site server; NOT the Configuration Manager client, which sits on every managed PC). For vCenter: an active Liongard system whose Inspector.Name is the VMware vCenter inspector (GET /api/v1/systems, already read by aisoc-liongard-health.ts). The result must NOT be posted on its own under source 'ninjaone': A.C.O.R.N keeps one row per client per source and replaces the whole list on each report, so a separate post would wipe the other NinjaOne checks. Either feed the match into the NinjaOne tool sweep's own report, or send it under a new source added to infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES.
- Rule: Descriptive; met when a match is found (evidence names the product and the server). Not found = send nothing, question stays needs_person; never not_met automatically (a single-host site without central management is not a fault). A Windows software list will not show a current vCenter: as far as I know (not re-read in VMware documentation) vCenter has been appliance-only since vSphere 7, so only System Center and legacy Windows vCenter can be found this way; vSphere Client, PowerCLI and VMware Tools must not match. UNCONFIRMED: the program display names; NinjaOne's software row field names (CORE's header says not checked against a live tenant); the exact Liongard Inspector.Name string. Liongard's doc index lists the vCenter inspector under its beta heading.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds: CORE does read /v2/queries/software and Inspector.Name. The mechanism had a data-loss trap: the software read runs in its own daily sweep, and a check posted from there 'under source ninjaone' would replace the client's whole NinjaOne check row (upsert on (tenant_id, source) with the list replaced). Corrected to merge into the NinjaOne report or use a new source registered in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES. Also narrowed the match (exclude the Configuration Manager client and VMware client tools) and made vCenter rely on the Liongard signal, since the Windows software route cannot see an appliance.

**A042. Are any servers end-of-life or unsupported by the manufacturer?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 16; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue configuration record (end-of-life date field), read by CORE
- How: Convention: each physical server's end-of-life date is kept on its IT Glue configuration. IT Glue's developer reference shows mitp-end-of-life-date, mitp-device-expiration-date, warranty-expires-at, purchased-at and installed-at on configurations (read this session). CORE reads configurations only in the documents sweep (/organizations/{id}/relationships/configurations) and configurationRecord() keeps none of those fields. Two ways in: (a) add 'End of life: <date>' and 'Warranty expires: <date>' lines to configurationRecord's body and let A.C.O.R.N derive from client_documents (smallest CORE change; needs the client_documents reader from A035); or (b) a new itglue check, which needs a NEW configurations read in the checks sweep, because aisoc-itglue-checks.ts reads only /domains and /ssl_certificates and the itglue row is replaced whole on each report.
- Rule: Polarity: a yes is bad. not_met = any physical server's end-of-life date is in the past; partly = none past but one within 12 months; met ONLY when every physical server configuration carries a date and all are in the future; some servers without a date and none past = no status (needs_person, evidence '2 of 5 servers have no end-of-life date in IT Glue'). Virtual servers must be left out (a VM has no hardware end of life): exclude configurations whose manufacturer/model is virtual, or match them to devices and use hypervisor_of(). Hardware only: the operating-system side is the question already mapped to ninjaone_unsupported_os and must not be reused here. UNCONFIRMED: whether anything fills the mitp-* fields in this account (they may be empty everywhere), and the configuration type name used for servers. Auvik's /v1/inventory/device/lifecycle exists in its spec but coverage of servers is not confirmed.
- Needs: Confirm the team will record a server's end-of-life date on its IT Glue configuration (and in which field), and which configuration type name(s) mean 'server'. One live configuration row would show whether these fields are filled today.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and the IT Glue attribute names hold (confirmed in IT Glue's reference this session; configurationRecord drops them, as read). Two fixes. 1) 'A small new check beside the domain / certificate checks' understated the work: that sweep does not read configurations at all, so it needs a new list read, or the cheaper route of adding two lines to the document record. 2) The rule let a client be met when only some servers carry a date ('every documented server' was silently 'every server with a date'), and did not exclude virtual servers; met now needs a date on every physical server.

**A043. Are all servers under hardware warranty?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 17; medium; confidence low).
- Source: NinjaOne warranty tracking (fallback: IT Glue configuration warranty-expires-at)
- How: NinjaOne: warranty start/end dates per device from its warranty tracking (per NinjaOne's documentation: workstations, servers and virtual hosts; HP Enterprise not supported). Expected path references.warrantyDate.endDate on GET /v2/devices-detailed rows (already fetched by the devices sweep): UNCONFIRMED, a public catalogue shows a 'WarrantyDates' schema with 3 properties and 'NodeReferences' with 8 but not their names; the path is from memory. CORE must add an explicit field (for example details.warranty_end in ninjaDevices()); flattenFacts() does NOT carry it today. Then an A.C.O.R.N rule over devices (using hypervisor_of() to leave out virtual servers), with the device list passed into assessment_answers as for A034. Fallback: warranty-expires-at on IT Glue configurations (confirmed in IT Glue's reference; dropped by configurationRecord), same route as A042.
- Rule: not_met = any physical server's warranty end date is in the past; partly = none past but one ends within 90 days; met ONLY when every physical server has an end date in the future; some physical servers with no date and none expired = no status (needs_person, evidence 'warranty date known for 2 of 4 servers'). Virtual servers are left out; no physical server or no dates at all = needs_person. Servers of a maker NinjaOne does not track (HPE) need the IT Glue date or a person. UNCONFIRMED: the NinjaOne field path, and whether warranty tracking is switched on in this NinjaOne account.
- Needs: Confirm NinjaOne warranty tracking is enabled (a NinjaOne setting), or that warranty dates are kept on IT Glue configurations. One live devices-detailed row would settle the field path.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds, but one claim is refuted: 'if devices-detailed returns it, flattenFacts() is already forwarding it inside details.facts and this becomes a rule over held data'. flattenFacts stops at two levels (children of a top-level object are copied only if they are plain values), so a nested references.warrantyDate.endDate is dropped, and it also stops at 100 keys. A CORE change is needed either way, so this cannot become derive_from_held_data. Also: the NinjaOne check sweep reads /v2/devices (not the detailed list), so the natural place is the devices feed (ninjaDevices), with the rule in A.C.O.R.N where hypervisor_of() already exists. Rule tightened: 'partly when some servers have no date' mixed unknown with at-risk; unknown now gives no status.

### Assessment: Servers / Physical security: server room access

**A044. Is there a dedicated room for all servers.**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess', keyed by the question text), as today. Lightest way to keep it current: treat a typed answer older than 12 months as due for re-confirmation, using the updated_at the row already has. review_overrides.py has no expiry logic today, so that is a small new rule plus a marker on the page. A client with no on-site physical server known to A.C.O.R.N can be shown a hint to type 'not applicable', never set automatically.
- Rule: A physical fact no connected system holds. A person answers: met = a dedicated, separate room; partly = a shared closet or comms cupboard; not_met = servers in open office space; not_applicable = no on-site servers. After 12 months the page asks for it to be confirmed again; the old answer stays visible until then.

### Assessment: Workstations / Physical Hardware

**A045. Are all workstations and laptops business class hardware with current warranties?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 17; medium; confidence low).
- Source: NinjaOne (warranty dates) plus the A.C.O.R.N device inventory (maker and model) for the business-class half
- How: NEW data, nothing exists today: grep for 'warranty' in hud/src hits only aisoc-slide-checks.ts, and no key ninjaone_workstation_warranty exists in either repo. Source field: NinjaOne device rows, references.warranty {startDate, endDate, manufacturerFulfillmentDate} (epoch seconds). A public copy of NinjaOne's OpenAPI shows it on GET /v2/devices (NodeWithDetailedReferences) and GET /v2/devices-detailed (Device), both read-only and both already called by CORE (aisoc-ninja-checks.ts pages /v2/devices; aisoc-devices.ts reads /v2/devices-detailed). Confirmed by reading: flattenFacts() stops at two levels, so references.warranty.* is dropped today. Two ways to carry it: (a) a new check ninjaone_workstation_warranty in the ninjaone sweep, or (b) cheaper for A045 and A047 together: add warranty_end, warranty_start, shipped_at to the ninjaone device details in ninjaDevices(), then one pure rule in A.C.O.R.N judges warranty and model together. Either way A.C.O.R.N needs new code, not only a CHECK_ANSWERS line: assessment_answers() gets checks only (endpoints/client_review.py line 574), so the devices already loaded there (`shown`, line 464) must be passed in for the model half. Business-class half: devices.manufacturer + devices.model (models/device.py, filled from NinjaOne system.manufacturer / system.model). Weaker second source: IT Glue configuration attribute warranty-expires-at (confirmed in IT Glue's developer reference; configurationRecord() does not keep it), but that sweep sends text documents, not checks, and its shapes are marked not verified live.
- Rule: UNCONFIRMED live: that the list calls actually return references.warranty (seen only in a third-party copy of the spec; NinjaOne's own warranty pages do not describe the API, and CORE's live-read note for /v2/devices does not list it). The per-device fallback GET /v2/device/{id} is not realistic for a daily sweep (thousands of machines). Workstations = nodeClass WINDOWS_WORKSTATION or MAC, last contact within 30 days (same window as ACTIVE_WITHIN_DAYS). Warranty half: met only when EVERY active workstation has a reading and every endDate is in the future (the question says 'all'; the proposal's 'met with up to half unread' was too generous). Partly when under 10% have lapsed, any ends within 60 days, or some machines have no reading. Not_met when 10% or more have lapsed. Not sent when more than half have no reading. Business-class half (A.C.O.R.N): Latitude/OptiPlex/Precision, EliteBook/ProBook/EliteDesk/ProDesk/Z, ThinkPad/ThinkCentre/ThinkStation, Surface, Mac = business; Inspiron/Pavilion/Envy/IdeaPad/Yoga/Legion = consumer; unrecognised models listed, not guessed (Lenovo often reports a type code such as 20XW... as the model; Vostro and XPS need a decision). Combined answer = the worse half. If only the model half is available: consumer models found gives partly or not_met; all business-class gives NO suggested status (needs_person with the model finding in the evidence), never met or partly on models alone. Macs: NinjaOne's automatic sync covers Dell, HP (non-Enterprise), Lenovo, Microsoft and Toshiba only (confirmed on NinjaOne's page), so Macs have a date only if typed into NinjaOne; Addigy-only Macs have none.
- Needs: Confirm the NinjaOne Warranty Tracking app is enabled (Administration > Apps > NinjaOne Warranty Tracking). Approve one live read of a /v2/devices row to confirm references.warranty arrives on list calls. Agree the business-class model list (Vostro, XPS, Surface Go, Macs).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds (warranty is not collected anywhere; confirmed by grep and by reading flattenFacts). Changes: (1) confidence medium to low, because the only evidence that list calls return references.warranty is a third-party spec copy, and the per-device fallback is too costly to count on. (2) 'Met' tightened: the question says 'all', so machines with no warranty reading cap the answer at partly. (3) Model half alone must not suggest partly when every model is business class; it gives no status. (4) Stated plainly that this is not a mapping-only change: assessment_answers() receives checks only, so the endpoint must pass devices in. (5) Added the cheaper carrying route (three fields on the device details from the devices-detailed read CORE already makes), which serves A047 too. (6) IT Glue second source kept but marked weaker: the attribute exists in the public reference, yet that sweep sends text, not checks. Confirmed as proposed: NinjaOne coverage list, the Apps location of the switch, the 30-day window, IT Glue attribute names.

**A046. Do the workstations and laptops meet the all of the following minimum specifications? Intel i5 or higher processor 8 GB RAM or greater. 250 GB HDD or greater.**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory (NinjaOne source row details; Addigy processor only, for Macs)
- How: device_sources.details for vendor ninjaone, sent by ninjaSpecs() in aisoc-devices.ts from GET /v2/devices-detailed: details.processor (name of the first CPU), details.memory_gb, details.disks[] {name, size_gb, free_gb} (up to 8 VOLUMES, no removable/fixed marker), details.node_class, details.last_contact. Stored as sent (details is a free JSONB dict at ingest, models/device.py). No rule in services/api/app reads memory_gb, size_gb or processor today (grep: no hits; only the web device page shows them). New pure rule in assessment_review.py; the endpoint must pass the devices it already loads (`shown`, endpoints/client_review.py line 464) into assessment_answers(), which today receives checks only (line 574). No CORE change needed for a first version.
- Rule: Not verified live: that NinjaOne fills memory, processors and volumes on devices-detailed rows (the public spec copy does not list them on that schema; CORE's code and unit test expect them). Workstations = node_class WINDOWS_WORKSTATION or MAC, not hidden, AND last contact within 30 days (the proposal had no recency filter, so retired machines still listed in NinjaOne would be judged). A machine passes when all three hold. (1) Processor: Intel Core i5/i7/i9, Core Ultra 5/7/9, the newer 'Core 5/7' names, or Xeon; i3, Celeron, Pentium, Atom, N-series fail. (2) memory_gb >= 7.5. (3) Disk: the C: volume is >= 220 (sizes are GiB of a volume, so a 250/256 GB drive shows about 230-238; note a 240 GB SSD also passes at this cut). Do NOT simply sum all volumes: a USB or second drive would inflate it. If C: is short but all volumes together reach 220 (split drive), list the machine as 'not sure', neither pass nor fail. For Macs use the largest volume. Met = every active workstation has all three readings and passes. Partly = under 10% fail, or some lack a reading. Not_met = 10% or more fail. Evidence names the count and the commonest shortfall. No workstation with readings = needs_person. As worded ('Intel i5'), AMD Ryzen 5/7/9, Apple M-series and Snapdragon X fail unless the owner says they count. Addigy sends processor type and speed only (ADDIGY_FACTS has no RAM or disk size), so a Mac that is only in Addigy can be judged on processor alone and counts as 'not fully read'.
- Needs: Decide whether AMD Ryzen 5 and up, Apple silicon and Snapdragon X count as 'i5 or higher'.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and effort hold: the fields are sent by ninjaSpecs(), stored untouched, and read by no rule. Changes: (1) Disk rule fixed. details.disks are volumes with no fixed/removable marker, so summing them lets a small drive pass with a USB or data drive attached; judge C: (largest volume on a Mac) and treat 'short C: but enough in total' as not sure. A cleaner later fix is for CORE to keep only fixed disks. (2) Added a 30-day last-contact filter, matching the NinjaOne checks. (3) Machines with missing readings cap the answer at partly instead of allowing met. (4) Processor list widened for Intel's newer 'Core 5/7' names and Snapdragon X added to the owner's decision. (5) Confidence high to medium: nothing was read live, and the public spec copy does not show memory, processors or volumes on the detailed device schema, so population rests on CORE's code and unit test only. Confirmed as proposed: Addigy has no RAM or disk size; the endpoint wiring gap.

**A047. Are any workstations end-of-life or unsupported by the manufacturer?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 17; medium; confidence low).
- Source: NinjaOne (warranty end and manufacturer ship date), used as a stand-in for 'end-of-life'; no tool holds a true manufacturer end-of-life fact for PCs
- How: NEW, nothing exists today: key ninjaone_workstation_end_of_life is in neither repo. Same read as A045: references.warranty.endDate and references.warranty.manufacturerFulfillmentDate on NinjaOne device rows (GET /v2/devices, GET /v2/devices-detailed; read-only; both already called by CORE; shown in a public copy of NinjaOne's OpenAPI). NinjaOne's own page confirms the ship date exists and calls it informational only. Best built together with A045: one collection of warranty_end / warranty_start / shipped_at, two rules. If sent as a check from the ninjaone sweep, A.C.O.R.N needs only a CHECK_ANSWERS line (source ninjaone is already in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES). Existing keys do NOT answer this as worded: ninjaone_unsupported_os and addigy_unsupported_macos judge the operating system, the first mixes servers with workstations and is already mapped to the neighbouring OS question; this question sits under 'Physical Hardware'. IT Glue configuration attributes mitp-end-of-life-date and warranty-expires-at exist in IT Glue's public reference but are not kept by configurationRecord() and that sweep sends text only.
- Rule: UNCONFIRMED live: that list calls return references.warranty (third-party spec copy only). Also a PROXY, not the fact asked: out of warranty and old is the team's working definition of end-of-life, not the manufacturer's; it only holds if the owner accepts it. Otherwise this is person_only (typed answer that expires after 12 months). Polarity: 'yes' is the bad answer, so met = NO workstation is end-of-life. Workstations = nodeClass WINDOWS_WORKSTATION or MAC, last contact within 30 days. A machine is end-of-life when its warranty endDate has passed AND it shipped more than N years ago (manufacturerFulfillmentDate, else warranty startDate; N suggested 5). Met = every active workstation has a reading and none is end-of-life. Partly = under 25% are end-of-life (same band as ninjaone_unsupported_os; strictly even one makes the literal answer 'yes', so partly is the softest honest status), or some machines have no reading. Not_met = 25% or more. Machines that are merely out of warranty but younger than N are NOT counted here; that belongs to A045. Evidence written in the good direction with machine names in items, e.g. '3 of 40 workstations are out of manufacturer warranty and over 5 years old'. Not sent when most machines have no reading. Macs: NinjaOne's automatic sync does not cover Apple or HP Enterprise, so Macs are 'not known' unless a date is typed in NinjaOne. The model year in an Addigy model name could date Macs, but that is unverified.
- Needs: Decide whether 'out of manufacturer warranty and older than N years' is what the team means by end-of-life or unsupported, and set N (5 suggested). Confirm NinjaOne Warranty Tracking is enabled. Approve one live read of a device row to confirm the warranty fields arrive on list calls.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category kept, but only conditionally: no connected tool holds a manufacturer end-of-life fact for PCs, so the route is a proxy built on warranty end plus ship date. If the owner does not accept that definition, the honest category is person_only. Changes: (1) confidence medium to low (field unconfirmed on list calls, proxy definition, Macs uncovered). (2) Removed 'some only out of warranty but younger than N' from the partly band: it contradicted the rule's own definition and would mark a fleet with zero end-of-life machines as partly; that finding belongs to A045. (3) Met now requires a reading on every active workstation; unread machines cap at partly. (4) Noted that A045 and A047 should share one collection. Confirmed as proposed: the existing OS keys do not answer this hardware question; polarity handling (met = none); IT Glue attribute names exist in the public reference; ship date is documented by NinjaOne.

### Assessment: Cybersecurity and risk / Credentials

**A048. Is the minimum number of remembered passwords set to 3 or more?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 18; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Liongard (Active Directory inspector)
- How: No check key exists today (grep of hud/src and services/api for liongard_ad_password*: zero hits). Route: a Liongard metric over the Active Directory inspector's Default Domain Password Policy, data field AccountPolicy.EnforcePasswordHistory, read with the calls CORE already makes in hud/src/aisoc-liongard.ts: GET /api/v1/metrics (findMetric matches on inspector + EXACT metric name) then POST /api/v2/metrics/evaluate (metricRows). Add one FACT_METRICS entry, add it to the rowsOf list in readFacts, a new EnvFacts field and a new check, proposed key liongard_ad_password_history, source "liongard" (in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES, so assessment_answers() receives it), plus one CHECK_ANSWERS line in assessment_review.py. IMPORTANT: the public page for "Active Directory: Enforce Password History" is a community SUBMISSION on Liongard's metrics-library feedback board (status "submitted"), not a built-in metric, so it almost certainly is not in the account under that name and must be created there as a custom metric (the one AD metric CORE reads today is also a custom one, suffixed "(L.O.)").
- Rule: Per client, over every Active Directory Liongard inspects: remembered-password count 3 or more on every domain = met; 3+ on some domains only = partly; below 3 on every domain (0 or not configured included) = not_met. No Active Directory row for the client (cloud-only client) = check not sent, question stays needs_person. Polarity is normal (yes is good). Limits: the value is the Default Domain Password Policy stored in AD (Liongard's support article confirms the inspector reports Password History from that policy and that it can differ from what a GPO report shows); a fine-grained password policy is not covered. UNCONFIRMED: the field name AccountPolicy.EnforcePasswordHistory comes only from the community submission page, and the value shape (number or text) is not stated anywhere I could read; the parser must accept both and send nothing when it cannot read a number.
- Needs: Create (or confirm) a custom Liongard metric on the Active Directory inspector that returns the password-history count (suggested query AccountPolicy.EnforcePasswordHistory) and give CORE its exact name; check one live value so the shape is known. Decide how cloud-only clients should read (left for a person, or not applicable).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and route hold. Fixed one material detail: the cited metrics-library page is a community submission on a feedback board (status "submitted"), not a shipped metric, so the owner step is "create a custom metric", not "confirm it exists". Also noted that findMetric needs the exact name and that the new key must be added to the rowsOf list in readFacts, not only to FACT_METRICS.

**A049. Are complexity requirements in place for privileged AD logins?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 18; medium; confidence low).
- Source: Liongard (Active Directory inspector)
- How: No check key exists today (grep: zero hits for liongard_ad_password_complexity). Route: a Liongard custom metric over the Active Directory inspector's Default Domain Password Policy, data field AccountPolicy.PasswordComplexity, read through the existing GET /api/v1/metrics + POST /api/v2/metrics/evaluate path in hud/src/aisoc-liongard.ts. I opened the page for "Active Directory: Password Complexity Enabled": it lists that data field and, like the history one, is a community submission (status "submitted"), not a built-in metric, so it must be created in the account. New check, proposed key liongard_ad_password_complexity, source "liongard"; then one CHECK_ANSWERS line. Possible later upgrade, UNCONFIRMED: Liongard's AD inspector also keeps a privileged-users list and a per-user security score that includes "is there a password complexity requirement in place"; if that per-user value is readable for privileged accounts it would answer the question exactly, but no data path for it was found in public docs.
- Rule: Complexity requirement enabled in the default domain password policy of every inspected Active Directory = met, with evidence that says plainly "the domain policy applies to every account, privileged ones included, unless a fine-grained policy overrides it; Liongard does not show fine-grained policies"; enabled on some domains only = partly; disabled = not_met. No AD row for the client = not sent, stays needs_person. Polarity normal. The question asks about PRIVILEGED logins specifically: the tool only sees the domain-wide default (Liongard's support article describes fine-grained policies as something to check by PowerShell and does not say the inspector reports them), so "met" means "not weaker than the domain default as far as Liongard shows". UNCONFIRMED: value shape (true/false, 1/0 or text) and that the field name on the community page matches the live inspector data.
- Needs: Create a custom Liongard metric on the Active Directory inspector returning the complexity setting (suggested query AccountPolicy.PasswordComplexity) and give CORE its exact name. Decide whether "domain default has complexity on" is accepted as the answer for privileged logins, or whether the question stays with a person wherever fine-grained policies may exist.
- Reviewer (corrected): Route holds. Filled in what the proposer had not opened: the metric page exists, names the field AccountPolicy.PasswordComplexity, and is a community submission (status "submitted"), so the metric has to be created in the account rather than looked up. Replaced the wrong evidence link (it pointed at the password-history page). Added the per-user security-score route as an unconfirmed way to answer the privileged-account part. Confidence stays low because the tool answers the domain default, not privileged accounts as such.

### Assessment: Cybersecurity and risk / Access Policies

**A050. Does any staff member have local administrator rights?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 18; medium; confidence low).
- Source: Liongard (Windows Workstation inspector, local group membership) for Windows; Addigy admin_users already held for Macs; NinjaOne custom field filled by a script as the fallback
- How: No existing key answers it (grep: zero hits for ninjaone_local_admins or any local-admin list on Windows; autoelevate_enforcing reads elevationMode only; ga_local_admin / local_admin_join / laps are Entra join settings in the "cipp" row, which assessment_answers() is not given). Cheaper route than the proposed script: Liongard's Windows Workstation inspector already reports local group membership (Liongard's own support article says so and names the local Administrators group as the usual alert target; a bug that reported groups as empty was fixed 2026-09-16). CORE already evaluates per-machine Windows Workstation metrics (encryption, EDR) through GET /api/v1/metrics + POST /api/v2/metrics/evaluate, so this needs one custom metric in Liongard, one FACT_METRICS entry and a new check, proposed key liongard_local_admins, source "liongard". A community idea post gives the query Users[?contains(Groups, `Administrators`)].Name (seen in search results only, not opened; UNCONFIRMED against live data). Macs: Addigy fact admin_users is already fetched (hud/src/addigy.ts ADDIGY_FACTS) and stored in DeviceSource.details.facts.admin_users (addigyFacts), read by nothing; fold it in with a second check in the Addigy sweep. Fallback if Liongard's list proves incomplete: a NinjaOne device custom field written by a scheduled script and read with GET /v2/queries/custom-fields (path confirmed only in a third-party catalogue and the community PowerShell module; CORE has no custom-field read today, grep zero hits). Then one CHECK_ANSWERS line.
- Rule: POLARITY: "yes" is the bad answer; status means the practice is as the template wants it. met = on no inspected computer does an account outside the allowed list (built-in Administrator, the provider's own admin / LAPS account, Domain Admins) sit in the local Administrators group; partly = found on under 10% of computers; not_met = 10% or more. Because the question says "any", the evidence must start with the plain answer ("Yes: N of M computers have a staff account with administrator rights") and items carry the machines and accounts. Only machines inspected recently count (same currentMachines window the EDR metric uses). No row, or an unreadable value, on every machine = check not sent, stays needs_person. Mac-only answer from Addigy is honest only for all-Mac clients. UNCONFIRMED and must be checked on one live machine before trusting "met": whether Liongard's Users table lists DOMAIN or Entra accounts and nested groups (for example Domain Users) that are members of local Administrators, or only local accounts; if it lists only local accounts, a domain-joined client would wrongly read met and the NinjaOne script route is needed instead. Also UNCONFIRMED: the text format of Addigy admin_users.
- Needs: 1) Create a custom Liongard metric on the Windows Workstation inspector listing members of the local Administrators group and give CORE its exact name; check one domain-joined machine to see whether domain accounts appear. 2) The list of account names allowed to be local admin on Windows and on Macs (the provider's own admin accounts), so staff accounts can be told apart. 3) Decision: do Domain Admins or Entra "selected users" count as staff with admin rights. 4) Only if Liongard's list is incomplete: a NinjaOne custom field plus a scheduled script, and confirmation that CORE's NinjaOne API client may read custom fields.
- Reviewer (corrected): The claim "no tool CORE reads today lists members of the local Administrators group" is wrong: Liongard's Windows Workstation inspector, which CORE already reads per machine, reports local group membership. Made that the primary route (one custom metric, no script to write or schedule) and moved the NinjaOne custom-field script to fallback. Added the open risk that the Liongard list may not include domain accounts, which would give a false met, and required the evidence to state the literal yes/no because of the inverted polarity. Confidence lowered to low until one live machine is checked.

### Assessment: Cybersecurity and risk / Security Continuous Monitoring

**A051. The network is monitored to detect potential cybersecurity events**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 2; small; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N's client-to-tool mapping (ClientAlias: Blumira, Field Effect, Huntress, DNSFilter) plus the held Huntress count "SIEM log sources" (ClientLicenseCount) and the held check dnsfilter_traffic
- How: New pure rule in assessment_review.py with its own tool list (infra_review.soc_answer cannot be reused as is: its MDR_TOOLS are huntress, fieldeffect, petra, with no Blumira, and Petra watches Microsoft 365 only). Inputs: enrolled_in / mapped_tools (built in endpoints/client_review.py at lines 478-479 and 559-569 but NOT passed to assessment_answers at line 574, so the endpoint must pass them), tool_checks["dnsfilter_traffic"], and whether Huntress SIEM has log sources. For that last fact do NOT parse the evidence sentence of huntress_extra_coverage (that key is sent as met for identity detection alone, and the only place the log-source count appears is the wording "N log sources feed its SIEM"; the review's one existing evidence-text rule, RDP, is already known not to work). Use the structured count CORE already sends: licence-count row source huntress, item "SIEM log sources" (huntressCounts in aisoc-huntress-checks.ts; known to A.C.O.R.N in license_counts.py line 119). The review endpoint does not read ClientLicenseCount today, so one small query is added. Optional stronger fact later (separate new CORE check): Meraki GET /networks/{networkId}/appliance/security/intrusion (read-only; mode detection / prevention / disabled; confirmed in Cisco's docs).
- Rule: met = the client is set up in a watched detection service that takes network or log data: Blumira, or Field Effect, or Huntress with a "SIEM log sources" count above zero. partly = only endpoint or DNS-level watching: Huntress without log sources, and/or DNSFilter with dnsfilter_traffic met. not_met = set up in none of these although other clients are (the same "mapped" test soc_answer uses, so a tool nobody uses says nothing). needs_person = none of these tools has any client mapped. Polarity normal. Evidence must carry the caveat soc_answer already uses: A.C.O.R.N sees that the client is in the tool, not which services are switched on (for example whether a Field Effect network appliance is installed or which logs Blumira receives), so met is a suggestion a person can overrule. The review applies neither tool exclusions nor staleness to tool rows today; the rule should ignore a dnsfilter_traffic check or a licence count that is months old.
- Needs: A decision on which tools count as "the network is monitored": proposed Blumira, Field Effect and Huntress SIEM with log sources = met; Huntress EDR and/or DNSFilter alone = partly; Auvik or Meraki uptime monitoring alone = does not count.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds; every cited file and line was found as described and the Meraki endpoint was confirmed. Changed how the Huntress SIEM fact is read: the proposal relied on the evidence sentence mentioning log sources, which is text matching on a key that is also met for identity detection alone. The structured count "SIEM log sources" is already sent and stored as a licence count, so the rule should read that (one extra query in the endpoint). Also made explicit that soc_answer's tool list cannot be reused because it has Petra and lacks Blumira.

**A052. The physical environment is monitored to detect potential cybersecurity events**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none (hint only: cameras in A.C.O.R.N's device inventory)
- How: No system holds whether premises are watched (alarm, door access, cameras someone reviews). The only related held data is the device inventory's kind == "camera" (device_kind() in endpoints/devices.py: Meraki product_type camera, UniFi Protect, Auvik device_type camera, NinjaOne node class containing CAMERA). Keep the status typed (client_review_answers, section assess) and add the camera count to the evidence line as a prompt, without setting a status from it.
- Rule: Status stays needs_person until a person types met / partly / not_met / not_applicable. Hint in evidence when cameras are seen: "N cameras are on the client's network (Meraki / UniFi Protect ...); confirm someone reviews them and whether there is an alarm or door access system". Cameras seen must NOT become met (presence does not show anyone watches them) and none seen must NOT become not_met (cameras, alarms and badge systems are often the landlord's or off the managed network). Keep it current by flagging a typed answer older than 12 months for re-confirmation: ClientReviewAnswer already has updated_at, but no expiry logic exists today (review_overrides.py only carries updated_at through), so that is a small addition shared by every person-only question.
- Needs: A decision that typed answers go stale after 12 months (shown as "confirm again", not wiped). Optional: an agreed IT Glue place (for example a "Physical security" flexible asset) if the team would rather record it there once; not needed for the typed route.

### Assessment: Backup and disaster recovery / Server Backup

**A053. Is data archived for a minimum of 30 days from time of backup or at least to compliance standards for your industry.**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 11; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Slide
- How: GET https://api.slide.tech/v1/agent -> local_retention_policy.retention_policy_max_age_months (enum 1,3,6,12,24,36,84; 'Maximum snapshot age in months') and retention_policy_name (lean/balanced/comprehensive). Both are in the Agent schema's required list. Plus GET /v1/device -> service_model_name / service_model_name_short (published example: '1 Year Cloud Retention') for the cloud copy. SLIDE_SWEEP.loadContext already fetches every /v1/agent and /v1/device row, so no new API call. New check (suggested key slide_retention) in hud/src/aisoc-slide-checks.ts + one CHECK_ANSWERS line in assessment_review.py. Source 'slide' is already in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES. No retention key exists today (grep of aisoc-slide*.ts for retention: zero hits).
- Rule: Polarity normal (yes is good). met: every protected system that is still being backed up has retention_policy_max_age_months >= 1; evidence names the shortest local value, the policy name and, when the appliance row carries it, the plan's cloud retention text. not_met cannot occur from this field (smallest allowed value is 1 month), so the check is effectively 'met whenever the client is on Slide'; say that in the evidence rather than present it as a measurement. A system with no value is left out, not counted as 'partly' (partly would read as 'practice half in place'); send nothing if no system has a value. Not sent when the client has no Slide system (stays needs_person). If the client ALSO holds the liongard_datto_bcdr check (it has Datto BCDR machines) the answer must be capped at partly by code, because a static scope note cannot stop 'Met' showing for a mixed client; CHECK_ANSWERS has no such mechanism today, so this needs a small special case like the RDP one. Stays with a person: 'or to compliance standards for your industry'. Datto BCDR retention: UNCONFIRMED in any Datto API (fields CORE reads from /v1/bcdr/agent have none). Optional evidence only: age of the oldest surviving snapshot via GET /v1/snapshot with backup_started_before / sort_asc (wire names read from the spec's components), but CORE's slideUrl allows only agent_id on /v1/snapshot today and a system deployed under 30 days ago would look short, so do not use it for the status. NOT VERIFIED live: the values this account returns.
- Needs: Decision: does a 1-month Slide retention count as '30 days' (it is the product minimum, so every Slide client would show Met)? Accept that Datto BCDR clients keep a typed answer, and that mixed Slide + Datto clients show Partly.
- Reviewer (corrected): Field and enum confirmed in Slide's published OpenAPI; category holds. Fixed: (1) the proposal said no cloud retention is visible; the Device schema's service_model_name(_short) carries the plan's cloud retention as text ('1 Year Cloud Retention' in the example) on rows CORE already reads, so it can go in the evidence (text only, no numeric field). (2) The snapshot filter's wire name the proposer could not read is backup_started_before (also backup_started_after, sort_asc, sort_by, snapshot_location with exists_cloud / exists_deleted_retention). (3) 'partly when a value is missing' removed: missing data is not a partial practice. (4) Added the mixed Slide + Datto cap; a scope note alone would let 'Met' stand for a client whose Datto machines are unread. (5) Stated plainly that the status can never be not_met.

**A054. Are all backups are encrypted with at least 256 bit AES encryption?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 11; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: Slide
- How: GET https://api.slide.tech/v1/agent -> encryption_algorithm ('Storage encryption algorithm of the agent', string, example 'aes-256-gcm'). Rows already fetched by SLIDE_SWEEP.loadContext, so no new API call: new check (suggested key slide_encryption) in hud/src/aisoc-slide-checks.ts + one CHECK_ANSWERS line in assessment_review.py. No such key exists today (grep of aisoc-slide*.ts for encryption_algorithm: zero hits).
- Rule: Polarity normal (yes is good). met: every protected system that is still being backed up returns an encryption_algorithm naming AES with a 256-bit key (aes-256 / aes256, any mode). not_met: any system returns a different or shorter-key algorithm (evidence names up to 3). The field is NOT in the Agent schema's required list, so it may be absent: a system with no value is left out, and nothing is sent when no system returns one (do not send 'partly' for missing data). Not sent when the client has no Slide system. When the client also holds the liongard_datto_bcdr check (Datto BCDR machines present), cap at partly by code: Datto agent encryption is not read anywhere in CORE and whether Datto's API exposes it is UNCONFIRMED, and the question says 'all backups'. Cloud backups of Microsoft 365 / Google are outside this 'Server Backup' group. NOT VERIFIED live: only the schema and its example were read, not what this account returns.
- Needs: Accept that Datto BCDR clients keep a typed answer for this question and that mixed Slide + Datto clients show Partly, not Met.
- Reviewer (corrected): Field confirmed in Slide's OpenAPI; category holds. Fixed: (1) encryption_algorithm is optional in the schema, so absence must mean 'not judged', not 'partly'. (2) A static scope note does not stop 'Met' for a client that also has Datto BCDR machines; added a cap using the liongard_datto_bcdr key that assessment_answers already receives. (3) needs_from_hal was empty although the proposal leaves Datto clients on a typed answer; that is an owner acceptance.

### Assessment: Backup and disaster recovery / Workstations

**A055. Is there a workstation image taken of all desktops at least daily?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N device inventory (Slide and Datto BCDR device sources; other tools for the list of workstations)
- How: devices + device_sources already held and fed by CORE's device sync (aisoc-devices.ts calls slideAgentDevices and bcdrDevices): sources[].vendor in infra_review.BACKUP_TOOLS ('slide', 'datto-bcdr'); datto-bcdr details.last_snapshot_at; slide details.backup_paused_indefinite / backup_paused_until / last_seen_at (agent check-in, NOT a backup time). Workstation = kind 'computer' with an OS that does not contain 'server' (infra_review._is_server / _windows_workstation). New pure rule in infra_review.py; the client_review endpoint must pass the device list (`shown`) into the assessment step, because assessment_answers() receives only checks today (line `assess_worked = assessment_answers({**tool_checks, ...})`). For a true 'daily' on Slide machines a small CORE device-feed addition is needed: agent_mode and backup_interval_minutes (workstation mode: 30 to 1440 minutes, i.e. at most 24 hours) from the /v1/agent rows already fetched.
- Rule: Polarity normal (yes is good). Denominator: the client's non-server computers with a known OS that are not hidden / long offline. A Datto-only device has os = null (bcdrDevices sends none), so it is a workstation only when another tool supplies the OS; otherwise leave it out. met: every workstation has a backup source that is provably daily: Datto last_snapshot_at within about 24 hours, or (after the CORE addition) Slide in workstation mode, not paused, interval <= 1440. With today's held data a Slide workstation can only count as 'protected, frequency not shown', so a Slide-only answer tops out at partly; 'not paused' is not evidence of daily (server-mode schedules can skip days). partly: some workstations protected. not_met: the client has machines in Slide or Datto BCDR but no workstation among them; evidence must say only Slide and Datto BCDR are read (NinjaOne Backup, Datto endpoint backup / /v1/dtc/assets are not collected), so a person can overrule. needs_person: no machine reports either tool. Existing STALE_SNAPSHOT_DAYS is 3, so this rule needs its own 24-hour threshold. NOT VERIFIED live: aisoc-datto.ts says the BCDR fields were never checked against a live account.
- Needs: Decision: do laptops count as 'desktops', and is 'workstations not imaged by design (files live in OneDrive/SharePoint)' a Not met or a Not applicable for your clients?
- Reviewer (corrected): Held data confirmed (both device feeds exist and carry the named fields); category holds for coverage and for Datto recency. Fixed: (1) 'Slide: not paused' was accepted as daily; it is not, and A.C.O.R.N holds no Slide backup time or mode, so Slide-only answers are capped at partly until CORE adds agent_mode / backup_interval_minutes to the Slide device details (fields confirmed in Slide's OpenAPI, rows already fetched). (2) Datto-only devices carry no OS, so they cannot be classed as workstations on their own. (3) Effort raised from S to M: new rule with two tools, a recency threshold different from the existing 3-day one, a changed assessment_answers input and endpoint, tests, plus the optional CORE field.

### Assessment: Backup and disaster recovery / Datacenter/Server Room Power

**A056. Is there UPS onsite?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 6; small; confidence medium).
- Source: Auvik (device inventory already stored in A.C.O.R.N)
- How: device_sources where vendor = 'auvik' and details.device_type = 'ups'. CORE's auvikDevices() stores Auvik's deviceType as details.device_type for every discovered device (aisoc-auvik.ts) and the device sync sends them (aisoc-devices.ts fetchDevices -> auvikDeviceRecords). 'ups' is an allowed deviceType value per the Celerium.Auvik wrapper's parameter list (third-party; Auvik's own spec is too long to reach DeviceTypeSchema, so the enum was not read first-hand). infra_review.network_role() already reads the same field. New pure rule in A.C.O.R.N; the endpoint must pass the device list to the assessment step (same change as A055). Optional later source: client_documents rows of kind 'configuration' whose folder (IT Glue configuration type name) is a UPS type; that needs the endpoint to start reading client_documents and belongs with the A058 convention.
- Rule: Polarity normal (yes is good). met: at least one Auvik device of type 'ups' is listed for the client (evidence: count, make/model, online_status). Never not_met automatically: a UPS with no network card is invisible to Auvik and only some clients have Auvik, so 'none seen' leaves needs_person. Limits to state in the evidence: Auvik sees one collector's network, so for a multi-site client a UPS at one site does not show the server room has one. NOT VERIFIED live: whether any client's Auvik inventory actually classifies a UPS as 'ups' (a UPS network card may come back as 'unknown').
- Reviewer (corrected): Auvik route confirmed in code and the 'ups' value confirmed (third-party source only). Changed: the IT Glue second source was presented as 'no CORE change' inside an S item; it needs a new client_documents read in the review endpoint and account-specific type names, and CORE's IT Glue docs sweep is marked not verified live, so it is moved out of this item (it belongs with the A058 convention) and needs_from_hal is now empty. Added the multi-site limit; 'onsite' is only true for the site the collector sits on.

**A057. Does the UPS have software installed and configured to shut down server in the event of a power failure**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue (the per-UPS record agreed for A058), read through the documents feed CORE already sends
- How: Convention: on the IT Glue UPS record used for A058, two fields: 'Shutdown software configured' (Yes/No) and 'Shutdown method' (e.g. agent on the server, network card + appliance). CORE's aisoc-itglue-docs.ts already sends every flexible asset as a client_documents row (kind 'asset', folder = asset type name, body = one 'field name: value' line per field); a new pure rule in A.C.O.R.N reads the line. No CORE change; the review endpoint must start reading client_documents (it reads none today). Optional supporting evidence later, not an answer: NinjaOne. GET /v2/queries/windows-services?name=<service> is read live in aisoc-ninja-checks.ts and would sit inside the existing NinjaOne check sweep under source 'ninjaone' with no new source (cheaper than the proposed /v2/queries/software route, which needs a new source row); the UPS agents' service names are UNCONFIRMED.
- Rule: Polarity normal (yes is good). met: every UPS record that protects a server says 'Shutdown software configured: Yes'. not_met: any says No. needs_person: no UPS record, or the field is blank. Evidence quotes the method and the record's date. This is a recorded statement, not a measurement: no connected tool shows the shutdown setting inside the UPS software. A software or service sighting from NinjaOne can only ever say 'an agent is installed on server X' (partly at best) and misses hypervisor hosts shut down by a network card, so it must not set the status on its own.
