- Needs: Agree the convention with A058: the IT Glue asset type name and the exact field names ('Shutdown software configured', 'Shutdown method'), and that techs fill them in when a UPS is installed or changed.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The proposed NinjaOne software check does not answer the question as worded: it can never reach met ('configured' is invisible to a software list), it stays silent when nothing is found, its product names came from memory, CORE's own header says the software rows' field names were not checked live, and it needs a new source row in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES. That is M of work for a permanent 'partly' that a person must finish anyway. Changed to documentation_convention on the same IT Glue UPS record as A058 (the proposer's own fallback), which can give met / not_met. The /v2/queries/software endpoint itself is real and already paged by CORE (aisoc-remote-tools.ts); if a sighting is ever wanted as supporting evidence, the windows-services name filter is the cheaper route.

**A058. Does the UPS have 15 minutes of run time to shut down server(s).**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 15; medium; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue (one flexible asset per UPS), read through the documents feed CORE already sends
- How: Convention: one IT Glue flexible asset per UPS (type e.g. 'UPS') with fields 'Runtime at current load (minutes)', 'Runtime checked on' (date), 'Protects' (servers), 'Batteries replaced' (date), plus the two A057 fields. CORE's aisoc-itglue-docs.ts already sends every flexible asset to A.C.O.R.N as a client_documents row: kind 'asset', folder = asset type name, body = one 'field name: value' line per field (numbers and dates arrive as text), vendor_updated_at. No CORE change; a new pure rule in A.C.O.R.N parses the runtime line, and the review endpoint must start reading client_documents (it reads none today). No tool measures it: grep of hud/src aisoc-*.ts for ups / battery / runtime finds nothing relevant, and nothing about battery or UPS runtime appears in the parts of Auvik's spec that could be read (UNCONFIRMED for the unread part).
- Rule: Polarity normal (yes is good). met: every UPS record that protects a server shows a runtime of 15 minutes or more. not_met: any shows under 15. needs_person: no UPS record, the runtime is blank or not a number, or 'Runtime checked on' is blank or older than 12 months (batteries age). Use the dated field for staleness, not vendor_updated_at: IT Glue's updated-at moves on any edit to the record, so it would keep an old figure alive. Evidence quotes the figure and the date. This is a recorded number, not a live measurement. Self-refreshing alternative, more work: a NinjaOne script on the server attached to the UPS writing the runtime to a device custom field, read by a new CORE check from /v2/queries/custom-fields (path seen only in a third-party catalogue; CORE reads no custom fields today; UNCONFIRMED). The IT Glue docs sweep is marked NOT VERIFIED live in CORE's header.
- Needs: Agree the convention: the IT Glue asset type name and exact field names for UPS records, and that techs record the runtime figure and the date it was read (from the UPS display or its software) when a UPS is installed or its batteries are changed.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, source and feed shape confirmed in code (assetRecord writes 'field name: value' lines; client_documents has kind, folder, body, vendor_updated_at; the review endpoint has no client_documents read). Fixed one rule detail: staleness was tied to vendor_updated_at, which changes on any edit of the record and so does not show when the runtime was read; added a dated field 'Runtime checked on' to the convention and made the 12-month expiry use it. Dropped the 'or the existing UPS configuration type with these as fields' option: configurationRecord() sends only a fixed set of configuration attributes, so custom fields on a configuration would not reach A.C.O.R.N; it has to be a flexible asset.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Apps - Groups for Business

**A059. Do you ensure accessing groups from outside this organization is set to private**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies?pageSize=100&filter=setting.type.matches('^settings/groups_for_business\\..*$') ; row type settings/groups_for_business.groups_sharing ; field setting.value.collaborationCapability (ANYONE_CAN_ACCESS / DOMAIN_USERS_ONLY). Same filter also returns settings/groups_for_business.service_status (value.serviceState). Proposed new key (does not exist today): gws_groups_external_access, new source 'google-workspace'.
- Rule: Verified by grep: no cloudidentity / admin.directory / groupssettings / gws_ string in hud/src or services/api; CORE's only Google sign-in is one mailbox (gmail.* + calendar scopes in setup/src/google-auth.ts); ThreatMate's Google routes CORE uses are user, failed-login and saas-app only. So nothing answers this today. With a connection: met when collaborationCapability is DOMAIN_USERS_ONLY (console 'Private', which is what CIS 3.1.6.1 asks for); not_met when ANYONE_CAN_ACCESS. No partly: this setting is customer-wide, so when several rows of the type come back keep the customer-level one with the greatest policyQuery.sortOrder (that is how one open-source scanner resolves Google's SYSTEM default next to the ADMIN row; Google's reference does not state it, UNCONFIRMED). If Groups for Business itself is off for everyone (service_status DISABLED), send met and say so. Absent row or field on a successful read: leave the check out until one live tenant shows whether never-changed settings are returned (Google's page only says missing settings fall back to documented defaults; the benchmark gives the default here as Private). NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Decision to build a Google Workspace connector (shared by A059-A069). Needs: one Google Cloud project with the Cloud Identity API on and one service account; for EACH Google client a super admin authorises that service account's client ID for domain-wide delegation with exactly https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and gives an admin address to impersonate (which admin privilege that address needs is UNCONFIRMED); a client mapping for the new vendor in A.C.O.R.N (vendor + Google customer id or primary domain). Quota is 1 request/second per project AND per customer and cannot be raised, so one pass over all clients must run one request at a time.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, endpoint, field and polarity hold: the docs page lists collaborationCapability with those two values, policies.list accepts the read-only scope, and a public scanner reads the same field with a service account, domain-wide delegation and that read-only scope. Fixed details: (1) the proposal's 'looser OU policy -> partly' does not fit a customer-wide setting; pick the customer-level row with the greatest sortOrder instead. (2) Added the Groups-for-Business-off case. (3) Quota is 1 request/second per PROJECT as well as per customer, which matters with one project serving every client. (4) Scope doubt resolved towards read-only (the setup page names only the read-write scope, the list reference names both). (5) A.C.O.R.N side is more than two lines: TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES maps source -> vendor and only mapped clients are stored, so the new vendor needs a client mapping; assessment evidence gets 'From <key>. ' put in front and is cut at 300. (6) Clients not on Google never get the check; a person sets not_applicable (an allowed typed status for this list).

**A060. Do you ensure creating groups is restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (same read as A059); row settings/groups_for_business.groups_sharing ; fields value.createGroupsAccessLevel (ADMIN_ONLY / USERS_IN_DOMAIN / ANYONE_CAN_CREATE), value.ownersCanAllowExternalMembers (boolean), value.ownersCanAllowIncomingMailFromPublic (boolean). Proposed new key: gws_groups_creation.
- Rule: No Google Workspace admin connection in CORE today (see A059). The benchmark behind this question (CIS 3.1.6.2) asks for 'Only organization admins can create groups' AND both owner options unticked, and names 'anyone in the organisation can create groups' as the out-of-the-box state it wants changed. So: met when createGroupsAccessLevel is ADMIN_ONLY and neither owner flag is true (an absent flag counts as false, Google's default); partly when ADMIN_ONLY but owners may allow external members or mail from the public (name which); not_met when USERS_IN_DOMAIN or ANYONE_CAN_CREATE. Customer-wide setting: use the customer-level row with the greatest sortOrder. If Groups for Business is off for everyone, send met and say so. Absent createGroupsAccessLevel on a successful read is, per the benchmark, the insecure default, but leave the check out until one live tenant confirms how never-changed settings come back. NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A059 (one service account, per-client domain-wide delegation with cloud-identity.policies.readonly, client mapping). No policy decision needed: the benchmark settles that 'users in the organisation' is not restricted.
- Reviewer (corrected): The rule was too lenient in two places. (1) USERS_IN_DOMAIN was proposed as partly pending an owner decision; the benchmark's remediation accepts only admins-only and lists organisation-wide creation as the default to fix, so it is not_met and no decision is needed. (2) The two owner flags were proposed as evidence only; the benchmark's remediation requires both unticked, so they now pull an admins-only tenant down to partly. Endpoint, field names (camelCase, confirmed on Google's page and in a public scanner's code) and category unchanged.

**A061. Do you ensure default for permission to view conversations is restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (same read as A059); row settings/groups_for_business.groups_sharing ; field value.viewTopicsDefaultAccessLevel (OWNERS / MANAGERS / GROUP_MEMBERS / DOMAIN_USERS / ANYONE_CAN_VIEW_TOPICS). Proposed new key: gws_groups_view_default.
- Rule: No Google Workspace admin connection in CORE today (see A059). The benchmark (CIS 3.1.6.3) asks for 'All group members' and names 'all organisation users' as the default it wants changed. So: met when the value is OWNERS, MANAGERS or GROUP_MEMBERS; not_met when DOMAIN_USERS or ANYONE_CAN_VIEW_TOPICS. No partly. Customer-wide setting: use the customer-level row with the greatest sortOrder; if Groups for Business is off for everyone send met and say so. This is the default for NEW groups only (owners and managers can change it per group), which is what the question asks; put that limit in the evidence. Existing open groups would need a per-group Groups Settings read (whoCanViewGroup), a different and larger job, not proposed. Absent field on a successful read: leave out until checked on one live tenant. NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A059. No policy decision needed: the benchmark treats 'all organisation users' as not restricted.
- Reviewer (corrected): DOMAIN_USERS was proposed as partly with an owner decision. The benchmark's remediation is 'All group members' and its stated default (the thing being flagged) is all organisation users, so DOMAIN_USERS is not_met and the decision is not needed. Field and enum values confirmed on Google's page (caption: 'Default for permission to view conversations'). Rest unchanged.

**A062. Do you ensure service status for Google Sites is set to off**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies?filter=setting.type.matches('^.*\\.service_status$') (Google's own filter example) ; row type settings/sites.service_status ; field setting.value.serviceState. Proposed new key: gws_sites_service_off.
- Rule: No Google Workspace admin connection in CORE today (see A059). Google's page lists 'sites' among the Workspace services whose service_status is reported per organisational unit or group. Read every row of this type: met when the customer-level row (greatest sortOrder) says the service is off and no organisational-unit or group row turns it on; partly when off at the top but on for some unit or group (name them in items); not_met when on at the top. The benchmark (CIS 3.1.7.1) wants 'OFF for everyone' and says the default is on for everyone. Field name serviceState comes from a public scanner's code, not from Google's page; the value strings (expected ENABLED / DISABLED) are UNCONFIRMED, so read one live row before writing the parser and leave the check out on any value not recognised. Absent row on a successful read: leave out until checked live (the default would be on, i.e. not_met). The question sits under the template's 'Groups for Business' heading but is about Sites. NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A059 (one service account, per-client domain-wide delegation with cloud-identity.policies.readonly, client mapping).
- Reviewer (corrected): Route holds. Filled the gap the proposer flagged: the field inside setting.value is serviceState (read that way by a public scanner for exactly this control), though its value strings are still unconfirmed. Confirmed 'sites' is in Google's list of Workspace services for service_status and that the filter example is Google's own. Added the benchmark's wanted state and default, and the top-level row selection by sortOrder.

**A063. Do you ensure access to external Google Groups is OFF for Everyone**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies with the service_status filter (same read as A062) ; row type settings/groups.service_status ('groups' = the Additional Google service 'Google Groups', separate from the core service 'groups_for_business') ; field setting.value.serviceState. Proposed new key: gws_external_groups_off.

The mapping is no longer a guess: the benchmark item with this exact title (CIS 3.1.8.1) is remediated under Additional Google services > Google Groups, 'OFF for everyone', default on for everyone; Google's page lists 'groups' under Additional Google services; a public scanner reads settings/groups.service_status for this control. met when the service is off at the customer level (greatest sortOrder) and no organisational-unit or group row turns it on; partly when off at the top but on for some unit or group (name them); not_met when on at the top. The parser must not confuse this row with groups_for_business.service_status. Value strings of serviceState (expected ENABLED / DISABLED) are UNCONFIRMED; read one live row first. Absent row on a successful read: leave out until checked live. NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A059. No separate confirmation of what the question means is needed any more.
- Reviewer (corrected): The proposer rated this low because the link between the question and the Additional-services switch was their own reading and the field name was unknown. Both are now sourced: the benchmark's remediation text names Additional Google services > Google Groups OFF for everyone, and a public scanner reads settings/groups.service_status -> serviceState for it. Confidence raised to medium and the owner's one-time confirmation dropped. Still open: the exact value strings and never-changed-default behaviour.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Apps - Chat/Hangouts

**A064. Do you ensure external filesharing in Google Chat and Hangouts is disabled**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies?filter=setting.type.matches('^settings/chat\\..*$') ; row type settings/chat.chat_file_sharing ; field value.externalFileSharing (Google's page prints it as external_file_sharing) with ALL_FILES / IMAGES_ONLY / NO_FILES / FILE_SHARING_OPTION_UNSPECIFIED. Proposed new key: gws_chat_external_files.
- Rule: No Google Workspace admin connection in CORE today (see A059). met when the value is NO_FILES (the only state the benchmark, CIS 3.1.4.1.1, accepts); partly when IMAGES_ONLY (restricted but not disabled; say so); not_met when ALL_FILES; leave the check out on UNSPECIFIED or an absent field. Chat settings can differ by organisational unit: judge the customer-level row (greatest sortOrder) and drop a met to partly when any unit or group row is looser (list them in items). Accept both externalFileSharing and external_file_sharing: a public scanner reads the camelCase name, Google's page shows snake_case. The template says 'Chat and Hangouts'; only the Chat setting exists, say so in the evidence. NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A059 (one service account, per-client domain-wide delegation with cloud-identity.policies.readonly, client mapping).
- Reviewer (corrected): Route, enum values and polarity hold. Details fixed: the JSON name is camelCase in practice (externalFileSharing) per a public scanner's code, so that is the primary name with snake_case as fallback; added the UNSPECIFIED value Google lists (leave out); noted that the benchmark passes NO_FILES only, so IMAGES_ONLY must never show as met.

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (same chat.* read as A064) ; row type settings/chat.chat_file_sharing ; field value.internalFileSharing (page prints internal_file_sharing) with ALL_FILES / IMAGES_ONLY / NO_FILES / FILE_SHARING_OPTION_UNSPECIFIED. Proposed new key: gws_chat_internal_files.
- Rule: No Google Workspace admin connection in CORE today (see A059); same row as A064. met when NO_FILES (the only state CIS 3.1.4.1.2 accepts); partly when IMAGES_ONLY; not_met when ALL_FILES; leave out on UNSPECIFIED or an absent field. Customer-level row (greatest sortOrder) decides; a looser organisational-unit or group row pulls a met down to partly. Accept camelCase and snake_case names. Most organisations keep internal file sharing on by choice, so not_met will be common and often an accepted decision that a person records as a typed answer; say that in the scope note. NOT VERIFIED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A059.
- Reviewer (corrected): Same detail fixes as A064: camelCase field name first (internalFileSharing), UNSPECIFIED value handled, benchmark passes NO_FILES only. Google's page gives the internal field's values as 'same' as the external one rather than listing them. Nothing else changed.

**A066. Do you ensure Google Chat externally is restricted to allowed domains**

**A069. Do you allow users to add and use incoming webhooks?**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).

- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (same chat.* read as A064) ; row type settings/chat.chat_apps_access ; field value.enableWebhooks (boolean; page prints enable_webhooks). Same row as A068. Proposed new key: gws_chat_webhooks.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Directory

**A070. Does more than one Super Admin account exist**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 4; medium; confidence high).
- Source: ThreatMate (Google Workspace integration), user rows CORE already reads
- How: No new vendor call. hud/src/aisoc-threatmate-google.ts already reads GET /api/v1/integration/google/workspace/{id}/user and adminsCheck() already filters live users with isSuperAdmin === true, but only emits threatmate_google_admins (2-Step). Add a PROPOSED key threatmate_google_super_admin_min (does not exist today; grep of hud/src and services/api finds only threatmate_google_admins / _stale_accounts / _failed_logins / _apps), sent under the existing source threatmate-google, plus one CHECK_ANSWERS row in assessment_review.py. threatmate-google is in infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES and its row is flattened into tool_checks, which is passed to assessment_answers (endpoint client_review.py lines 551 and 574), so no endpoint change. Needs a per-workspace count, so ClientRows must keep which workspace each user came from (today users are flattened per client).
- Rule: Yes is good. Count live users (not deleted, not suspended, with an e-mail) with isSuperAdmin true PER WORKSPACE, not summed across the client's workspaces: met when every workspace has 2 or more; not_met when any workspace has exactly 1; send nothing when a workspace shows 0 (incomplete data) or when the user list came back at the 5000-row limit (single unpaged read, could be truncated). Evidence states the number per workspace. Coverage: only clients whose Google Workspace is integrated in ThreatMate and mapped; all others stay needs_person. Deriving in A.C.O.R.N from held data does not hold: Multi-Factor rows set admin = isSuperAdmin // isDelegatedAdmin (line 91), identity_sources ORs super_admin across Microsoft and Google for one address (aisoc-identities.ts line 548), and the review endpoint does not read identity_sources at all.
- Reviewer (corrected): Route and category hold (verified in code: the field is read and filtered, no existing key answers the question, the source is already passed to the assessment). Fixed the rule: the proposal counted super administrators across all of a client's workspaces, which would call two workspaces with one admin each 'met'; CIS 1.1.1 is per Workspace account, so count per workspace and let the worst decide. That needs the sweep to keep the workspace per user, which the proposal did not mention. Added: skip when the 5000-row unpaged user read may be truncated. Also not mentioned: the new key will appear on the Client posture page and feed the posture score and check history, so it needs a label in ToolChecks.tsx and a help line in checkHelp.ts, or it shows as a raw key. Not verified by me: that ThreatMate's isSuperAdmin equals Google's super administrator role (the file header says the fields were checked live; I only read that comment), and how fresh ThreatMate's copy of the workspace is.

**A071. Is there more than 4 Super Admin accounts existing in the account**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 4; small; confidence high).
- Source: ThreatMate (Google Workspace integration), user rows CORE already reads
- How: Same rows and same place as A070: a second PROPOSED key threatmate_google_super_admin_max (does not exist today) in hud/src/aisoc-threatmate-google.ts from the same per-workspace isSuperAdmin count, sent under source threatmate-google, plus one CHECK_ANSWERS row. Two keys are needed: one admin fails A070 and passes A071, five admins the reverse, and CHECK_ANSWERS has no way to invert or reinterpret a status.
- Rule: Yes is the BAD answer. Per workspace, count live super administrators: met when every workspace has 1 to 4 (meaning NOT more than 4); not_met when any workspace has 5 or more; nothing sent on 0 or on a possibly truncated user list. Because the page prints 'Met' beside a question worded 'Is there more than 4...', the evidence must say it plainly, e.g. '3 Google super administrators; the template wants no more than 4.', with the addresses in items when over. Same coverage limit as A070 (ThreatMate-integrated, mapped workspaces only).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, polarity and the two-key reasoning hold (CIS 1.1.2 is 'no more than 4'; the question text in assessment_questions.py line 164 is unique, so a CHECK_ANSWERS prefix will match exactly one). Same fix as A070: count per workspace, not summed across a client's workspaces (two workspaces with three admins each would wrongly read as 6 = not_met). Same additions: skip on a possibly truncated 5000-row read; add a label and help line in the web app for the new key. Effort S is only true once A070's per-workspace change exists.

**A072. Is directory data access is externally restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API); CORE has no Google Workspace admin connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies with filter setting.type.matches(...) on the setting type settings/directory.external_directory_sharing (Admin console: Directory settings > Sharing settings > External Directory sharing), field sharing_option with values SHARING_OPTION_UNSPECIFIED / REQUESTER_BASIC_PROFILE_ONLY / ORGANIZATION_DIRECTORY_DATA. Read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly is listed on the method's reference page. Verified absent from CORE: grep of hud/src for admin.directory, cloudidentity, admin/directory finds nothing outside connectors-poll.ts and the ThreatMate Google file (which reads only users, failed sign-ins and third-party apps); the only Google sign-in is one person's Gmail and Calendar (setup/src/google-auth.ts scopes gmail.readonly, gmail.send, gmail.modify, calendar).
- Rule: Yes is good. met when the customer-level value is REQUESTER_BASIC_PROFILE_ONLY (CIS 1.2.1.1 wants 'Public data and authenticated user basic profile fields'); not_met when ORGANIZATION_DIRECTORY_DATA ('Domain and public data'); partly when the customer-level value is restrictive but an organisational-unit or group policy is wider; not sent when unspecified, missing or unreadable. The API returns Google's SYSTEM default next to the ADMIN policy, so take the customer-level policy with the highest sortOrder, not 'every policy returned'. UNCONFIRMED: that the two enum names correspond to those two console labels (inferred from the names; check on one live tenant); what is returned when the setting was never touched. New checks need a new source (e.g. google-workspace) in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES and a client mapping for the new vendor, or the review never reads them.
- Needs: A decision to build a Google Workspace admin connector. Then: ONE Google Cloud service account in the MSP's own project (not one per client), its key stored safely on the CORE VM; per client, a super administrator of that Workspace must authorise that service account's client ID for domain-wide delegation with exactly the scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and name an admin address for it to act as (minimum admin role not stated in the docs I read); and a way to map each Google customer to an A.C.O.R.N client. Until then: typed answer.
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, setting type, field, enum values, read-only scope and the CIS wording all confirmed in Google's public docs and the Prowler CIS mapping; category and effort L hold. Corrections: (1) the setting type string carries a 'settings/' prefix and the filter uses setting.type.matches(...); (2) the setup page says the delegated scope must match exactly and documents only a service account with domain-wide delegation, but one service account can be authorised by many clients, so 'per client: a service account' overstated the ask; (3) the rule must reduce to the customer-level policy with the highest sortOrder (Prowler's reader documents that SYSTEM defaults come back beside ADMIN policies) and treat unit or group overrides as partly. Note Prowler has NO check for 1.2.1.1 even though the setting is listed, so nobody I could find has exercised this setting through the API: confidence stays medium. Whether ThreatMate's own API exposes Google settings is unknown (its docs are not public); CORE reads none.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Apps - Calendar

**A073. Do you ensure external sharing options for primary calendars are configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence high).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API); CORE has no Google Workspace admin connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies filtered to setting type settings/calendar.primary_calendar_max_allowed_external_sharing (Admin console: Calendar > Sharing settings > External sharing options for primary calendars), field max_allowed_external_sharing with values EXTERNAL_FREE_BUSY_ONLY / EXTERNAL_ALL_INFO_READ_ONLY / EXTERNAL_ALL_INFO_READ_WRITE / EXTERNAL_ALL_INFO_READ_WRITE_MANAGE; also read calendar.service_status from the same call. Same new connector as A072. Prowler's check calendar_external_sharing_primary_calendar reads exactly this setting through this API, which shows it works in practice.
- Rule: Yes is good; 'configured' means the CIS 3.1.1.1.1 value 'Only free/busy information (hide event details)'. Use the customer-level policy with the highest sortOrder (SYSTEM defaults are returned beside ADMIN policies): met when EXTERNAL_FREE_BUSY_ONLY, or when Calendar is switched off for everyone; partly when the customer-level value is free/busy only but a unit or group override is wider; not_met for any of the three wider values. No policy returned: Prowler treats it as Google's default (free/busy only) and passes; that default is UNCONFIRMED from Google's own docs, so send nothing until one live read confirms it. Evidence names the value and any unit that differs.
- Needs: Same as A072: decision to build a Google Workspace admin connector; one MSP service account, authorised per client for domain-wide delegation with scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and an admin address per client to act as.
- Reviewer (corrected): Setting, field, four enum values, console location and CIS value confirmed in public docs. Fixed the rule: 'met when every policy returned is EXTERNAL_FREE_BUSY_ONLY' is wrong because the API returns Google's SYSTEM default beside the admin's policy; the effective value is the customer-level policy with the highest sortOrder (per Prowler's reader). Added the Calendar-off case and the no-policy case, which the proposal left as not verified: Prowler passes it as Google's default, but I could not confirm that default from Google. Effort L is the shared connector; once A072's connector exists this check is a small addition. needs_from_hal corrected to one service account authorised per client.

**A074. Do you ensure external invitation warnings for Google Calendar are configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence high).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API); CORE has no Google Workspace admin connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies filtered to setting type settings/calendar.external_invitations (Admin console: Calendar > Sharing settings > External Invitations), boolean field warn_on_invite (warnOnInvite in the JSON). Same new connector as A072. Prowler's check calendar_external_invitations_warning reads this setting through this API.
- Rule: Yes is good. Using the customer-level policy with the highest sortOrder: met when warn_on_invite is true (CIS 3.1.1.1.3: 'Warn users when inviting guests outside of the domain' checked), or when Calendar is off for everyone; not_met when false; partly when the customer-level value is true but a unit or group override turns it off. No policy returned: Prowler passes it as Google's default (enabled); that default is UNCONFIRMED from Google's own docs, so send nothing until one live read confirms it.
- Needs: Same as A072: decision to build a Google Workspace admin connector; one MSP service account, authorised per client for domain-wide delegation with scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and an admin address per client to act as.
- Reviewer (corrected): Setting, field and CIS wording confirmed in public docs; category and polarity hold. Added what the proposal left open: effective value = customer-level policy with the highest sortOrder (SYSTEM defaults come back beside ADMIN ones); Calendar-off counts as met; a missing policy is treated by Prowler as Google's default (enabled) but that is not confirmed from Google, so do not answer on it yet. Setting type string carries the 'settings/' prefix. needs_from_hal corrected to one service account authorised per client. Effort L is the shared connector, not this check alone.

**A076. Do you ensure internal sharing options for secondary calendars are configured**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none
- How: No read API found. I read Google's full list of settings the Cloud Identity Policy API exposes (both halves, 2026-11-11): the calendar settings are appointment_schedules, external_invitations, interoperability and the two EXTERNAL max-sharing settings; there is no internal-sharing setting for primary or secondary calendars. Prowler's CIS mapping has an empty check list for 3.1.1.2.2. CORE has no Google admin connection anyway. Route: the typed answer that already exists on the review (client_review_answers, section assess, statuses met / partly / not_met / needs_person / not_applicable). review_overrides.py has no expiry of any kind today, so a 12-month expiry would be new code.
- Rule: A person opens Admin console > Apps > Google Workspace > Calendar > Sharing settings (CIS says General settings) > Internal sharing options for secondary calendars. met when it is 'Only free/busy information (hide event details)' (the CIS 3.1.1.2.2 value); not_met when it shares all information. Record the date checked in the note; if the expiry is built, fall back to needs_person after 12 months. This is a setting Google holds, not a judgement: recheck Google's Policy API settings list if a Google connector is built, since settings are added over time. The Admin SDK Reports admin activity log could at best show the last change inside its retention, never a dependable current state (NOT CONFIRMED, not proposed).

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Apps - Drive and Docs

**A078. Do you ensure users are warned when they share a file outside their domain**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection exists today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (read-only list; filter setting.type.matches('^settings/drive_and_docs\\..*$'), pageSize max 100). Setting drive_and_docs.external_sharing, fields warn_for_external_sharing (boolean) and external_sharing_mode (DISALLOWED / ALLOWLISTED_DOMAINS / ALLOWED). Proposed NEW check key (does not exist in either repo): gws_drive_external_warning, under a new source/vendor that must be added to infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES (the ingest refuses a source not listed there), plus a client mapping for the new vendor and a CHECK_ANSWERS row.
- Rule: Yes is good. Work out the effective setting the way Google documents it: of the policies that apply (org unit, group, licence), the one with the greatest policyQuery.sortOrder supplies every field (reducer 'Max' for this setting); a field missing from it takes Google's documented default (warn_for_external_sharing default true, external_sharing_mode default ALLOWED). Mode DISALLOWED -> met. Mode ALLOWED: warn_for_external_sharing true -> met, false -> not_met. Mode ALLOWLISTED_DOMAINS: use warn_for_sharing_outside_allowlisted_domains the same way (default true). Customer-wide value good but an org-unit or group policy gives a weaker value -> partly. When the answer rests on a default and not a returned value, say so in the evidence. UNCONFIRMED: whether a tenant that never touched the setting returns a SYSTEM policy or nothing at all (Google's page covers missing fields; the settings page points to the same default table for missing settings) - check one live tenant before trusting a default-based 'met'.
- Needs: Decision to build a Google Workspace admin connector (none exists). Per client: a Google Cloud service account with domain-wide delegation authorised in that client's Admin console, acting as a super administrator of that client (Google: only a super administrator can use the Policy API), Cloud Identity API enabled, and a client mapping in A.C.O.R.N. Scope: the list method accepts cloud-identity.policies.readonly, but Google's setup guide authorises cloud-identity.policies and says the scope must be authorised exactly as written - NOT VERIFIED that the read-only scope works with delegation. Impersonating a super administrator in every client is a security decision for the owner and each client.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, endpoint, field names and enum values hold (re-read in Google's docs; no existing key or connector in either repo). Two things were wrong or missing. (1) The proposer said defaults could not be confirmed and so 'no policy -> do not send'. Google's Policy API concepts page has a default-value table: warn_for_external_sharing true, external_sharing_mode ALLOWED, and so on, so a missing field has a documented meaning. (2) 'Judge the root policy, weaker override -> partly' is not how Google resolves it: the policy with the greatest sortOrder wins per user (reducer Max), filtered by org unit, group and licence. Rule rewritten accordingly. Also added that Google's setup guide names the read/write scope, so the read-only scope with delegation is unproven. I found no cheaper source: ThreatMate's Google reads in CORE have no Drive settings.

**A079. Do you ensure users cannot publish files to the web or make visible to the world as public or unlisted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection exists today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (same single read as A078); setting drive_and_docs.external_sharing, field allow_publishing_files (boolean; Google's caption: when outside sharing is allowed, users can make files and published web content visible to anyone with the link), read with external_sharing_mode. Proposed NEW check key: gws_drive_publishing_blocked.
- Rule: Yes (cannot publish) is good; the API field is the opposite sense. Effective value as in A078 (greatest sortOrder wins; missing field takes the documented default, and the default here is allow_publishing_files true, i.e. the bad answer). Mode DISALLOWED -> met. Mode ALLOWED: false -> met, true -> not_met. Mode ALLOWLISTED_DOMAINS: UNCONFIRMED what the field means there (the caption only speaks of outside sharing being allowed), so do not send in that mode until one live tenant is compared with its console. Weaker org-unit or group value -> partly.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector and decision as A078 (per-client service account with domain-wide delegation acting as a super administrator).
- Reviewer (corrected): Field and caption confirmed. Changed: 'no policy returned -> not sent' replaced by Google's documented default (allow_publishing_files defaults to true, so an untouched tenant is not_met, not unknown); and the proposer's 'otherwise false -> met' silently covered allowlist mode, where the field's meaning is not documented - that mode is now left to a person until checked live. Effort L is the shared connector; once it exists this check is small.

**A080. Do you ensure document sharing is being controlled by domain with allowlists**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection exists today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies; setting drive_and_docs.external_sharing, field external_sharing_mode (DISALLOWED / ALLOWLISTED_DOMAINS / ALLOWED). Proposed NEW check key: gws_drive_sharing_allowlist.
- Rule: Yes is good. Effective value as in A078 (greatest sortOrder wins; documented default is ALLOWED). ALLOWLISTED_DOMAINS -> met; DISALLOWED -> met (stricter than asked; say so in the evidence); ALLOWED, including the default -> not_met. Customer-wide value good but an org unit or group is ALLOWED -> partly. The check cannot judge whether the domains on the allowlist are sensible; that stays with a person.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector and decision as A078.
- Reviewer (corrected): Enum values confirmed. Added the documented default (ALLOWED), so a tenant that never changed the setting is not_met and need not be left unanswered, and replaced 'root vs org unit' with Google's sortOrder resolution. Nothing else changed.

**A081. Do you ensure users are warned when they share a file with users in an allowlisted domain**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection exists today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies; setting drive_and_docs.external_sharing, field warn_for_sharing_outside_allowlisted_domains (boolean; despite the name, Google's caption is a warning when files are shared with users in allowlisted domains), read with external_sharing_mode and warn_for_external_sharing. Proposed NEW check key: gws_drive_allowlist_warning.
- Rule: Yes is good. Effective value as in A078 (documented default for this field is true). Mode ALLOWLISTED_DOMAINS: true -> met, false -> not_met. Mode DISALLOWED -> met (no outside sharing at all). Mode ALLOWED (no allowlist in force): warn_for_external_sharing true -> partly, with evidence that users are warned on every outside share but no allowlist exists (A080 already fails for that); false -> not_met. Weaker org-unit or group value -> partly. Because the field name and the caption disagree, compare one live tenant with its console before trusting the boolean.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector and decision as A078.
- Reviewer (corrected): Field and caption confirmed in Google's settings list. Changed the ALLOWED-mode branch: the proposer sent a flat not_met, which would state users are not warned when the tenant may warn on every outside share; it is now partly when warn_for_external_sharing is true. Added the documented default (true) and the sortOrder resolution.

Proposed NEW check key: gws_shared_drive_creation.

Some org units or groups blocked while the customer-wide value allows -> partly. Google documents NO default for any drive_and_docs.shared_drive_creation field, so when the field or setting is absent do not send the check (question stays with a person). **A089. Do you ensure Add-Ons is disabled**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).

- How: No readable source for the setting the question is about (Admin console > Apps > Google Workspace > Drive and Docs > Features and Applications > Add-ons). The words add-on/addon do not occur anywhere in Google's Policy API settings list (both halves of the page read). Keep as a typed answer in client_review_answers (section assess). The Marketplace setting workspace_marketplace.apps_access_options.access_level exists in the Policy API but must NOT be used to answer this: Google states that service-specific settings override the Marketplace installation setting. threatmate_google_apps (OAuth grants to third-party apps) is a different fact and does not answer it either.

A person types met when the Docs editors Add-ons box is off for the whole organisation, partly when it is off for some org units only, not_met when it is on, with the date looked at in the note. Same proposed 12-month fall-back to needs_person as A088. No automatic suggestion: Marketplace ALLOW_NONE -> met is UNCONFIRMED (the override sentence reads the other way) and ALLOW_LISTED_APPS -> partly has no basis in the question.
- Needs: Agreement that typed review answers should expire after 12 months (same as A088). Nothing else.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The proposed check does not hold. Its one 'safe' case (Marketplace ALLOW_NONE means add-ons are off) is contradicted in direction by Google's Marketplace article, which says service-specific settings override the Marketplace installation setting; so a tenant with the Marketplace closed can still have the Docs add-ons box on, and the check could show Met wrongly. Its other branches either sent nothing (ALLOW_ALL, the documented default for non-school tenants) or a 'partly' that the question does not support. The actual Add-ons box is in no API I could find. Changed from new_core_check (L, new connector) to person_only (S, typed answer with the proposed expiry).

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Apps - Gmail

**A090. Do you ensure users cannot delegate access to their mailbox**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies?filter=setting.type.matches('^settings/gmail\\..*$') (read-only, 50 per page, max 100, paged) -> setting type gmail.mail_delegation, field enable_mail_delegation (console caption: Let users delegate access to their mailbox to other users in the domain; User Settings > Mail Delegation). Proposed key, does not exist yet (grep of hud/src and services/api: zero hits): google_gmail_mail_delegation. A.C.O.R.N side: a new vendor + source row in infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES, a client mapping under that vendor, and a CHECK_ANSWERS row; no endpoint change needed because assessment_answers already reads every TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES row.
- Rule: Polarity: the template wants delegation OFF. The API returns only policies where a value was explicitly set, each tied to an org unit or group with a sortOrder; resolve per field (highest sortOrder that carries the field wins), not just 'root plus overrides'. met = enable_mail_delegation is false in every policy that carries it, OR no policy carries it while the same call returned other gmail.* policies (Google documents the default as false); evidence must then say 'left at Google's default (off)'. partly = off for the root org unit but true in a policy for some org unit or group. not_met = true for the root org unit. Call failed or returned no gmail policy at all = send nothing. Default-from-absence is documented but NOT tested on a live tenant. Non-Google clients get no check and stay needs_person.
- Needs: Decision to build a Google Workspace connector (CORE has none; its Google sign-in is one mailbox with Gmail + Calendar scopes). Per Google client: a service account with domain-wide delegation authorised in that client's Admin console for exactly https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, Cloud Identity API enabled on the project, a SUPER ADMINISTRATOR in each client to impersonate (Google: only a super administrator can use this API), and a mapping of each Google customer to its A.C.O.R.N client. Telling which org unit is the root and naming org units/groups in evidence probably needs one more read-only Directory scope: UNCONFIRMED.
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, read-only scope, setting type and field name all confirmed in Google's docs; category and effort hold. Three fixes. (1) Google documents a default of false for enable_mail_delegation and says the API only returns explicitly set values, so 'no row = send nothing' would leave most clients unanswered; absent now means met, guarded by the call having returned other Gmail policies. (2) The impersonated user must be a super administrator, which the proposal did not say. (3) Resolution is per field by sortOrder across org-unit and group policies, and knowing the root org unit may need an extra scope (unconfirmed).

**A102. Do you ensure protection against any unauthenticated emails is enabled**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies?filter=setting.type.matches('^settings/gmail\\..*$') (read-only list, scope cloud-identity.policies.readonly). Setting gmail.spoofing_and_authentication, fields detect_unauthenticated_emails (boolean) and unauthenticated_email_consequence (WARNING / SPAM_FOLDER / QUARANTINE / NO_ACTION). New key (does not exist in hud/src or A.C.O.R.N today): gws_gmail_unauthenticated_email, under a new source that must be added to infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES.
- Rule: Yes is the good answer. The API returns the explicit ADMIN and SYSTEM policies, not the effective value, so CORE must first reduce them: for each target (org unit or group) the policy with the highest policyQuery.sortOrder wins (this setting uses the Max reducer). Then: met = effective value at the top org unit is detect_unauthenticated_emails true with a consequence other than NO_ACTION and no sub org unit or group overrides it off; partly = on at the top but off or NO_ACTION in a sub org unit or group; not_met = false (or NO_ACTION) at the top. If no policy carries the field, do NOT send the check: Google's default-values table has no entry for this field, so the default is UNCONFIRMED. The existing dmarc / threatmate_dmarc / liongard_email_domain_records keys are about DNS records and do not answer this.
- Needs: Decision to build a Google Workspace admin connection (he put the Google service-account route on hold on 2026-11-04). Needs: a Google Cloud project with the Cloud Identity API on; a service account whose client id is authorised for domain-wide delegation in EACH client's Google Admin console for exactly https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly; a SUPER ADMIN user in each client domain to impersonate (Google: only a super administrator can use the Policy API); where the service-account key is kept on the CORE VM; and a client mapping (alias) for the new vendor so A.C.O.R.N stores the rows. One connection serves all ten questions in this batch.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category, endpoint, setting and field names hold (re-read in Google's docs; no cloudidentity / admin.directory string and no gws_ key exists in hud/src; the question has no CHECK_ANSWERS row). Three fixes. (1) The rule 'true on every policy returned' is wrong: the API returns explicit admin and system policies that must be reduced by sortOrder, otherwise a lower-priority system policy would wrongly break 'met'. (2) The admin role the proposer could not find is stated: the impersonated user must be a super administrator. (3) The default table was read: it has no row for detect_unauthenticated_emails, so 'absent' must mean no check sent. Also added: the new vendor needs a client alias mapping or apply_m365_checks drops the report. Whether ThreatMate exposes Gmail settings stays unconfirmed (its spec was too large to read through).

Corrected the rule to work on reduced (effective) values per target, noted that imap_access uses the Merge reducer (per-field), added the super-admin requirement, and stated that the default table has no pop_access or imap_access row, so 'absent' cannot be judged.
