Setting gmail.spam_override_lists, list spam_override[], sub-fields bypass_internal_senders (boolean) and rule_id; optionally gmail.rule_states (rule_states[].rule_id, rule_states[].enabled) to tell a switched-off rule. Both use the MaxMap reducer (entries with the same key are combined, not listed twice); Google's page does not name the key field. New key: gws_gmail_spam_internal_bypass.
- Rule: Polarity: a bypass is the bad state. not_met = any spam_override entry in the top org unit's (or any sub org unit's / group's) policies has bypass_internal_senders true and is not shown as disabled; partly = the only entries with the bypass are marked enabled=false in gmail.rule_states (the link between rule_id here and rule_states is read from the field names only: UNCONFIRMED, Google's page gives no description of rule_states); met = spam_override entries exist and none has the bypass. If gmail.spam_override_lists is absent from the response, treating that as 'no bypass' is UNCONFIRMED (no default is documented): send nothing until one live tenant with no custom spam rule has been read. Evidence names the org unit and the rule description.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connection as A102 (service account with domain-wide delegation per client, read-only policies scope, a super admin to impersonate in each client domain), plus one tenant whose spam settings are known, to prove the absent case and the rule_states link.
- Reviewer (corrected): spam_override[].bypass_internal_senders and rule_id confirmed. The proposer counted 'no spam override rules at all' as met; that is not supported by anything I could read (no default row), so the absent case now sends no check. Added the MaxMap reducer note, the super-admin requirement and a known test tenant to needs.

**A110. Do you ensure comprehensive mail storage is enabled**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 23; large; confidence low).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: Same read as A102: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (gmail filter). Setting gmail.comprehensive_mail_storage (only field: rule_id; Max reducer) joined to gmail.rule_states (rule_states[].rule_id, rule_states[].enabled; MaxMap reducer). New key: gws_gmail_comprehensive_storage.
- Rule: Yes is good. UNCONFIRMED rule: met = after reduction a gmail.comprehensive_mail_storage policy applies at the top org unit and its rule_id is enabled in gmail.rule_states; partly = on at the top but missing or disabled in a sub org unit; not_met = its rule is marked disabled. Google's page lists only rule_id for this setting, gives no description of rule_states and no default, so (a) whether 'policy present' alone means on, and (b) whether 'no policy' means off, are both unknown. Until one tenant with the setting on and one with it off have been read, send the check only when a rule_states entry for that rule_id is found; otherwise send nothing and leave the question to a typed answer.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connection as A102 (service account with domain-wide delegation per client, read-only policies scope, a super admin to impersonate in each client domain), plus a test tenant where this setting's state is known so the rule can be proven.
- Reviewer (corrected): The setting and its single rule_id field are confirmed, and the proposer's low confidence is right. Tightened the rule so 'no such policy' is no longer counted as not_met (nothing documents that), noted the reducers, and added the super-admin requirement. Category kept because the setting is listed as supported by the API; if the live read shows it cannot tell on from off, this falls back to a typed answer.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Apps - Marketplace

**A111. Do you ensure users access to Google Workspace Marketplace apps is restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence high).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no CORE connection today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies with filter setting.type.matches('^settings/workspace_marketplace\\..*$') (or one unfiltered read shared with the Gmail checks). Setting workspace_marketplace.apps_access_options, field access_level (ALLOW_ALL / ALLOW_LISTED_APPS / ALLOW_NONE), Merge reducer; the default table also shows a field allow_all_internal_apps. Optional detail: workspace_marketplace.apps_allowlist (apps[].application_id, apps[].access ALLOWED / BLOCKED). New key: gws_marketplace_restricted.
- Rule: Yes (restricted) is good. After reducing policies by sortOrder (Merge: per field): met = access_level is ALLOW_LISTED_APPS or ALLOW_NONE at the top org unit and no sub org unit / group is ALLOW_ALL; partly = restricted at the top but ALLOW_ALL in a sub org unit; not_met = ALLOW_ALL at the top. If no policy sets the field, Google's documented default is ALLOW_ALL (ALLOW_NONE for K-12 customers), so for a non-education client that case is not_met with evidence saying 'Google default, no admin policy set'; CORE cannot tell K-12 from this read, so for an education client send nothing. The existing key threatmate_google_apps counts third-party OAuth apps already granted Gmail or full-Drive access (aisoc-threatmate-google.ts, .../saas-app); it says what got in, not whether Marketplace installs are restricted, and must not be mapped as the answer.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connection as A102 (service account with domain-wide delegation per client, read-only policies scope, a super admin to impersonate in each client domain).
- Reviewer (corrected): access_level and its three values confirmed; the refusal to map threatmate_google_apps is right (read in aisoc-threatmate-google.ts: it scores granted OAuth scopes). Fixes: the proposer's example filter only matches gmail settings, so this setting needs the marketplace filter or an unfiltered read; the absent case is settled from Google's default table (ALLOW_ALL, K-12 ALLOW_NONE); rule changed to reduced values; super-admin requirement added.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Security - Accounts

**A112. Do you ensure 2-Step Verification (Multi-Factor Authentication) is enforced for all users**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 2; small; confidence high).
- Source: ThreatMate Google Workspace integration (already swept by CORE every 12 hours), stored in A.C.O.R.N as Multi-Factor people
- How: identity_mfa_users.per_user for rows with source = "threatmate" (mfa_registration.GOOGLE_SOURCES). CORE's googleMfaItem() sets per_user = "enforced" when ThreatMate's isEnforced2FA is true, else null; the ingest (identity.py apply_mfa_users) keeps per_user, enabled and refreshed_at. The review GET already loads every IdentityMfaUser row for the client, but assessment_answers() takes only checks, so the Google rows must be passed in as a second argument.
- Rule: New pure rule in assessment_review.py over the client's Google rows only (source in GOOGLE_SOURCES, enabled = true; suspended people arrive as enabled = false and deleted ones are not sent). met = every such person has per_user == "enforced". partly = some but not all (evidence: "2-Step Verification is enforced for N of M Google accounts", plus how many of the rest are at least enrolled). not_met = nobody is enforced. No Google rows = needs_person, never met on an empty list. Ignore rows whose refreshed_at is older than about a week (rows are only removed by a later snapshot, so a disconnected workspace would otherwise answer forever). Polarity normal. Do not reuse the readiness MFA band (it counts enrolment) and do not map threatmate_google_admins or threatmate_cloud_identity (super admins only / enrolment only). Not verified: whether a new user inside Google's enrolment grace period reports as not enforced (would show as partly); ThreatMate's user row has no 'archived' flag, so archived accounts may be counted. The CIS control behind this question (1.3.0, 4.1.1.3) also asks for a 2-week enrolment period, no trusted devices and no text/phone codes; the question as worded only asks about enforcement, so those are left out.

**A113. Do you ensure Super Admin account recovery is enabled**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 23; large; confidence low).
- Source: Google Workspace: Cloud Identity Policy API (no Google Workspace admin connection exists in CORE today)
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies (filter with setting.type.matches(...)), setting type security.super_admin_account_recovery, field enableAccountRecovery (boolean). Read-only: scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and Google's settings table marks every security.* setting 'Mutate supported: No'. Google's documented default when the field is absent is false. CORE has no cloudidentity or admin.directory call (grep: zero hits; its only Google scopes are Gmail and Calendar for one mailbox).
- Rule: BLOCKED on a polarity decision. The template says 'enabled', but the current CIS Google Workspace benchmark (1.3.0, control 4.1.2.1, read from Prowler's public mapping) is titled 'Ensure Super Admin account recovery is disabled', and Google's default is off. Mapping met = enabled, as proposed, would mark a correctly hardened client 'Not met'. Suggested: new check google_super_admin_recovery with met = enableAccountRecovery false at the root organisational unit (current CIS), not_met = true, and an evidence sentence saying the template's wording comes from an older revision; or, if the owner wants the template followed literally, the reverse. partly is not used. When the list call succeeds but returns no policy for this setting, use Google's documented default (off) and say so in the evidence; when the call fails, send nothing. The new source must be added to infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES plus one CHECK_ANSWERS line. UNCONFIRMED live: nothing was called, and which older CIS revision the template was copied from was not checked.
- Needs: 1) Decide the polarity: follow the template's word 'enabled' or the current CIS 1.3.0 guidance 'disabled'. 2) A decision to add a Google Workspace admin connector: one Google Cloud project with the Cloud Identity API enabled and a service account; in EACH client's Google Admin console, domain-wide delegation authorised for that service account with the read-only scope cloud-identity.policies.readonly; and per client the address of a SUPER ADMINISTRATOR to act as (Prowler's documentation for the same API says the Policy API returns data only to super admins; Google's own setup page names no role), plus a client mapping in A.C.O.R.N. Not verified: whether ThreatMate could supply this instead.
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, field and read-only scope confirmed in Google's public documentation. Three fixes. (1) Polarity: the proposal made 'enabled' the good answer; the current CIS benchmark 1.3.0 control 4.1.2.1 says DISABLED, so this now needs an owner decision and confidence drops to low. (2) 'No entry returned = send nothing' was wrong: Google documents a default (false) for a missing field, so a successful read with no entry means off. (3) The account to act as must be a super administrator (per Prowler's documentation), which the proposal left as unknown.

**A114. Do you ensure User account recovery is enabled**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace: Cloud Identity Policy API (no Google Workspace admin connection exists in CORE today)
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies, setting type security.user_account_recovery, field enableAccountRecovery (boolean); read-only scope cloud-identity.policies.readonly; 'Mutate supported: No'. Same single list call as A113 and A116. Google's documented default when the field is absent is false.
- Rule: New check google_user_recovery in the new Google source, plus one CHECK_ANSWERS line. Polarity normal and matches the current CIS benchmark (1.3.0, 4.1.2.2 'Ensure User account recovery is enabled'). met = enableAccountRecovery true at the root organisational unit and no unit or group policy turning it off; partly = on at the root but off for one or more units or groups (name them); not_met = false at the root. When the list call succeeds but has no policy for this setting, treat it as Google's documented default (off) = not_met, and say 'never changed from Google's default' in the evidence; when the call fails or returns no policies at all, send nothing. UNCONFIRMED live: how unit and group overrides appear (policyQuery, sortOrder) was read in the docs only.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A113: service account with domain-wide delegation authorised in each client's Google Admin console for cloud-identity.policies.readonly, a super administrator address to act as per client, and the client mapping. No extra scope beyond A113.
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, field, scope and polarity confirmed. Changed the missing-entry rule: Google documents the default as off, so a successful read with no entry is not_met, not 'send nothing' (otherwise most clients, who never touched the setting, would get no answer). Added that the account to act as must be a super administrator.

**A115. Do you ensure login challenges are enforced**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer on the Client review page (client_review_answers, section assess). A technician reads it in the client's Google Admin console: Security > Authentication > Login challenges. No API holds the main setting: the Cloud Identity Policy API's security.login_challenges has only enableEmployeeIdChallenge, and Prowler's CIS 1.3.0 mapping lists no automatic check for this control (4.1.4.1).
- Rule: Per CIS 1.3.0 control 4.1.4.1 the answer is met when, under Post-SSO verification, 'Logins using SSO are subject to additional verifications (if appropriate) and 2-Step Verification (if configured)' is selected AND 'Use employee ID to keep my users more secure' is unchecked; not_met when post-SSO verification is left at 'bypass additional verifications'. A person types it; suggest the typed answer is re-asked after 12 months (typed answers have no expiry today, so that would be a small new feature). If the Google connector is built for A113/A114/A116 anyway, CORE could add a hint to the evidence only (employee ID challenge on or off; whether any SAML or OIDC sign-in assignment exists), without setting a status.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The automatic route does not hold. The setting the question is about (Post-SSO verification) is not in the Policy API settings list, and the proposed work-around (met when no single sign-on is assigned) cannot be established reliably: the sign-in assignment's usual mode DOMAIN_WIDE_SAML_IF_ENABLED means 'use the domain-wide provider if one is configured', and the API page does not say whether one is. The proposal also had the one readable field backwards as a side note: CIS wants the employee ID challenge OFF. The proposer's recollection of the CIS control was right and is now confirmed from Prowler's public copy. Changed to a typed answer; effort drops from L to S.

**A116. Ensure password policy is configured for enhanced security**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace: Cloud Identity Policy API (no Google Workspace admin connection exists in CORE today)
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies, setting type security.password, fields allowedStrength (STRONG / WEAK), minimumLength, maximumLength, allowReuse, enforceRequirementsAtLogin, expirationDuration (seconds, 0 = never); read-only scope cloud-identity.policies.readonly; 'Mutate supported: No'. Documented defaults for absent fields: STRONG, 8, 100, reuse false, enforce at sign-in false, expiry 0. A.C.O.R.N holds nothing for Google passwords today.
- Rule: New check google_password_policy in the new Google source, plus one CHECK_ANSWERS line. Thresholds from CIS 1.3.0 control 4.1.5.1: strong passwords enforced, minimum length 14 or more, policy enforced at next sign-in, reuse not allowed, reset frequency 365 days. Suggested: met = allowedStrength STRONG and minimumLength >= 14 and enforceRequirementsAtLogin true and allowReuse false (and expirationDuration = 365 days if the owner wants CIS followed to the letter); not_met = allowedStrength WEAK, or minimumLength 8 or less (Google's default); partly = anything between, naming what is short (for example 'minimum length is 12; not enforced at next sign-in'). Fill absent fields with Google's documented defaults, so an untouched tenant reads as not_met (length 8, not enforced at sign-in) rather than no answer; send nothing only when the call fails. Read the root organisational unit and report a unit with a weaker policy as partly. Put the actual numbers in the evidence, since the review has no value field. UNCONFIRMED live.
- Needs: Same Google Workspace connector as A113 (service account, domain-wide delegation per client for cloud-identity.policies.readonly, a super administrator address per client, client mapping). Plus a decision on thresholds: accept CIS's 14 characters, and whether the 365-day forced reset counts toward 'met' (CIS asks for it; current NIST guidance advises against forced expiry).
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint and all six fields confirmed in Google's documentation. The proposed minimum length of 12 was a guess; CIS 1.3.0 (4.1.5.1) says 14 or more and also asks for a 365-day reset, which the proposal left out of the verdict, so that is now an explicit owner decision. Added Google's documented defaults for absent fields so an untouched tenant gets an answer, and that the account to act as must be a super administrator.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Security - Access and Data Control

**A117. Do you ensure application access to Google services is restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 23; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no Google admin connection exists in CORE today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies with filter setting.type.matches('^settings/api_controls\\..*$'). Primary fact: setting api_controls.google_services, services[] each {scopes_group (DRIVE_ALL, DRIVE_HIGH_RISK, GMAIL_ALL, GMAIL_HIGH_RISK, CALENDAR_ALL, CONTACTS_ALL, GSUITE_ADMIN_ALL, ...), is_enabled}. Secondary: api_controls.unconfigured_third_party_apps.access_level (enum ACCESS_LEVEL_UNSPECIFIED, BLOCK_ALL, ALLOW_SIGN_IN_ONLY). New key google_api_access_restricted, sent by a new vendor whose source is added to infra_review.TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES, plus one CHECK_ANSWERS line.
- Rule: Polarity normal (met = restricted). The control behind this wording (CIS Google Workspace 4.2.1.1) is Admin console > Security > API controls > Manage Google Services set to Restricted, so judge on api_controls.google_services, not on the unconfigured-apps setting. met: every listed service (at least the Drive and Gmail groups) is restricted. partly: some restricted (or only the high-risk groups). not_met: none restricted. Evidence may add the unconfigured-apps level (BLOCK_ALL / ALLOW_SIGN_IN_ONLY = good). UNCONFIRMED: which boolean value of is_enabled means restricted (the doc labels it only 'Restricted/Unrestricted'), and how 'allow any app' is returned (there is no ALLOW_ALL value; probably ACCESS_LEVEL_UNSPECIFIED or the policy absent). Both must be read on a live response before coding. threatmate_google_apps is not an honest mapping (it counts apps already holding Gmail / full-Drive scopes; it says nothing about restriction).
- Needs: Decision to build a Google Workspace admin connector. Per client: a SUPER ADMINISTRATOR must authorise it (Google: only a super administrator can use the Cloud Identity Policy API), e.g. a service account with domain-wide delegation impersonating a super admin, read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly. Also a client mapping for the new vendor (Google customer id to A.C.O.R.N client).
- Reviewer (corrected): Endpoint, filter syntax, read-only scope and field names confirmed in Google's docs; no cloudidentity / admin.directory / admin.reports string exists in CORE (grep, zero hits), so the connector really is new. Fixed: (1) the rule was built on the wrong setting. The CIS control with this exact title is 'Manage Google Services > Restricted', i.e. api_controls.google_services; the unconfigured-third-party-apps level is secondary. (2) access_level enum values are now read: BLOCK_ALL, ALLOW_SIGN_IN_ONLY, ACCESS_LEVEL_UNSPECIFIED; there is no allow-all value. (3) The 'NOT VERIFIED admin privilege' is now known: super administrator only, which makes the per-client ask heavier. (4) A new vendor also needs client mapping, since a report is only stored for a mapped vendor_client_id and a source may only be named by its own vendor.

**A118. Review third-party applications periodically**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none for the review itself; ThreatMate Google (existing check threatmate_google_apps) supplies a list to review
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess', keyed by the question text 'Review third-party applications periodically'), with the review date in the note. Expiry computed from ClientReviewAnswer.updated_at (new code). Supporting sentence from the existing check threatmate_google_apps (source threatmate-google, already in tool_checks passed to assessment_answers).
- Rule: The question asks whether a person reviews the app list; no system records that. Status stays needs_person until a person types met; the typed answer falls back to needs_person after the agreed interval. A.C.O.R.N shows the threatmate_google_apps evidence as a prompt only ('N of M third-party apps can read mail or every Drive file'). Never map that check's status to this question: met there means no app holds mail / full-Drive scopes, not that a review happened. Limits verified in code: the check is absent when ThreatMate's app list is empty or unread; its items list only the risky apps (mail or full Drive), up to 40, and is empty when the status is met, so 'reopen when a new app joins' can only fire for a new risky app, not for any new app. Typed answers have no expiry today (review_overrides.py), so expiry is new code.
- Needs: Agree the review interval. The CIS control behind this wording says weekly review (secondary source); 12 months is far looser, so pick deliberately (for example quarterly). Decide whether a new risky app should reopen the question.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds: threatmate_google_apps exists (aisoc-threatmate-google.ts appsCheck), measures risky scopes only, and threatmate-google is in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES so the endpoint already passes it to assessment_answers. Fixed details: the typed-answer model is in models/client_review_answer.py (not signin.py) and already has updated_at, so expiry needs no migration; the check's items contain only risky apps, so the proposed 'new app joins' trigger is narrower than stated; the CIS interval is weekly, not yearly. Effort S covers expiry plus the prompt line; the reopen-on-new-item trigger is extra (a query on client_check_item_history in the endpoint, closer to M).

**A119. Do you review domain-wide delegation for applications periodically**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence high).
- Source: none today; a future Google connection could add change events (Admin SDK Reports API), not the review itself
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess') with the review date in the note; expiry from updated_at (new code, shared with A118). Optional later aid only if a Google connector is built: GET https://admin.googleapis.com/admin/reports/v1/activity/users/all/applications/admin with eventName AUTHORIZE_API_CLIENT_ACCESS (parameters API_CLIENT_NAME, API_SCOPES, DOMAIN_NAME) and REMOVE_API_CLIENT_ACCESS (API_CLIENT_NAME, DOMAIN_NAME).
- Rule: The question asks whether a person reviews the delegation list; no system holds that. needs_person until someone types met; expire after the agreed interval. No documented API lists the CURRENT domain-wide delegation entries (none found in the Policy API settings page; the Prowler CIS mapping has no automated check for this control either), so a complete automatic list is not available. The audit events only show grants and removals inside the audit log's retention window (length UNCONFIRMED this session), and the events page names them 'API client access', not 'domain-wide delegation' by name (UNCONFIRMED that they cover exactly the delegation list). threatmate_google_apps is user-granted OAuth apps, not domain-wide delegation, so it does not answer this. A CORE Google connector using a delegated service account would itself be an entry on each client's list.
- Needs: Agree the review interval (the CIS control says weekly; 12 months is much looser). Only if the optional aid is wanted: the Google Workspace connector decision plus read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/admin.reports.audit.readonly.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category confirmed. Both audit events exist on Google's page. Fixed: REMOVE_API_CLIENT_ACCESS carries only API_CLIENT_NAME and DOMAIN_NAME (no API_SCOPES, as the proposal implied for both); the page does not use the words 'domain-wide delegation', so that link is an inference; the CIS interval is weekly. Confidence raised to high for the category because a second source (the Prowler CIS mapping) also has no automated check.

**A120. Do you ensure blocking access from unapproved geographic locations**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess', question 'Do you ensure blocking access from unapproved geographic locations'), refreshed on the shared expiry (new code, as A118). No automatic check proposed.
- Rule: Polarity normal (met = blocked). met: a person confirms a Context-Aware Access level with a region condition is assigned to the apps in Active mode. not_met: no such assignment, or the client's Google edition has no Context-Aware Access (Google lists Frontline Standard, Enterprise Standard/Plus, Education Standard/Plus, Enterprise Essentials Plus and Cloud Identity Premium; Business editions are not listed, so most small clients are not_met by licence). Why not automatic: the only documented read is Access Context Manager accessLevels.list (GET https://accesscontextmanager.googleapis.com/v1/{parent=accessPolicies/*}/accessLevels, scope cloud-platform, IAM permission accesscontextmanager.accessLevels.list), which shows a region level EXISTS but not that it is assigned; assignment is visible only as audit events (TOGGLE_CAA_ENABLEMENT, CHANGE_CAA_APP_ASSIGNMENTS with MODE ACTIVE/MONITOR) inside the audit retention window, so a steady-state tenant could never be shown as met. UNCONFIRMED whether a delegated Workspace admin holds that Cloud IAM permission at all.
- Needs: Nothing to start (typed answer). If an automatic aid is ever wanted: the Google connector decision, plus Google Cloud organisation access per client (cloud-platform scope and an Access Context Manager read permission), which is more than a Workspace delegation.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The automatic route does not hold. I confirmed the Access Context Manager list call (path, cloud-platform scope, IAM permission) and both audit events, but together they cannot produce an honest 'met': the API shows that a region access level exists, not that it is assigned to any app, and the audit log only shows assignment changes within its retention. The proposal itself admitted the check 'will often top out at partly and a person confirms'. It also needs Google Cloud IAM on each client's organisation on top of an L connector. The Prowler CIS mapping likewise has no automated check for this control. Edition list is now verified (Business editions lack the feature), which lets a person answer not_met quickly for most clients. Changed to person_only, effort S.

**A121. Do you ensure DLP policies for Google Drive are configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 23; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no Google admin connection exists in CORE today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies filtered to setting type rule.dlp. Fields: display_name, triggers[] (Drive trigger is google.workspace.drive.file.v1.share), action (Drive: driveAction with blockAccess, warnUser, auditOnly, restrictCopyPrintDownload, applyLabels), state (ACTIVE / INACTIVE), condition, rule_type_metadata. New key google_drive_dlp under the new Google source in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES plus a CHECK_ANSWERS line (full text 'Do you ensure DLP policies for Google Drive are configured').
- Rule: Polarity normal. met: at least one rule.dlp with state ACTIVE, triggers containing google.workspace.drive.file.v1.share, and a driveAction stronger than auditOnly (blockAccess, warnUser or restrictCopyPrintDownload). partly: Drive DLP rules exist but all are INACTIVE or auditOnly / applyLabels only (same shape as dlp_enabled for Microsoft 365). not_met: no rule with the Drive trigger. Evidence names the count and the strongest action. UNCONFIRMED: the exact filter string for this setting type (whether it is 'settings/rule.dlp' or another prefix) and that the v1 list call returns rule.dlp (the page marks it as mutable in v1beta1); the editions that include Drive DLP were not verified, so a tenant without the feature reads as no rules = not_met and the evidence should say that it may be a licence limit.
- Needs: Google Workspace connector decision; per client a super administrator's authorisation (only a super administrator can use the Policy API), read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and client mapping for the new vendor.
- Reviewer (corrected): Route holds: rule.dlp is on Google's supported-settings page and an open-source scanner maps an automated check to this same CIS control. Filled in what the proposal had not read: the Drive trigger string (google.workspace.drive.file.v1.share), the Drive action names (blockAccess, warnUser, auditOnly, restrictCopyPrintDownload, applyLabels) and the state values (ACTIVE / INACTIVE). Added the super-administrator requirement and the client-mapping need. The question text is unique in assessment_questions.py, so the CHECK_ANSWERS line is safe.

**A122. Do you ensure Google session control is configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 9; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace (Cloud Identity Policy API) - no Google admin connection exists in CORE today
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies with filter setting.type.matches('^settings/security\\.session_controls$'); field webSessionDuration (seconds), per org unit. New key google_session_control under the new Google source plus a CHECK_ANSWERS line using the full text 'Do you ensure Google session control is configured'.
- Rule: Polarity normal. met: webSessionDuration is 12 hours (43,200 seconds) or less in every org unit. partly: the root org unit meets it but another org unit is longer. not_met: longer than 12 hours, or the setting is not returned (Google says a missing setting means its default applies; the default value and how 'never expires' is represented were NOT read, so treat absent as not_met only after checking a live response). Evidence states the duration in hours. The 12-hour figure is the CIS Google Workspace 4.2.4.1 threshold, read from a secondary source (an open-source scanner's copy of the benchmark).
- Needs: Google Workspace connector decision; per client a super administrator's authorisation (only a super administrator can use the Policy API), read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly, and client mapping for the new vendor. Confirm 12 hours as the threshold.
- Reviewer (corrected): Setting type security.session_controls and field webSessionDuration (seconds) confirmed. The threshold the proposal left open is 12 hours or less (CIS 4.2.4.1, secondary source), not '12 or 24'. Added the super-administrator requirement, client mapping, and the missing-setting-means-default caveat. Prefix claim checked in code: _question() tries an exact match first and neither session-control question is a prefix of the other, so the full text is safe. Note the audit log also has CHANGE_SESSION_LENGTH (OLD_VALUE / NEW_VALUE) as supporting history.

**A123. Do you ensure Google Cloud session control is configured**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none (no documented read of the current setting); change events only via Admin SDK Reports API if a Google connector existed
- How: Typed answer in client_review_answers (section 'assess'), on the shared expiry (new code); 'not_applicable' (already an allowed typed status for assess) for clients that do not use Google Cloud. Possible later aid: Reports API admin event SESSION_CONTROL_SETTINGS_CHANGE with parameters REAUTH_APPLICATION (ADMIN_CONSOLE, CLOUD_ADMIN_TOOLS), REAUTH_SETTING_OLD / REAUTH_SETTING_NEW, ORG_UNIT_NAME.
- Rule: Polarity normal. met: a person confirms reauthentication is required for Google Cloud tools (the CIS control suggests every 16 hours with a security key; secondary source). not_met: set to never require it. not_applicable: the client has no Google Cloud use. No setting type for this exists on the Policy API's supported-settings page (only security.session_controls, the web session of A122), and the open-source CIS mapping has no automated check for it, so the current value cannot be read from a documented API. The audit event shows that the setting was changed and when, inside audit retention, which supports but does not prove the current state. Typed-answer expiry does not exist today (new code shared with A118 / A119).
- Needs: Nothing to start (typed answer). For the optional aid: the Google Workspace connector decision and read-only scope https://www.googleapis.com/auth/admin.reports.audit.readonly.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds; I looked for a setting type containing 'cloud_session' or 'reauth' on the Policy API page and found none. Details fixed: the audit event's parameters are now read (REAUTH_APPLICATION with ADMIN_CONSOLE and CLOUD_ADMIN_TOOLS), which confirms it is the right event; its description on the page is 'change in session control settings', not the sentence the proposal quoted. Added the CIS target (16 hours, security key) so a person knows what 'configured' means. Confidence raised from low to medium because a second source also treats this control as manual only. Strictly this is a setting a system holds with no read API, not a judgement, but a typed answer is still the cheapest honest route.

**A124. Do you ensure less secure app access is disabled**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 2; small; confidence medium).
- Source: A.C.O.R.N's own knowledge that the client has a Google Workspace (threatmate-google check row), plus a fixed platform fact
- How: New pure rule in assessment_review.py: when the checks passed to assessment_answers contain any key starting 'threatmate_google_' (source threatmate-google, already in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES and already passed in), answer 'Do you ensure less secure app access is disabled' as met with a fixed evidence sentence. No CORE change and no Google connector.
- Rule: Polarity: the question is worded so that met = disabled. met for every client known to have a Google Workspace, with evidence such as 'Google ended less secure app sign-in for all Workspace accounts on 1 May 2025 and removed the setting from the Admin console.' Clients with no threatmate-google row stay needs_person (a person types met, or not_applicable if they do not use Google). Do not report not_met from this rule. Caveats: app passwords still work and are a separate thing this question does not ask about; the Policy API still lists security.less_secure_apps.allowLessSecureApps, but Google's admin help says accounts no longer support less secure apps, so the flag no longer changes the answer. The review does not check how old a tool row is, so a client whose Google Workspace was disconnected from ThreatMate keeps the answer until the row is removed.
- Needs: Agree that a fixed 'met, Google retired this' answer is acceptable for this template question, and that the signal for 'client uses Google Workspace' is the ThreatMate Google connection.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category changed to a cheaper one. The proposed API read is real (security.less_secure_apps, allowLessSecureApps boolean, confirmed), but the thing the proposal left NOT VERIFIED settles the question: Google's admin help page states 'Starting May 1, 2025, Google Workspace accounts no longer support less secure apps' and that the enforcement setting is no longer in the Admin console. So an L connector with per-client super-admin consent is not needed for this item; a small rule in A.C.O.R.N keyed on the client having a Google Workspace gives the honest answer. Confidence is medium only because the 'has Google Workspace' signal covers just clients connected in ThreatMate, and the owner must accept a fixed answer. If the Google connector is built anyway for A117 / A121 / A122, reading allowLessSecureApps there is an optional extra, not a requirement.

### Assessment: Google Workspace / Rules

**A125. Do you ensure Government-backed attacks is configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 23; large; confidence medium).
- Source: Google Workspace: Cloud Identity Policy API (CORE has no Google Workspace admin connection today)
- How: GET https://cloudidentity.googleapis.com/v1/policies with filter setting.type.matches('^settings/rule\\.system_defined_alerts$'), paged (pageSize max 100). Setting type rule.system_defined_alerts is in Google's supported-settings page, marked Mutate supported: No, with fields display_name, description, state (ACTIVE/INACTIVE), action.alertCenterAction.alertCenterConfig.status (ENABLED/DISABLED) and .severity, action.alertCenterAction.recipients. Pick the policy whose display_name is "Government-backed attacks". Proposed NEW key gws_rule_government_backed_attacks (does not exist in hud/src or services/api; grep zero hits). A.C.O.R.N side: new source in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES (infra_review.py), one CHECK_ANSWERS row with the full question text, AND a new client-mapping vendor, because checks are only stored for clients mapped through ClientAlias (targets_by_tenant).
- Rule: Yes is the good answer. met = state ACTIVE and it notifies someone (alert center ENABLED or at least one recipient). partly = ACTIVE but alert center DISABLED and no recipients. not_met = INACTIVE. Rule absent from the response = leave the check out (never not_met, never assume the default). Google's help page lists this rule Active by default. UNCONFIRMED: (1) that display_name equals the Admin console name (docs give no values); (2) whether rules an admin never edited are returned at all (the page has a 'missing from the response / default field values' note I could not read for this setting; if untouched rules are omitted, most clients get no automatic answer); (3) whether ACTIVE can coexist with both notifications off (partly may be unreachable); (4) the 'settings/' prefix for this type (taken from the list page's Gmail example).
- Needs: Decision to build a Google Workspace admin connection. Per Google's setup page: a Google Cloud project with the Cloud Identity API on, a service account, and in EACH Google client's Admin console a super admin must authorise domain-wide delegation for exactly https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-identity.policies.readonly; CORE must also hold one admin address per client to impersonate. Which admin role that address needs is not stated in the docs. CORE's existing Google sign-in cannot be reused (device flow does not allow this scope). Also decide whether 'configured' means active only, or active with a recipient. One connection serves A125 to A131.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and effort hold: I read the setting type, its fields, the GET endpoint and the read-only scope in Google's docs, and confirmed no such key or Google admin scope exists in either repo. Fixed: (a) added the risk that never-edited rules may be missing from the response, which would leave most clients unanswered; (b) the A.C.O.R.N work is more than TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES plus one row, since each Google client must also be mapped under a new vendor; (c) the owner needs a per-client admin address to impersonate, and Google's device sign-in that CORE uses today cannot request this scope (confirmed in Google's allowed-scopes list); (d) the partly branch may never fire. Until built, the honest fallback is a typed answer.

### Microsoft 365: M365 Admin Center

**M01. Ensure `third-party storage services` are restricted in `Microsoft 365 on the web`**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 8; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP (Microsoft Graph passthrough ListGraphRequest, already used by the M365 baseline sweep)
- How: New key m365web_third_party_storage (grep: not in hud/src or services/api today). One extra read in readM365Checks (hud/src/aisoc-m365-checks.ts): GET /api/ListGraphRequest?tenantFilter=<tenant>&Endpoint=servicePrincipals&$filter=appId eq 'c1f33bc0-bdb4-4248-ba9b-096807ddb43e'&$select=appId,accountEnabled, with $filter and $select as SEPARATE query parameters (the pattern in hud/src/aisoc-defender.ts line 164), NOT packed into Endpoint through the existing graph() helper. Not the existing owa_storage_providers key (that reads Get-OwaMailboxPolicy and is mapped to the Outlook-on-the-web question). Do not use CIPP ListTests CIS_1_3_7 as the source (see rule).
- Rule: Polarity: restricted is the good answer. Service principal found with accountEnabled=false -> met. Found with accountEnabled=true -> not_met. Read succeeded with an empty list (principal absent) -> not_met, evidence 'the Microsoft 365 on the web service principal is not present, which is the default (allowed) state'. Read failed (CIPP answers 400 on a Graph error, so the helper returns null) -> send nothing. Reason for absent = not_met: the CIS remediation script creates the principal when missing and then disables it, a Tenable copy of the benchmark gives the default as enabled, and Maester's CIS test fails when the principal is not found. CIPP's own test CIS_1_3_7 scores absent as Passed, so ListTests would give a false met for untouched tenants. A.C.O.R.N side: CHECK_QUESTIONS line, CORE_CHECK_KEYS and the count 72 in tests/test_m365_review.py, a `key` line in docs/M365_CHECKS.md. Shared blocker for this batch: M365ChecksReport.checks has max_length=80 (endpoints/client_review.py line 193), the baseline can send 72, CORE does not trim, and the 11 new keys make 83; an over-length report is refused whole (422), so the A.C.O.R.N cap must be raised and deployed BEFORE CORE sends any new key. Not run live.
- Reviewer (corrected): Two fixes. (1) The request as written would not filter: readM365Checks' graph() helper URL-encodes the whole path into Endpoint, and CIPP's Get-GraphRequestList rebuilds the query string only from the separate $filter/$select parameters (it overwrites the URI query, line 162 of the current source), so a '?$filter=' inside Endpoint is dropped and CIPP would page every service principal in the tenant. Pass $filter and $select as their own query parameters. (2) The 'absent' case no longer needs an owner decision: Maester's CIS test and the benchmark's default both treat absent as not restricted, so absent = not_met; CIPP's ListTests row (absent = Passed) is ruled out as a source. Rest verified: key does not exist, owa_storage_providers is a different question, the 80 cap and the 72 count are real.

**M02. Ensure 'User owned apps and services' is restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 8; small; confidence high).
- Source: CIPP (Microsoft Graph beta passthrough ListGraphRequest)
- How: New key user_owned_apps (not in either repo today). One extra read in readM365Checks through the existing graph() helper: Endpoint=admin/appsAndServices (no query needed; ListGraphRequest defaults to Graph beta and to the delegated token, which is what CIPP's own nightly cache uses for this same path). Fields isOfficeStoreEnabled and isAppAndServicesTrialEnabled, nested under `settings` (accept row.settings, row.value.settings, or the row itself). Alternative: /api/ListDBCache?type=AppsAndServices (CIPP stores the settings object flattened).
- Rule: Polarity: restricted is good. Both false -> met. Exactly one false -> partly, evidence names which is still on (CIPP's own CIS test is stricter: anything but both false fails; use not_met for one-on if you want to match it). Both true -> not_met. Either field missing or read failed -> send nothing (needs_person). A.C.O.R.N side as M01 (CHECK_QUESTIONS, CORE_CHECK_KEYS + count, docs/M365_CHECKS.md) and the shared 80-check cap, raised first. Not verified live: that each client tenant's CIPP consent includes the org-settings read (Graph permission OrgSettings-AppsAndServices.Read.All); when it does not, the read fails and the check is left out, which is safe.
- Reviewer (corrected): Route holds: Microsoft documents GET /admin/appsAndServices (beta) returning settings.isOfficeStoreEnabled and settings.isAppAndServicesTrialEnabled, readable with OrgSettings-AppsAndServices.Read.All, and CIPP reads the same path nightly. Details changed: the two fields sit under `settings` (Microsoft's sample wraps them once more in `value`), so the rule must look in all three places; effort lowered from M to S because this is one read and one pure rule, with the shared cap work counted once under M01.

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 8; small; confidence high).
- Source: CIPP (Microsoft Graph beta passthrough ListGraphRequest)
- How: New key forms_phishing_protection (not in either repo today). One extra read in readM365Checks through the existing graph() helper: Endpoint=admin/forms/settings, field isInOrgFormsPhishingScanEnabled. CIPP's FormsPhishingProtection standard and its nightly settings cache both read https://graph.microsoft.com/beta/admin/forms/settings with the same delegated token ListGraphRequest uses by default. Alternative: /api/ListDBCache?type=FormsSettings.

Not verified live: per-tenant consent for the Forms org-settings read (OrgSettings-Forms.Read.All); a refused read just leaves the check out.
- Reviewer (corrected): Mechanism and rule confirmed against Microsoft's Graph page (formsSettings.isInOrgFormsPhishingScanEnabled) and CIPP's standard, cache and CIS test. Only change: effort M to S (one read, one two-way rule; the shared cap work is counted under M01).

The control is an alert policy that fires on any sign-in by the break-glass accounts; CIPP's own CIS test CIS_2_2_1 is hard-coded 'This is a task performed manually' and its note describes a Defender alert policy. Keep it as a typed answer in client_review_answers (section m365). The existing emergency_access_accounts check can be shown beside it but does not answer it: it only says two break-glass style accounts appear to exist, is only ever 'partly', and its evidence carries a count, not the account names.
- Rule: Typed by a person: met = an alert on any sign-in by the emergency accounts exists and reaches someone; not_met = no such alert. Flag for re-confirmation 12 months after the typed answer's updated_at (review_overrides has no expiry rule today, but the rows already carry updated_at, so this is a display rule, not a schema change). An automatic route exists only if the monitoring moves somewhere CORE or A.C.O.R.N can see, for example A.C.O.R.N raising its own alert from the sign-in feed it stores (signin_events, Entra-premium tenants only) when a flagged emergency account signs in; that is a new detection plus CORE sending the account names, not a mapping, so it is not counted as automatic. Not verified: whether Defender for Cloud Apps activity policies are readable through anything CIPP exposes.

Small fix: the 12-month re-confirm needs no new column, because typed answers already carry updated_at; and the owner decision is optional (it only matters for a future automatic route), not a blocker for the typed answer.

**M05. Ensure Priority account protection is enabled and configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 8; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP (Exchange Online passthrough ListExoRequest, already used by the baseline sweep)
- How: New key priority_account_protection (not in either repo today; the existing priority_strict_preset key answers the separate 'Strict protection presets' question). Two extra reads with cippExoRead (hud/src/cipp.ts; Get- cmdlets only, which both are): Get-EmailTenantSettings -> EnablePriorityAccountProtection, and Get-User with cmdParams {IsVIP:true} -> count of accounts tagged as priority. cippExoRead already takes cmdParams, but the exo() helper inside readM365Checks passes only cmdlet and Select, so it needs a small widening. Licence gate from the subscribedSkus read the sweep already makes. CIPP has no native read: its test CIS_2_4_1 is hard-coded manual and a code search of CIPP-API finds no reference to EmailTenantSettings.
- Rule: Three-part control (on, accounts tagged, alert policies for priority accounts). Tenant has no Defender for Office 365 Plan 2 -> not_met with 'needs Defender for Office 365 Plan 2, which this tenant is not licensed for' plus a LICENSE_NOTES line, the same convention checkP2Gated uses (plan name to test: THREAT_INTELLIGENCE, from memory, UNCONFIRMED; check Microsoft's service-plan list before coding). Licensed and EnablePriorityAccountProtection=false -> not_met. On but zero priority-tagged users -> not_met (on, protecting nobody). On and at least one tagged user -> partly, evidence 'on, N priority accounts tagged; the alert policies for priority accounts are not read here, so confirm them'. Never met automatically: the alert-policy part needs Get-ProtectionAlert (CIPP caches it nightly as ListDBCache type=ExoProtectionAlert, or ListExoRequest with Compliance=true, which cippExoRead does not send), and telling whether an alert is scoped to priority accounts from that output is UNCONFIRMED. Either read missing or failed -> send nothing. UNCONFIRMED live: that Get-EmailTenantSettings is among the cmdlets CIPP's Exchange session may run, and that a switch passed as IsVIP:true works through CIPP. A.C.O.R.N side as M01, cap raised first.
- Reviewer (corrected): Confirmed from Microsoft's pages: 'Get-EmailTenantSettings / Format-List Identity,EnablePriorityAccountProtection' is the documented check (so the output property name is no longer an assumption), the feature is for Defender for Office 365 Plan 2 and is on by default there, and Get-User has a cloud-only -IsVIP switch. Changed: (1) unlicensed tenants should get not_met with a licence note, matching how this codebase already treats plan-gated questions, rather than no check; (2) the exo() helper in readM365Checks does not pass cmdParams today, so that is part of the work; (3) the alert-policy part is reachable through CIPP's nightly ExoProtectionAlert cache as well as the Compliance flag, but still not judged automatically; (4) availability of Get-EmailTenantSettings through CIPP is marked UNCONFIRMED (ListExoRequest's AvailableCmdlets option can show it with one read-only call).

### Microsoft 365: Microsoft Entra admin center

**M06. Ensure access to the Entra admin center is restricted**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 8; medium; confidence low).
- Source: CIPP (Microsoft Graph beta passthrough ListGraphRequest), access UNCONFIRMED
- How: New key entra_admin_center_restricted (not in either repo today). The fact is at Graph beta GET /admin/entra/uxSetting, field restrictNonAdminAccess; through CORE that would be one read with the existing graph() helper, Endpoint=admin/entra/uxSetting. Whether CIPP's token is allowed to read it is UNCONFIRMED and the public evidence leans against: Microsoft's page lists no supported permission for either delegated or application access and says the signed-in user must be a Global Administrator; CIPP's DisableEntraPortal standard that reads this URI carries the comment that it is still unlisted because Microsoft is fixing permissions; CIPP's CIS test CIS_5_1_2_4 is hard-coded manual. Do not send AsApp=true (application access is documented as not supported).
- Rule: UNCONFIRMED until probed. Step 1: one read-only probe on one client tenant with the existing helper. If it answers: restrictNonAdminAccess true (Microsoft's sample returns the string "false", so accept boolean or string) -> met; false -> not_met; anything else or a failed read -> send nothing. No partly state. Then A.C.O.R.N side as M01 (CHECK_QUESTIONS, CORE_CHECK_KEYS + count, docs/M365_CHECKS.md, cap raised first). If the probe is refused (403): do not build the check; the question stays a typed answer (Entra admin center > Users > User settings > 'Restrict access to Microsoft Entra admin center' = Yes), re-confirmed every 12 months from the typed answer's updated_at.
- Needs: Approve one read-only probe of admin/entra/uxSetting through CIPP on one client tenant before any build; the result decides whether this is a check or stays typed.
- Reviewer (corrected): The endpoint and field are real and read-only, but the proposal's claim that CIPP's standard 'shows the CIPP app can read it' does not stand: that standard's own comment says it is unlisted while Microsoft fixes permissions, Microsoft's Graph page shows 'Not supported' for every permission type and requires a Global Administrator sign-in, and CIPP left its CIS test manual. Confidence lowered from high to low, an owner-approved probe added as a precondition, and a typed-answer fallback written in. Category kept only because the API holds the fact; treat it as person-typed until the probe succeeds.

### Microsoft 365: SharePoint admin center

**M07. Ensure SharePoint and OneDrive integration with Azure AD B2B is enabled**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 10; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP reporting cache (SharePoint tenant settings); a CIPP function CORE does not call today
- How: New key sharepoint_b2b_integration (not in either repo today). Not in what CORE reads now: ListSharepointSettings returns only Graph admin/sharepoint/settings, which has no B2B property, and a code search of CIPP-API finds no live HTTP function that returns the SharePoint tenant object. It is in the object CIPP caches nightly (Set-CIPPDBCacheSPOTenant, all properties of the SharePoint admin tenant object): GET /api/ListDBCache?tenantFilter=<tenant>&type=SPOTenant -> EnableAzureADB2BIntegration, plus GET /api/ListDBCache?tenantFilter=<tenant>&type=SPOTenant&countsOnly=true -> LastRefresh for age. Both fit the existing read() helper (unwrap accepts { Results }). ListDBCache needs the CIPP.Core.Read role, the same role as ListGraphRequest and ListExoRequest that CORE already calls. One read serves M07 to M11.
- Rule: EnableAzureADB2BIntegration=true -> met. false -> not_met. Row missing or field absent -> send nothing (needs_person). No partly state. The age check is required, not optional: when a tenant has no SharePoint consent CIPP logs a warning and leaves the old row and its old timestamp in place, so without it a stale answer would keep being reported. If LastRefresh is older than about 7 days, send nothing. A.C.O.R.N side as M01, cap raised first. NOT VERIFIED: that the owner's CIPP instance has ListDBCache and the SPOTenant collection (the cache and CIS files first appear in CIPP-API on 2026-05-04), that ListDBCache resolves the tenant id form CORE passes, and that SharePoint consent for CIPP's certificate sign-in exists in each client tenant.
- Needs: Confirm (one read-only call on one tenant: ListDBCache with type=_availableTypes) that the CIPP instance has ListDBCache with an SPOTenant collection, and that SharePoint access has been consented for CIPP in client tenants.
- Reviewer (corrected): Mechanism confirmed in CIPP's source (ListDBCache exists and is read-only, SPOTenant is written nightly, the field is in CIPP's field list and its CIS test, no live function exposes it). One change: the cache-age guard is made mandatory, because CIPP's code keeps the old row when a tenant has no SharePoint consent, so 'answer can be a day old' understates the risk. Also noted that the countsOnly call gives the age and that the tenant id form is unverified.

**M12. Ensure anonymous users and dial-in callers can`t start a meeting**

CIPP caches the Global meeting policy nightly (Set-CIPPDBCacheCsTeamsMeetingPolicy: Get-CsTeamsMeetingPolicy, Identity Global): GET /api/ListDBCache?tenantFilter=<tenant>&type=CsTeamsMeetingPolicy -> AllowAnonymousUsersToStartMeeting, plus the countsOnly=true call for LastRefresh. The same row carries the fields CIPP lists for the other Teams meeting questions (AllowAnonymousUsersToJoinMeeting, AutoAdmittedUsers, AllowPSTNUsersToBypassLobby, MeetingChatEnabledType, DesignatedPresenterRoleMode, AllowExternalParticipantGiveRequestControl, AllowExternalNonTrustedMeetingChat, AllowCloudRecording). - Rule: Polarity: the setting is 'can start a meeting', so false is the good answer. AllowAnonymousUsersToStartMeeting=false -> met. true -> not_met. Row or field absent -> send nothing (needs_person). No partly state. The one Teams setting covers anonymous users and dial-in callers, so the question as worded is answered. Say in the evidence that only the Global (org-wide default) policy is read; a custom meeting policy assigned to some users is not seen. Cache-age guard required: CIPP's cache step swallows a failed Teams read and leaves the old row, so send nothing when LastRefresh is older than about 7 days. NOT VERIFIED: that the owner's CIPP instance has ListDBCache with this collection and that Teams access works for CIPP in each client tenant.
- Needs: Confirm the CIPP instance has ListDBCache with a CsTeamsMeetingPolicy collection for client tenants (the same one read-only call as M07).
- Reviewer (corrected): Mechanism, field, polarity and the Global-only scope all confirmed in CIPP's source. One change: a cache-age guard is added as a requirement, because the Teams cache step catches its own errors and keeps the previous row, so a tenant whose Teams read has been failing would otherwise keep reporting an old answer.
