**M21. Ensure app permission policies are configured**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 10; medium; confidence low).
- Source: CIPP (Teams app permission policies from its nightly reporting cache)
- How: GET /api/ListDBCache?type=CsTeamsAppPermissionPolicy&tenantFilter=<tenant> (CIPP stores every policy, ListAll); take the row whose Identity is 'Global'; read GlobalCatalogAppsType with GlobalCatalogApps (third-party apps) and PrivateCatalogAppsType with PrivateCatalogApps (custom apps). Proposed NEW key teams_app_permissions (no such key today). ListTests row CIS_8_4_1 is weaker and should not be used.
- Rule: Per class (third-party, custom): type AllowedAppList = restricted (empty list means block all, a filled list means allow only those); type BlockedAppList = open (empty list means allow all, a filled list means block only those). Both classes restricted -> met. Exactly one class restricted, or a class open but with a filled block list -> partly, with both modes named in the evidence (Maester's test for this control would fail these cases; partly is the softer reading, flagged so a reviewer can disagree). Both classes BlockedAppList with empty lists -> not_met. No Global row or the type fields missing -> not sent. Microsoft apps are not judged. UNCONFIRMED: that the cached row carries PrivateCatalogAppsType and the two list fields under these names (CIPP's manifest lists only Identity, GlobalCatalogAppsType, DefaultCatalogAppsType; the other names come from Maester's use of the cmdlet output). Larger limit: for a tenant moved to app centric management these permission policies are no longer the control in force, nothing in the cache says whether a tenant has moved, and I found no CIPP read for the org-wide app settings, so a met here can describe a dead policy. Evidence must say 'according to the org-wide app permission policy; confirm under Manage apps if the tenant uses app centric management'.
- Needs: One look in a typical client tenant's Teams admin center: are clients on app centric management (Teams apps > Manage apps > Org-wide app settings in charge, Permission policies page gone)? If most are, this check should not be built and the question stays typed.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category kept but only just. Confirmed: the cache holds all app permission policies, and CIPP's test passes on BlockedAppList whatever the list holds, so it is too loose to use. Corrections: (1) the list field names the proposer could not verify are GlobalCatalogApps and PrivateCatalogApps in Maester's test of the same cmdlet output; still unconfirmed for CIPP's cached row. (2) Maester's test (CIS v7.0.0 8.4.1) passes only when third-party AND custom apps are each block-all or allow-list; I kept the proposer's 'partly' for the in-between cases but say plainly that the benchmark reading fails them. (3) The app-centric-management limit is the real risk: the check cannot tell whether the policy it reads is still in force. I did not verify Microsoft's migration rules this session. If the owner's look shows clients are on app centric management, treat this as person_only (typed answer, re-confirmed yearly).

**M22. Ensure only people in my org can bypass the lobby**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 10; small; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP (Teams Global meeting policy from its nightly reporting cache)
- How: Same row as M13: GET /api/ListDBCache?type=CsTeamsMeetingPolicy&tenantFilter=<tenant>, field AutoAdmittedUsers. Proposed NEW key teams_lobby_bypass (no such key today).
- Rule: AutoAdmittedUsers equal to 'EveryoneInCompanyExcludingGuests', 'OrganizerOnly' or 'InvitedUsers' -> met; any other non-empty value -> not_met with the value named; missing or empty -> not sent. No partly. The same three values pass in both CIPP's CIS_8_5_3 and Maester's CIS 8.5.3 test. Wording caution to keep in the evidence: 'InvitedUsers' lets invited outsiders skip the lobby, so name the value. Global (org-wide default) policy only.
- Reviewer (corrected): Field and the three passing values confirmed in CIPP's test and independently in Maester's test. Rule reworded to 'any other value fails' so it does not rest on an enumerated list I did not re-read. Effort S on top of M13; nothing separate from the owner. Not verified live.

**M23. Ensure users can report security concerns in Teams**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 10; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP (Teams Global messaging policy from its nightly cache; Defender report submission policy through Exchange Online)
- How: Two reads: (1) GET /api/ListDBCache?type=CsTeamsMessagingPolicy&tenantFilter=<tenant>, field AllowSecurityEndUserReporting (Global policy). (2) The existing cippExoRead('Get-ReportSubmissionPolicy', [...]) through POST /api/ListExoRequest (its Get- only guard accepts this cmdlet; CIPP's endpoint allows Get and Search verbs and CIPP itself runs this cmdlet for its own cache), fields ReportChatMessageEnabled, ReportChatMessageToCustomizedAddressEnabled, ReportJunkToCustomizedAddress, ReportPhishToCustomizedAddress; or the cached copy, ListDBCache type=ReportSubmissionPolicy. Proposed NEW key teams_security_reporting (no such key today). ListTests row CIS_8_6_1 should not be used alone.
- Rule: Here true is the good answer for the Teams switch. AllowSecurityEndUserReporting === false -> not_met. AllowSecurityEndUserReporting === true AND ReportChatMessageToCustomizedAddressEnabled === true (reports go to the organisation's own reporting mailbox) -> met, with the junk/phish custom-address settings named in the evidence. Teams switch true but chat reports go to Microsoft only (ReportChatMessageEnabled true, custom false), or both Defender chat flags false, or the Defender policy unreadable -> partly, saying which. Teams policy unreadable -> not sent. Person part no tool holds: whether anyone watches the reporting mailbox; that stays a typed note. Global messaging policy only. Add a LICENSE_NOTES line in m365_review.py: Maester labels this control 'E5 L1', so the Defender half may not exist on lower plans (not verified which plan exactly). NOT VERIFIED live that Get-ReportSubmissionPolicy returns through this CIPP instance.
- Reviewer (corrected): Both reads confirmed in source: the messaging-policy cache (Global) with AllowSecurityEndUserReporting, and Get-ReportSubmissionPolicy via CIPP's read-only Exchange route (cipp.ts guard and CIPP's verb allow-list both pass it). Rule tightened: the proposer gave met when chat reports go to Microsoft only. Maester's write-up of this control says the point is that reports stay inside the organisation (ReportChatMessageEnabled false, ReportChatMessageToCustomizedAddressEnabled true, custom addresses set), and CIPP's test also requires a custom address. So met now needs the custom-mailbox routing and Microsoft-only routing is partly. I did not go as far as Maester (which also fails on missing junk/not-junk/phish addresses) because those are e-mail settings beyond the question's wording; they go in the evidence. Added the licence note. needs_from_hal emptied (nothing separate from M13); the mailbox-is-watched half remains a typed answer.

### Technology stack: Technology stack (always unknown today)

**T-backup_isp. Does each location have a secondary/backup ISP connection**

- Route: Map a check we already get (build item 1; small; confidence high).
- Source: Cisco Meraki (dashboard API, already swept)
- How: Existing check key meraki_dual_wan (source "meraki"), built by dualWanCheck in hud/src/aisoc-meraki-checks.ts from GET /organizations/{id}/appliance/uplink/statuses and sent by MERAKI_SWEEP.checksFor. A.C.O.R.N side: tech_stack() has no mapping table and is not given tool_checks; the endpoint already builds tool_checks (meraki is in TOOL_CHECK_SOURCES), so pass it in and replace put("backup_isp", "unknown", ...) with a short rule.
- Rule: Yes is good. meraki_dual_wan met -> seen, suggested "Yes" ONLY when every firewall in the device inventory is a Meraki one; if network_answer/inventory also lists a firewall of another make (UniFi, Auvik-seen), say seen "Yes at the Meraki sites" and name the uncovered firewalls, because the check cannot speak for them. partly -> not_seen, suggested "Not at every location" (evidence already names up to 3 serials). not_met -> not_seen, "None seen". Key absent -> unknown (it is absent when the Meraki licence has expired, the read failed, or the client has no Meraki firewall). Say in the evidence: it counts firewalls, not locations (a teleworker gateway or a spare unit counts as a firewall); it counts uplinks that are active or ready today, so a second line that is down reads as missing; two lines from one carrier, or a cellular uplink, read as a backup. Non-Meraki clients stay unknown and need a person.
- Reviewer (corrected): Key and its meaning confirmed by reading the code (met = every firewall has two uplinks active/ready; partly = some single; not_met = all single). Added one guard the proposal missed: a client with a Meraki firewall at one site and another make elsewhere would have read as a plain "Yes" for every location. Also noted the check is absent on an expired licence, and that spare units and teleworker gateways are counted as firewalls. Strictly this is more than "a mapping line" (the tech list has no table, so it is a few lines of code plus a new argument), but the check itself exists, so the category stands.

**T-encryption. Are you currently utilizing encryption for laptops?**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 2; small; confidence high).
- Source: Device inventory A.C.O.R.N already holds (NinjaOne and Liongard BitLocker readings; Addigy FileVault fact)
- How: infra_review.encryption_readiness(devices), which reads DeviceSource.details.bitlocker_encrypted (written by CORE aisoc-devices.ts for NinjaOne Windows workstations and aisoc-liongard.ts for Liongard workstations). tech_stack() already receives the same device list, so no endpoint change. Mac half: DeviceSource.details.facts.filevault_enabled from Addigy (in ADDIGY_FACTS, stored by addigyFacts(); read by nothing in A.C.O.R.N today).
- Rule: Yes is good. encryption_readiness met or partly -> seen, suggested "BitLocker", with its own sentence (N of M workstations with a reading are encrypted; keep the "have no reading yet" tail). not_met with at least one encrypted machine -> seen "BitLocker (some machines)"; not_met with none encrypted -> not_seen "None seen". Result "unknown" (no reading yet) or None (no Windows workstation) -> unknown, unless Macs report FileVault on, then seen "FileVault". The FileVault value may arrive as a boolean or as the text "true" (addigyFacts keeps whichever Addigy sends), so accept both. Limit: the inventory has no chassis field, so Windows laptops cannot be told from desktops and the answer is about workstations; say so. For Macs a model name containing "MacBook" would mark a laptop (NOT checked against a live Addigy row).
- Reviewer (corrected): The route holds: the function exists, both tools populate the field, and tech_stack already has the devices. Two details added: the FileVault fact can be a boolean or a string depending on what Addigy returns, so the rule must accept both; and a grep of A.C.O.R.N confirms there is no chassis or laptop field anywhere, so the "workstations, not laptops" wording is required, with the Mac model name as a possible (unverified) laptop marker.

**T-spam_filter. Do you have a spam filter in place beyond the stock MS/Google provided. (We prefer Inky)**

- Route: New check in CORE (build item 21; medium; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP / Exchange Online (connectors, mail flow rules and the mail route of the client's tenant)
- How: New check (proposed key cipp_mail_filter; no such key exists today). Three reads, two of them already made by the baseline sweep in aisoc-m365-checks.ts: (1) ListDomainAnalyser rows, field MailProvider (CORE already reads it and only keeps "Microsoft 365" rows): a gateway in front of the MX (CIPP's list: Proofpoint, Mimecast, Barracuda ESS, Sophos, SpamTitan, HornetSecurity, AppRiver, Intermedia, Reflexion, Symantec Cloud) is named here with no new call; (2) ListTransportRules rows (already read); (3) NEW: GET /api/ListExchangeConnectors?tenantFilter=... (confirmed in CIPP's public source: role Exchange.Connector.Read, runs Get-OutboundConnector and Get-InboundConnector, returns every property plus cippconnectortype; read-only; answers HTTP 500 with a message string on failure). Cheapest placement is the baseline sweep (source "cipp", one extra call, 73 of 80 keys) since tech_stack() has to be given that row for T-anti_phishing and T-external_email_banner anyway; under "cipp-health" it needs the reads repeated. Vendor name in items[0] or in the evidence (a check has no value field; no review function reads items today).
- Rule: Yes is good, and the stock filter does not count: Microsoft's own protection alone is NOT a yes. Third-party product found by name in MailProvider, in enabled connector names / smart hosts / TLS certificate names, or in enabled mail flow rule names -> check met; A.C.O.R.N shows seen, suggested = product. None found -> check not_met; A.C.O.R.N shows not_seen "None seen", evidence "no third-party filter in the mail route, connectors or mail flow rules". UNCONFIRMED: the exact connector and rule names Inky creates (a Kaseya help article, seen as a search summary only, says an Inky deployment has required connectors and transport rules that must be present and enabled); test the pattern list on one live Inky tenant before trusting a "None seen". Products that work only through a consented application leave no connector and would read as none: ListOAuthApps does NOT cover them (CIPP's source shows it lists delegated grants only), so a service-principal name read would be needed (Graph servicePrincipals through the existing passthrough; path from my own knowledge, UNCONFIRMED). Interim, held data only: a HaloPSA recurring-invoice line naming the product (client_license_counts, source halopsa) -> seen; yes-side only. Google Workspace mail: no data, stays unknown.
- Needs: One client known to run Inky, to confirm the connector and rule names to match; and whether Inky appears as its own line on HaloPSA recurring invoices (for the interim rule).
- Reviewer (corrected): Category holds. Fixes: (1) ListExchangeConnectors is now confirmed from CIPP's own source, not a third-party module (path, role, cmdlets, read-only, 500-with-string on error). (2) The proposal's fallback for application-based filters was wrong: ListOAuthApps lists delegated consent grants only, so it would miss a product that uses application permissions. (3) Added a signal that costs nothing: the MailProvider field of ListDomainAnalyser rows CORE already fetches names a gateway that sits in the MX. (4) Placement: the baseline sweep already holds two of the three reads, so the claim that it must go under cipp-health is not required; either works. The Inky name patterns remain unconfirmed. The HaloPSA interim is more plausible than stated (the code comment shows bundled products are itemised on recurring invoices) but still not verified for Inky.

**T-anti_phishing. Do you have an Anti-Phishing Filter in place. (We prefer Inky)**

- Route: Map a check we already get (build item 1; small; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP / Microsoft 365 baseline (anti-phishing policies of Exchange Online)
- How: Existing check key anti_phishing (source "cipp"; checkAntiPhishing in aisoc-m365-checks.ts counts rows of CIPP ListAntiPhishingFilters with Enabled true; CIPP's source shows that endpoint returns every Get-AntiPhishPolicy row, the default policy included). A.C.O.R.N side: the endpoint already loads the "cipp" row (m365_row) but gives it only to m365_answers; pass its checks to tech_stack() and replace the hard-coded unknown.
- Rule: Yes is good, and this line has no "beyond the stock" wording, so Microsoft's own filter counts as worded. anti_phishing met AND the client's mail is on Microsoft 365 (the same row holds an spf, dkim or dmarc key, which CORE only sends when a mail domain's provider is Microsoft 365) -> seen, suggested "Microsoft 365 built-in anti-phishing". Do not call it "Defender": the base policy exists without a Defender for Office 365 licence. Be plain that this answer will be the same for practically every Microsoft client: Microsoft documents that a default anti-phishing policy applies to all recipients in every organization, so the check's not_met branch is close to unreachable (that the default row carries Enabled true is from my own knowledge, not read on a live tenant). If the proposed mail-filter check (T-spam_filter) names a third-party product, that name replaces the suggestion. anti_phishing present but no spf/dkim/dmarc key (mail routed elsewhere) -> unknown. Key absent or Google mail -> unknown.
- Needs: Decision: does Microsoft's built-in policy count as a yes for this template line, or only a third-party product such as Inky? If only third-party, this item has no existing check and becomes the same new check as T-spam_filter.
- Reviewer (corrected): The key exists and literally answers the line, so the category stands, but the limit the proposer "could not check" is now resolved against them: Microsoft's documentation says a default anti-phishing policy applies to every recipient in every organization, so this mapping returns the same yes for nearly every Microsoft client and tells the reviewer nothing about a product. Changed the suggested label (not "Microsoft Defender"), and added a guard for tenants whose mail does not flow through Microsoft 365 (an M365 tenant with mail on Google or behind another route would otherwise read as filtered). The owner decision is the real gate on whether this is worth wiring.

**T-password_manager. Are you Utilizing a password manager( we prefer lastpass)**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 14; small; confidence low).
- Source: HaloPSA recurring-invoice lines (client_license_counts) and IT Glue password entries (client_documents), both already stored
- How: client_license_counts rows with source "halopsa": item = the invoice line's item_shortdescription (at most 80 characters), count = quantity, sent by CORE's HaloPSA sweep (billedLines). Second held signal: client_documents rows vendor itglue, kind "password" (title = entry name; body has "URL: ..."), where an entry for a password-manager console exists. The client-review endpoint reads neither table today; it would load both for the client and pass the names to tech_stack().
- Rule: Yes is good. A halopsa item name containing a password-manager product (lastpass, keeper, 1password, bitwarden, dashlane, nordpass, passportal...) -> seen, suggested = product, evidence "HaloPSA bills N x '<line>' each month". Failing that, an IT Glue password entry whose URL or name is a password-manager admin console -> seen, suggested = product, evidence names the entry (weaker: it may be one person's login). Neither -> stay unknown, NEVER not_seen: the client may have bought one itself or a bundle may not itemise it. This shows the product is sold to or documented for the client, not that staff use it; that part still needs a person. NOT VERIFIED that a password manager appears as its own invoice line or IT Glue entry for any client (no live data read).
- Needs: Confirm the item name LastPass is billed under on HaloPSA recurring invoices, or agree to bill it as its own line; otherwise this stays a typed answer (which today has no expiry; a 12-month expiry would be a small addition).
- Reviewer (corrected): Route and its honesty limits hold (yes-side only, billing is not use, low confidence). Added a second held signal the proposer did not use (IT Glue password entries, the same query T-abm needs). Corrected one statement: CORE does already read NinjaOne's full software list every sweep (aisoc-remote-tools.ts), so a software-based check is a catalogue change in CORE rather than a new read; it would still miss browser-extension-only use, so billing stays first. Typed answers do not expire today (review_overrides.py has no expiry), so the stated fallback needs that small feature.

**T-external_email_banner. Do currently have notifications turned on for external emails? (We prefer Inky)**

- Route: Map a check we already get (build item 1; small; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP / Microsoft 365 baseline (Exchange Online external sender tag)
- How: Existing check key external_sender_tag (source "cipp"; checkExternalSenderTag in aisoc-m365-checks.ts reads Get-ExternalInOutlook, field Enabled). A.C.O.R.N side: pass the "cipp" row's checks into tech_stack() and replace the hard-coded unknown.
- Rule: Yes is good. external_sender_tag met AND mail is on Microsoft 365 (an spf, dkim or dmarc key is in the same row) -> seen, suggested "Outlook external sender tag". not_met -> unknown, NOT not_seen, with the evidence "Outlook's own external tag is off; a banner added by a mail flow rule or another product is not checked": the usual alternative is a mail flow rule that prepends "[EXTERNAL]" or a warning to mail from outside, and Inky adds its own banner, and this check reads neither. It can become not_seen only once a check covers both: the proposed mail-filter check (T-spam_filter) for Inky, plus a rule test on the ListTransportRules rows CORE already fetches in the same sweep (enabled rule, sender outside the organization, prepends subject text or a disclaimer; those field names are from my own knowledge of Get-TransportRule, UNCONFIRMED). If the mail-filter check names Inky -> seen "Inky banner". Key absent, mail elsewhere, or Google Workspace -> unknown.
- Reviewer (corrected): Key and field confirmed; the yes side is a true mapping. Tightened the no side: the proposal let not_met become "None seen" when the mail-filter check found no third-party product, but that still ignores a plain mail flow rule that stamps external mail, which is a common way to do this and is not covered by either check. So a no needs the rule test as well (the rows are already fetched, so it is cheap to add to the same new check). Added the same mail-not-on-Microsoft-365 guard as T-anti_phishing.

**T-cloud_servers. What service provider is the client leveraging for their cloud servers (Azure is our standard)**

- Route: A person answers; ask again when it ages (build item 3; small; confidence medium).
- Source: none
- How: Typed answer on the Client review page (client_review_answers, section "tech", key cloud_servers, which already accepts an answer string). A.C.O.R.N can show hints beside the box from data it holds, without choosing the answer: Device.manufacturer / Device.model of machines whose os contains "server" (the fields infra_review.hypervisor_of reads), and client_license_counts source "halopsa" item names containing "azure".
- Rule: A "which" question; the person's typed provider is the answer and should be asked again after 12 months (typed answers have no expiry today; review_overrides.py keeps updated_at, so the rule is small to add). Hints only: "N servers are Microsoft virtual machines: Azure or on-site Hyper-V, cannot be told apart"; "HaloPSA bills '<line naming Azure>'"; a server whose maker reads Amazon EC2 or Google could be shown as a suggestion (seen "AWS" / "Google Cloud"), but those maker strings are from memory and UNCONFIRMED against any stored row. No server listed -> unknown, never "none": a cloud server without an agent is invisible.
- Needs: Whether Azure hosting is billed as its own line on HaloPSA recurring invoices (and under what name); only that would let A.C.O.R.N suggest "Azure" on its own.
- Reviewer (downgraded): The derive route does not hold for the cases that matter. The team's standard is Azure, and the code's own comment says an Azure machine and an on-site Hyper-V machine report the same make and model, so the most common real answer cannot be derived; "no cloud servers" cannot be asserted either. What is left is AWS/Google by maker strings nobody has checked, and an Azure invoice line nobody has confirmed exists. No connected tool lists Azure subscriptions. Honest category is a typed answer with hints; if the owner confirms an Azure invoice line, that one hint can be promoted to a suggestion.

**T-abm. Is the client already using ABM or at least have an ABM account.**

- Route: New rule over data we already hold (build item 14; small; confidence low).
- Source: IT Glue password ENTRIES already stored as client documents (name, user name, URL, category; never the secret); Addigy enrolment fact as a second signal
- How: client_documents rows vendor itglue, kind "password": title = entry name, body holds "URL: ..." and "Category: ..." (CORE passwordRecord). The client-review endpoint does not read client_documents today; it would query this client's password entries (the table is under per-client row-level security, so the read must run in the client's scope). Second signal needs a small CORE change, so it is new collection rather than held data: add the Addigy device fact enrolled_via_dep to ADDIGY_FACTS; addigyFacts() loops that list, so it arrives in details.facts with no other change, and no whitelist on A.C.O.R.N's device ingest was found that would drop it.
- Rule: Yes is good. A password entry whose URL contains business.apple.com (or school.apple.com), or whose name matches "Apple Business Manager" or "ABM" as a whole word -> seen, suggested "ABM account documented", evidence names the entry and links it (the "at least have an account" half). Any Mac with enrolled_via_dep true -> seen "In use (N Macs enrolled through Automated Device Enrollment)", since that enrolment needs Apple Business (or School) Manager. Neither -> unknown, never not_seen: an account nobody documented is invisible. Clients with no Macs: unknown. Confirmed: Addigy's own blog lists a device fact "Enrolled via DEP (Automated Device Enrollment)" for iOS, iPadOS and macOS with true/false values. UNCONFIRMED: the identifier spelling enrolled_via_dep (search summary of Addigy's support article only), so check it against the live fact list; and CORE's IT Glue documents sweep still says its record shapes were not verified against a live account.
- Needs: Convention: every client's ABM login is saved in IT Glue Passwords with the URL business.apple.com (or a name containing "Apple Business Manager"). Without it the IT Glue half only fires where someone happened to file the entry that way.
- Reviewer (corrected): Kept the category because the IT Glue entries are held and a positive hit needs no convention, but lowered confidence from medium to low: it rests on a sweep whose shapes are still marked unverified, on entries nobody has confirmed exist, and the answer is yes-only. Made explicit that the Addigy half is a CORE change (new collection), not held data. Upgraded one piece of evidence: the Addigy fact's existence is now read on Addigy's own page (display name and platforms); only the identifier spelling remains unconfirmed.

### Insurance readiness: Cyber-insurance readiness (always unknown today)

**R-email_filtering. Is e-mail filtered for phishing and malware?**

- Route: Map a check we already get (build item 1; small; confidence medium).
- Source: CIPP / Microsoft 365 baseline (Exchange Online protection policies)
- How: Existing keys under source "cipp": spf / dkim / dmarc (present only when a mail domain's provider is Microsoft 365, so they show that mail flows through Exchange Online), anti_phishing, and safe_attachments, safe_links, common_attachments as the strength detail. A.C.O.R.N side: readiness() receives tool_checks only; the endpoint already loads the "cipp" row, so pass its checks in and replace the hard-coded unknown in services/client_review.py with a small rule. The wording is A.C.O.R.N's own, so no template text constrains it.
- Rule: Yes is good. Mail on Exchange Online (an spf, dkim or dmarc key is in the cipp row) -> met: Microsoft documents that anti-malware scanning is on for every cloud mailbox and cannot be turned off, and that a default anti-phishing policy applies to all recipients, so the literal answer is yes. Evidence then lists what is added on top, quoting the checks' own sentences: Safe Attachments, Safe Links, the common attachment types filter, and a third-party product if the proposed mail-filter check (T-spam_filter) names one. Do NOT grade the malware half on common_attachments or safe_attachments as proposed: a tenant with both off is still scanned for malware, so that rule would show partly or not_met for mail that is filtered. cipp row present but no spf/dkim/dmarc key (mail routed through another provider or gateway) -> unknown, unless the mail-filter check names the gateway, then met. No cipp row, or Google Workspace mail -> unknown. Limits: says filtering exists, not how well it is tuned or that every mailbox is covered by the stronger policies.
- Needs: Decision: should Microsoft's always-on filtering alone show as Met (literal reading; every Microsoft client then reads the same), or as Partly unless Safe Attachments / Safe Links or a third-party filter is also in place?
- Reviewer (corrected): The keys exist, but the proposed rule read them as if they measured whether mail is filtered. They do not: common_attachments is an extra file-type block and safe_attachments is a licensed add-on, while base malware scanning and the default anti-phishing policy are always on for Exchange Online. The proposed rule would therefore have marked filtered mail as partly or not met. Rewrote the rule around what is actually true (mail on Exchange Online is filtered; the keys describe the extras), added a guard for tenants whose mail is not on Microsoft 365, and raised the owner decision on whether stock-only should count as fully met, since otherwise the item is the same for every Microsoft client.

**R-ir_plan. Is there a written incident response plan?**

- Route: Agree where it is written down (IT Glue), then read it (build item 14; small; confidence medium).
- Source: IT Glue documents already synced into A.C.O.R.N (client_documents)
- How: client_documents rows vendor itglue, kind "document" (title, folder, body text, url, vendor_updated_at from IT Glue's updated-at), sent by CORE's IT Glue documents sweep (registered in direct-sweeps.ts as itglue-docs; full read of a client every 7 days, changed documents on other days). Convention: each client's plan is an IT Glue document whose title contains "Incident Response Plan". The client-review endpoint does not read client_documents today; it would look up the client's matching document (in the client's row-level-security scope) and pass it to readiness().
- Rule: Yes is good. A document titled "...Incident Response Plan..." with vendor_updated_at within the last 12 months -> met; evidence gives the title, last-updated date and link. Same document not updated for over 12 months -> partly ("not reviewed since <date>"). A matching title with almost no text -> partly ("filed, but the text could not be read") rather than missing, because a plan kept as an uploaded file has no readable sections (whether IT Glue's document list returns uploaded files at all is UNCONFIRMED). No such document -> unknown until the convention is agreed, then not_met ("no plan filed in IT Glue"), but ONLY when the client has other fresh IT Glue document rows; if the client has none, show unknown, because A.C.O.R.N drops a record not refreshed for 14 days and two missed weekly reads would otherwise turn a filed plan into "missing". What no tool sees: whether the plan is any good, was tested, or the client knows it exists; that stays a person's note. Also UNCONFIRMED: the sweep's header still says its record shapes were not verified against a live account.
- Needs: Agree the convention (document title, and whether a missing document counts as Not met), and say whether incident response plans are kept as IT Glue documents or as attached files, whose text CORE does not read.
- Reviewer (corrected): Category and data path confirmed by reading the model, the ingest and the sweep. Two rule fixes: the proposed "body over a few hundred characters" test would have marked a plan filed as an uploaded file as missing, so an empty match is partly, not absent; and the not_met answer is guarded against the 14-day drop (a failed sweep must read as unknown, not as "no plan"), which the proposer named as a risk but did not put in the rule.
